## Project Overview

**JARVIS AI-OS** - An AI-controlled operating system using seL4 microkernel with autonomous decision-making. AI runs at Ring 3 (50-500ms decisions), microkernel runs at Ring 0 (<1ms interrupts). 36-month timeline.

| Phase | Status | Period | Summary |

V6: **16.1 min uptime with `err=0` and `restart_count=0` across all 13 samples**, q_total 138,500. V7: the one-shot consumed, box back on Ubuntu, `BootOrder 0001,0000`. **(P-JCON) THE OWED PROOF IS DELIVERED — the JCON slot re-provisioned 2026-09-03 to 192.168.68.63 IS parsed by seL4 and its reply reaches the console.** `reply_log.jsonl` carries `{"event": "reply_accepted", "seq": 53135, "verdict": 0, "tlen": 457, "src": "192.168.100.143:51000"}` against its own `sent` `seq16=53135`, round trip 31 s. `reply_accepted` is emitted ONLY after both crc_ok and hmac_ok, so it is an AUTHENTICATED reply rather than merely an arriving datagram — and it is discriminating in the way `TLM_F_CONTROL_IN` is not, since any checksum-valid slot sets that flag and the boot line prints mac:port only, never the parsed IP. **(P-PROV) THE WIDENED PROVENANCE WROTE NON-ZERO `n` AND `emit` ON REAL RECALLING TURNS, read back off the store after the power-cycle.** Store total **141 → 144** = T0 + the 3 turns, checksum OK. Pre-boot the store held **0** records with `n!=0` or `emit!=0` (no widened build had ever run on the box), so every non-zero below can only have come from this code. `[24:00141]` `"what is a page fault?"` → `kind=1 exact src=102 n=1 emit=1` — src 102 is a PRIOR-BOOT record, i.e. cross-session exact recall on hardware. `[24:00142]` same query → `kind=1 exact src=141 n=1 emit=1`, newest-wins picking up the record turn 141 had just written. **`[24:00143]` `"why do page faults happen?"` → `kind=2 SEMANTIC src=142 cos=804 n=2 src2=141 cos2=804 emit=3`** — the exact key missed, the semantic lane selected TWO facts at cosine 0.804 (clear of the 0.55 floor), named both, and `emit=3` proves BOTH reached the preamble. **That is precisely the `[23:00110]` shape whose old-format provenance would have named only the first fact: the case that motivated the widening is now self-describing on the box.** (`cos == cos2 == 804` is internally consistent — both candidates hold the identical stored answer to the identical question.) **DURABLE READ-BACKS:** `Boot ID 55`, `err=0` the ONLY err value across 2709 lines, 0 `[FATAL]`/`[MODEL-BAD]`/`[ANOMALY]`/`[RESTART]`/`UNUSABLE`, `[SNAP]` carrying `model=loaded` and `NN=6` as their only values with `disp=1024x768x32` (the bare-metal framebuffer, versus KVM's `no-fb`); `[CTRL-IN-STATS] acc=3 drop=1 (parse=1 rl=0 auth=0 replay=0) bp=0 down=0 recall=3 idx=131` on the first retained line, rising only in `parse`/`drop` to 7 by the last. JACT: exactly **3** `action=5 NOTIFY/EXECUTED/OK risk=0 trigger="control-in answered"` records for this boot (JACT's own boot counter 41, not 55 — each store carries its own) and no other action, with **all four raw-query probes returning 0**, so no query text ever entered the audit trail. **TWO HONEST DEVIATIONS FROM THE PLAN'S EXPECTED VALUES, both explained by measurement rather than waved through. (1) `control_in_dropped` is NOT 0 — it read 1 rising to 7, and the durable breakdown says `parse` only, with `rl=0 auth=0 replay=0`:** the drops are non-JCTL traffic reaching the NIC and being rejected by the parser — not authentication, not replay, not rate-limiting, and unrelated to the three sends, all of which were accepted (`acc=3`). This is exactly what the per-cause breakdown exists to distinguish, and a bare aggregate counter would have read as a channel fault. **(2) The `idx=` check landed in its INDIRECT form.** The prediction computed off the pre-boot store was **128** (129 tag-3 records minus the 1 with `outcome!=0`; the 12 tag-4 `CTRLIN-LOCAL` records correctly excluded by `g3_candidate_usable`, and the thought-marker filter rightly not applying because it acts at SELECT time, not index time), and every RETAINED `[CTRL-IN-STATS]` line reads `idx=131` = 128 + the 3 recallable turns this boot added. The lines carrying the boot-scan value 128 itself had already WRAPPED OUT, because **the durable ring's retention on this boot was ~7.4 minutes, not the ~11 this file has carried**: the workload settled at ~203 q/s, so ~2 windows/s × 3 durable entries ≈ 6.1 entries/s against 2700 slots. **A12's "~11 min" is a RATE-DEPENDENT figure, not a constant — a future plan that needs a specific early durable line must either power-cycle inside that window or accept the indirect form.** **(P-RIDER) THE WIRED THIRD-HOST CAPTURE:** the Pi's eth0 rider (`promiscuity` measured **0 → 1** at start and **1 → 0** at exit, read via `ip -d link show` because this Pi's kernel 6.18.34 exposes **no** `/sys/class/net/*/promiscuity` file at all — the netlink `IFLA_PROMISCUITY` counter is the same value by a different transport, and the before/after pair is strictly better evidence than a bare after-reading) captured 2135 frames on the box MAC: **`:51000` = 2041** (the broadcast positive control, which is what makes the zero meaningful rather than a dead capture) and **`:51002` = 0**, agreed by two independent counting methods. **BOTH STANDING QUALIFICATIONS APPLY VERBATIM: this is a property of THIS switch, measured in its most-favourable CAM state — it rules out a hub, a mirror port or routine flooding, and says nothing about a CAM-flood or ARP-spoof adversary. SIGNED-NOT-ENCRYPTED is unchanged.** **(P-MESH) FIRST BARE-METAL I211 BOOT ON THE RE-ADDRESSED MESH (192.168.68.0/22):** telemetry reached both the Main PC and the Pi and the control-IN round trip completed in both directions, with `JARVIS_BOX_IP` still the compile-time off-subnet 192.168.100.143 by design — the off-subnet measurements had previously been made from Ubuntu's driver and never from seL4's I211, so this closes that UNVERIFIED note. **PENDING-DEPLOY DELTA: ONE deployed-path commit (2026-09-04, the -Wall cleanup of `main_x86.c` — PA main.c.obj byte-identical on .text (68,624), .data, every .rodata*, nm (545) and .bss; .text.startup 2394 -> 2378, the 16 B fully accounted as the removed xor %eax,%eax (the AL vector-count zeroing SysV requires before an unprototyped call, gone once the prototype is visible) plus alignment nops, with all four argument registers and the int return handling unchanged - the delta refutes a signature mismatch rather than suggesting one; the strategist's pre-registered stop rule had treated any delta as a mismatch and was over-broad), not yet on the box.** No console or telemetry change rode this deploy — the wire stays v14/276 B and no UI surface was touched, so the UI-parity rule has nothing to satisfy here. **SOAK ROW — STARTED 2026-08-13, boot_id=54, UNATTENDED for ~1 month with NO diagnostic support.** OUTCOME: a single boot of 7 d 18 h 18 m ended by a grid power outage ≈ 2026-08-21, NOT by the box — `q_total` 132,731,400, err=0 the ONLY error value in every witness, ZERO restarts/faults/anomalies, the 168 h digest within 608 ms of the mark; 39/39 control-IN turns answered incl. a 12-turn adversarial battery that minted NOTHING; forensics `phase6/docs/SOAK_2026-08_FINAL_REPORT.md`, the `[23:00110]` recall anomaly CLOSED at `37938fe`. **THE EVIDENCE RANKING IS NOT THE OBVIOUS ONE AND THE DURABLE LOG IS THE TRAP:** measured at 4.01 entries/sec, the 2700-entry ring **WRAPS IN 673 s = 11.2 MINUTES**, so a 30-day run retains only its final ~11 minutes — PRIMARY evidence is the JACT audit store and the CUMULATIVE per-boot telemetry counters, which means the headline result survives TOTAL capture failure. **`-i any` is DELIBERATE and must not be narrowed to a named interface:** the Pi is DUAL-HOMED and either link can drop independently, so a named interface would produce a SILENT GAP. **(1) `-i any` yields LINUX_SLL2 (linktype 276, a 20-byte header) while `telemetry_receiver.py` slices a HARDCODED Ethernet `frame[42:]`, so THE REPO'S OWN READER FINDS NOTHING in these captures.** **(2) THE WIRE MAGIC IS LITTLE-ENDIAN:** `JARVIS_TLM_MAGIC` is `0x4A54454C` ("JTEL") stored as a LE u32, so the bytes ON THE WIRE are `4C 45 54 4A` ("LETJ") — the no-gap proof used SYNTHETIC packets carrying literal ASCII `"JTEL"`, so the emitter and the extractor shared the same wrong assumption and validated each other; the defect surfaced only against real telemetry. **The no-gap proof itself STANDS but it did NOT prove the reader worked on real telemetry, and only the live re-verification did.** **The weekly snapshot's BPF filter is NOT optional:** without it `-c 5` grabs the first five packets of ANY kind (almost certainly the operator's own ssh) and captures zero telemetry. **THE SNAPSHOT, NOT THE CONTINUOUS CAPTURE, IS THE BACKUP THAT MATTERS** — the ring restarts at file 0 after any restart or reboot. NOT a 30-day result; timeout/empty/fault/degraded control-IN exits still never fired outside KVM. Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Project Overview — SOAK ROW 2026-08-13".

## Architecture

### Model B: Microkernel with AI Control

```
Hardware Layer
    ↓
Microkernel (Ring 0, cores 0-1)
• Interrupt handling (<1ms)
• Memory management, IPC primitives
• ~12K LOC, seL4 (formally verified*)
    ↓ ← Lock-free IPC →
AI Decision Engine (Ring 3, cores 2-N)
• Specialist agents (4-6) — domain-expert agents (device, network, filesystem, user), NOT model-size tiers
• Decision cache (50ms→<1ms for 85% ops)
• SHIELD safety framework
    ↓
User Space Services (Ring 3)
• Device drivers, File systems, Applications
```

> *\*seL4 "(formally verified)" refers to its verified configurations. JARVIS's x86-64 build runs `KernelFastpath=ON`, which is **outside** the verified X64 config ("C-level functional correctness, **no fast path**") — so JARVIS runs a functional-but-unverified seL4 configuration by design (IPC/AVX perf > holding the proof). See `phase4/docs/PHASE_4_GOAL1_INFERENCE_PERF.md` §3.*

**Why:** AI inference (50-500ms) is 3 orders of magnitude too slow for Ring 0 interrupt handling (<1ms). Microkernel isolates time-critical ops from AI.

### Architecture Enhancements

- **Decision Cache:** Pre-compiled query→bytecode patterns. 135x speedup (50ms→<1ms), 85.7% hit rate.
- **SHIELD Safety:** Staged execution sandbox, continuous risk scoring (0-1.0), shadow execution, deterministic rollback.
- **Shared Context Pool:** Lock-free shared state for agent coordination. 220x faster than serialization.

### Key Constraints

**AI cannot:** handle interrupts (<1ms), generate drivers real-time, run at Ring 0
**AI can:** make high-level decisions (50-500ms), optimize configs, coordinate agents, learn over time

## Coding Conventions & Patterns

### C/Python IPC Struct Alignment

Python ctypes structs MUST exactly match C struct layout:

```python
class RingMessage(ctypes.Structure):
    _pack_ = 1  # REQUIRED: No padding
    _fields_ = [
        ("type", ctypes.c_uint32),
        ("id", ctypes.c_uint32),
        ("payload_size", ctypes.c_uint32),
        ("payload", ctypes.c_char * MAX_MESSAGE_SIZE),
        ("timestamp", ctypes.c_uint64)
    ]
```

### Query Normalization

Both C and Python must normalize identically: lowercase → collapse spaces → trim → FNV-1a 64-bit hash.
- C: `cache_normalize_query()` in `decision_cache.c`
- Python: `QueryProcessor.normalize_query()` in `query_processor.py`

### seL4 Tutorials CMakeLists.txt Pattern

```cmake
include(${SEL4_TUTORIALS_DIR}/settings.cmake)
sel4_tutorials_regenerate_tutorial(${CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR})
include(rootserver)
include(simulation)
add_executable(hello-world src/main.c ...)
target_link_libraries(hello-world sel4 muslc utils sel4muslcsys sel4platsupport sel4utils sel4debug)
DeclareRootserver(hello-world)
```

Note: `DeclareTutorialApp()` does NOT exist. Use `add_executable()` + `DeclareRootserver()`.

### Testing Philosophy

- Every component has standalone tests printing PASS/FAIL
- C tests: compile and run directly (no deps)
- Python tests: `python test_*.py` (mock mode if seL4 unavailable)
- Always aim for 100% pass rate

### Development Documentation Pattern

- **Phase 2** used weekly status files: `phase2/weeks/weekN/WEEK_N_STATUS.md` (weeks 27-49), committed weekly with the week number in the message.
- **Phase 3** was documented via topic/milestone docs in `phase3/docs/` (KICKOFF, HARDWARE_RESEARCH, IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN, the QEMU/native test-results docs, MODEL_BENCH_OFF, the security audits), the ADRs in `docs/decisions/`, and `phase3/docs/PHASE_3_FINAL_REPORT.md` — **not** weekly status files (there is no `phase3/weeks/`).
- **Phase 4** resumes weekly status files at `phase4/weeks/weekN/WEEK_N_STATUS.md` (like Phase 2); Phase 3 used topic docs.
- Implement, test, update the relevant doc; commit with a descriptive subject referencing the milestone/ADR where applicable.

### Bare-Metal Development Rules

- **Always test in QEMU before flashing USB** — run `phase3/scripts/qemu_test.sh` after every build. **⚠ ITS NO-KVM FALLBACK DOES NOT BOOT, and the warning used to say only "very slow" (corrected 2026-08-09).** Measured at A10 on a GitHub runner: the committed fallback line is `-cpu Nehalem,...,-xsave,-xsaveopt,-xsavec` — a pre-AVX CPU with xsave explicitly disabled — while the kernel has been built `CONFIG_XSAVE=1` (feature-set 7) since Phase 4 M0, so it dies at **`XSAVE not supported` -> `seL4 called fail ... boot_sys.c:723` -> `halting...`** having produced 2,521 bytes of serial. It never reaches AVX2; the KERNEL dies before userspace exists. **`-accel tcg -cpu max` DOES boot** (QEMU >= 7.2 implements AVX2 and xsaveopt under TCG; measured: self-test 5/5 PASS) but runs ~2 orders of magnitude slower — 1 `[SNAP]` in 300 s versus 158 `[STATS]` windows in 150 s under KVM. The fallback line itself was deliberately NOT rewritten, only its warning. **Note the stale claim "the committed TCG Nehalem sim cannot run AVX2" lived in `jarvis-strategist/SKILL.md` (its Working Rules bullet + Build config comment) and was CORRECTED in the 2026-09-01 SKILL refresh — it now appears nowhere in the repo**; the committed evidence is `qemu_test.sh`'s own `-cpu` line. If it crashes in QEMU, don't waste time reflashing. **The script IS the gate invocation (fixed 2026-07-25): it derives `-smp` from the BUILT kernel config, attaches the NVMe image, and defaults to `-snapshot`.** Before that fix it passed **no `-smp` and attached no drive at all** (handing the model to seL4 as an initrd module — the ABANDONED path), so it booted non-SMP with no model and a green run proved nothing; `qemu_test_nvme.sh` is now a deprecated shim that forwards here. **Image size is load-bearing:** the raw-LBA stores begin at LBA 21,100,000 (10.06 GiB), so an image smaller than ~10.1 GiB cannot contain them and every store silently fails to init — the script now REFUSES such an image instead. The durable telemetry log at LBA 4,000,794,624 (1907 GiB) is not KVM-reachable at any practical size; read it on the box.
- **Build without embedded model for fast iteration** — use `-DJARVIS_EMBED_MODEL=""` for quick boot tests. Model loading is tested separately via the NVMe FAT32 runtime path (the GRUB-module approach was abandoned — seL4 overwrites module memory during rootserver relocation).
- **Verify GRUB menu entry works** — wrong image names cause silent boot failures (keyboard dies, no output). Check grub.cfg image names match `ls ~/sel4-x86/jbuild/images/`.
- **All on-screen UI MUST mirror to the NVMe log (verify UI from the log, not photos)** — the box is headless-except-monitor and showing on-screen UI to a reviewer is impractical and gets worse as the UI grows. So every render routes through a single log-mirror chokepoint (e.g. `fb_status_line` → `[PANEL] <text>`) — **⚠️ BUT MEASURED 2026-08-04 (boot 50): that chokepoint is a plain `puts_serial`, and the `puts_serial`→NVMe capture lives inside `#if JARVIS_DBG_BOOT_LOG` (`main_x86.c:845`) which is 0 in every deployed/probe image, so `[PANEL]` reaches the SERIAL CONSOLE ONLY and `grep -c PANEL` over a real boot's durable log returns 0. This rule therefore holds for renders whose state ALSO rides `nvme_log_write` — `[SNAP]` carries the model/dimension/counters and is the one to read — and does NOT hold for `[PANEL]` itself unless BOOT_LOG is raised for that boot. Do not plan a UI proof on a `[PANEL]` line without checking the flag first;** — emit a periodic full-UI snapshot (all fields/widgets in one block) at init + a steady cadence; add a boot-relative timestamp (TSC→ms) so the log shows what **and when**. "UI state fully reconstructable from the log" is an **exit criterion** for every goal-#2+ UI slice. Caveat: the log proves **content, not pixels** — do ONE visual confirm per rendering/font/layout change, then trust the log. Gotcha: anything drawn before `nvme_log_init` is serial-only (re-emit it via the post-init snapshot).
- **UI must track real features (the UI is the living truth-map)** — every user-visible feature/capability MUST be reflected in the UI: primarily the **Remote Telemetry Console** (`phase4/console/`, fed by the `/events` SSE telemetry), secondarily the on-box HUD. When a feature ships, either surface its **real live signal** on the relevant console screen, or expose it in the console's auto-populated **Capabilities/Features** section (driven from real telemetry / a declared feature manifest — never hardcoded claims). Honesty corollary: the UI shows ONLY real, live state — a feature that exists but is missing from the UI is a **gap to close**; anything shown without a live source is **fiction to remove**. So "what's working vs what isn't (or isn't meant to be there)" stays obvious to both the user and the AI at a glance. A new feature means updating the console (or its feature section) **in the same change**; enforced by the console honesty gate (`phase4/console/test_console_honesty.py`).

## Key Technical Notes

### NVMe Write Logging

- Log region: LBA 4000794624 (past p4, the 1.7G vfat tail partition). Drive confirmed via lsblk: **Lexar NM790 2TB**, 4,000,797,360 sectors (1907 GiB). The log's 2701 sectors (header + 2700 entries) fit the ~2,736-sector tail with only ~35 sectors (~18 KB) of headroom — TIGHT. (nvme_log.h's header comments match — "Lexar NM790 2TB", "~2,736-sector tail, ~35 sectors spare".)
- Max entries: 2700 — **circular / rolling buffer** (keeps the latest 2700; never fills): `cursor` is the wrapping write slot (0..2699), `total_entries` is the monotonic lifetime count, and `nvme_log_cursor()` returns entries-stored-capped (rolling-full once total ≥ 2700). `nvme_log_write` no longer returns -2; a stale pre-wrap cursor (== 2700 from the old no-wrap build) is clamped on init. The telemetry `log_cursor` field value/shape is **unchanged** by this (identical until the first wrap) — no wire-contract change.
- Periodic stats cadence: serial `[STATS]` prints every 100 queries (free); the NVMe `LOG_IPC_STATS` entry is written every `JARVIS_STATS_NVME_INTERVAL` = **100** queries (`jarvis_debug.h`). Justified by the **measured** bare-metal rate ~3k queries/day (single core, scalar) → ~870 entries over 30 days, well under the 2700-entry cap. (Task 3 originally used 1000 on a 16k/day estimate the bare-metal run disproved; q=400 err=0 verified on real hardware 2026-06-15. If a future build speeds the query rate up ~5x+, raise this back toward 1000.)
- Format: 512-byte records (header + entries), XOR checksum
- Auto-capture: `puts_serial()` intercept writes every line to NVMe log
- Tags: `[VGA]` = visible on monitor, `[SER]` = serial only, `[PB]` = Process B via MSG_DEBUG IPC
- Recovery: `sudo dd if=/dev/nvme0n1 bs=512 skip=4000794624 count=2701 | python3 phase3/scripts/parse_nvme_log.py` (count=2701 = header + all 2700 slots; the parser reads wrap-order — oldest→newest from `cursor` — once the buffer has rolled)
- `seL4_Yield` only yields to equal-priority — PA and PB both at MaxPrio

### Bare-Metal Debugging Rules

- Use `#if EMMC_DEBUG` preprocessor guards for diagnostics, NOT code deletion (deleting debug code caused mysterious boot failures)
- `seL4_DebugPutChar()` works for TX without device frame mapping
- Device frame mapping required for UART RX
- Serial console: 115200 baud, 8N1
- `put_dec()` and `put_hex()` go through `putc_serial()` -> NVMe log. To suppress NVMe logging temporarily (e.g. verbose dumps), save/clear `g_nvme_ptr`.
- Process A and Process B run at `seL4_MaxPrio` (equal priority). `seL4_Yield` only works between equal-priority threads — MaxPrio-1 gets starved.
- Response ring has 15 slots. **`pb_can_log()` reserves 3 for MSG_RESPONSE — but this is INTENT, NOT SHIPPED FACT** (verified 2026-07-25, C/M1b pre-mortem): `pb_can_log` is `__attribute__((unused))` (`inference_server.c:151`) and its SOLE call site (`:164`) is inside `#if JARVIS_DBG_PB`, which is **default 0** — so in the deployed build the debug sends compile out entirely and **no ring reservation is enforced at runtime**. Harmless today (nothing competes), but it means there is **no existing backpressure mechanism to lean on**: the MSG_RESPONSE chunk loop DISCARDS `shmem_ipc_send`'s return code (`(void)rc`, `inference_server.c:500-516`) while that function returns −1 on a full ring, so a full ring would silently drop a chunk and the loop would advance. Latent only because `char text_out[512]` (`:479`) caps a response at ≤3 chunks. **Any future bulk payload (e.g. a 1024-float embedding = 18 chunks) must NOT go through this ring** — see `phase6/docs/PHASE_6_GOAL_C_M1B_DESIGN.md` §4.5.
- `qmodel_forward` stack budget: keep below ~8KB. Large temporaries (>4KB) MUST use `state->fwd_scratch` heap buffer. seL4 Process B has limited stack.
- **PA response consumption: POLL the ring, do NOT `seL4_Wait(resp_notif)` in the workload loop.** The inference path polls and never consumes `resp_notif`, leaving it signaled; heartbeat/shield used to `seL4_Wait(resp_notif)` + a single recv, which returned immediately on that stale signal and read before PB responded (~7% spurious errors, all in hb/shield). Fixed via `wait_for_response()` which polls the ring for the expected type and drains stale messages (race-free, like inference). The only legit `seL4_Wait(resp_notif)` is the pre-loop ready handshake. NOTE: the race is **Heisenbug** — enabling `JARVIS_DBG_IPC` serial prints inserts a scheduling window that masks it, so verify by err-rate ([STATS]) at `JARVIS_DBG_IPC=0`, not per-event logs.
- **The PB-touching poll budget is `POLL_TIMEOUT`/`poll_max`/`wpoll_max` = `5,000,000` ITERATIONS (`wait_for_response` for heartbeat+shield, the inline workload INFER poll in `main_continued`, `pa_ctrl_gate`'s `poll_max` for control-IN, and the wake dispatch site — all the same value). One iteration = ring-drain + `jarvis_telemetry_tick` (+ `pa_fault_check` on the ctrl lane) + `seL4_Yield`. THE WALL-TIME OF THAT BUDGET IS STATE-DEPENDENT AND WAS MEASURED FOR THE FIRST TIME AT N2 (KVM `-smp 6`, 2026-08-02) — the pervasive "~60–120 s on bare metal" comment was an ESTIMATE and is NOT confirmed:** a **WEDGED** PB (PB-main `seL4_TCB_Suspend`ed → PA spins the poll loop with nothing to yield to) exhausts the full 5,000,000 iterations in **~5.9 s** (`budget_ms=5924`, ~1.18 µs/iter); a **LIVE** generation completes far sooner in wall-time (a coherent control-IN answer in ~22 s) but consumes only **~25,000–66,000 ITERATIONS** (~0.85 ms/iter), because a live generation yields the shared core to PB every iteration and accrues iterations ~700× slower than a pure spin. **CONSEQUENCE, the N2 finding: at the deployed 5M-iteration budget a live-but-slow generation and a wedge ARE distinguishable — the wedge reaches 5M (≈5.9 s in KVM), a live generation completes 2–3 orders of magnitude below it — so §2's "a slow-but-alive generation is indistinguishable from a wedged PB" premise is NOT reproduced at this budget.** The measured wall-times are KVM's; bare-metal wall-time is a carry-forward that can ride a future supervised boot's probe (KVM `-smp 6` reaches ~bare-metal tok/s but scheduling differs). See the `JARVIS_PB_TICK` flag row for the liveness-tick mechanism built (gated default-0) that would make the extend path load-bearing only if the deploy window were ever shrunk to speed wedge detection.

### Quick Reference
- **Phase 2 (Pi 4) pointers, Build/Deploy/Serial:** `phase2/CLAUDE.md` (auto-loads under `phase2/`; moved 2026-09-02, verbatim)
- **Net Stack:** `phase2/src/drivers/net_stack.c` — **and its phase3 sibling `phase3/src/drivers/net_stack.c`, where TWO defects were fixed/dispositioned 2026-08-11. (1) THE ICMP CHECKSUM READ PAST THE REPLY BUFFER, fixed: `handle_icmp` clamped the COPY (`total_len = min(ETH_HLEN+ip_total, len, NET_MAX_FRAME)`) but derived the CHECKSUM length from the UNCLAMPED `ip_total`, so `net_checksum(rep_icmp, icmp_len)` read to `ETH_HLEN + ip_total` — up to 64 bytes past a 1536-byte reply on a 1600-byte frame. Now `icmp_len = total_len - icmp_off` with an underflow guard, which is VALUE-IDENTICAL for every in-contract frame (unclamped ⇒ `total_len == ETH_HLEN + ip_total` ⇒ the two expressions are equal); only an already-truncated oversized frame changes, and there it now checksums the bytes actually copied. **THE ORDER IS THE PROOF: the harness was tightened FIRST and the gate went RED — `AddressSanitizer: heap-buffer-overflow`, `READ of size 1`, `0 bytes after 1536-byte region`, frames `net_checksum` ← `net_process_frame` ← `nfs_call` — and only then was the fix applied; Not in the deployed x86 image - the phase3 copy is compiled by neither build_jarvis_x86.sh nor either vendored CMakeLists, its only callers the fuzzer and unit suite; the ICMP checksum overrun was fixed 2026-08-11 (ASan red then green, test_net_stack 35/35 under both CI flags and ASan) while the x86 lifetime ICMP rate cap was dispositioned, not fixed. re-run EXIT=0, 0 ASan errors, `test_net_stack` 35/35 under both CI flags and ASan.** **TWO THINGS HAD TO BE SOLVED, and conflating them would have made the fix unprovable: VISIBILITY** (the harness's `uint8_t reply[2048]` STACK array let the overrun land in slack it owned — in-bounds for ASan, out-of-contract for the function, which is why ~900K sanitized iterations never reported it) **and REACHABILITY** (even exact-size buffers stayed green, because a random 1600-byte frame must simultaneously carry a valid IP header checksum, `dst_ip == our_ip`, protocol ICMP, no fragment bits and ICMP type 8/code 0 — the corpus has never once satisfied that conjunction, exactly as the coverage instrument independently measured). The red needed a DIRECTED oversized well-formed echo request, added as sub-test 8. **(2) THE x86 ICMP RATE LIMIT IS A LIFETIME CAP, dispositioned not fixed:** that `#else` arm has no clock, so `icmp_reply_count` never resets and the box stops answering pings permanently after 10 replies — `ICMP_MAX_REPLIES_PER_SEC` is a misnomer there, and `net_icmp_rate_reset()` has no caller outside `test_net_stack.c` (measured, with `net_init`'s callers as the positive control). Same shape as the K/M2b-2 `KM2B_HEALTHY_RESET` defect. A correct window needs a monotonic source this arm does not have (the Pi4 arm uses `systimer_read()`), so the constraint is named in a comment rather than faked with a call counter. **NEITHER IS IN THE DEPLOYED IMAGE — verified, and it is why neither was urgent: `phase3/src/drivers/net_stack.c` is copied by NEITHER `build_jarvis_x86.sh` NOR either vendored CMakeLists**, and its only phase3 callers are the fuzzer and the unit suite; the one production caller anywhere is phase2's separate copy (same `icmp_len` line) driven from `main_arm64.c` with self-built FIXED 42/74-byte frames, so the `len > 1536` precondition cannot arise there. **THE REPLY-BUFFER CONTRACT IS NOW DOCUMENTED in `net_stack.h` — `reply_buf` must be at least `min(len, NET_MAX_FRAME)` — because it was UNSTATED, and that was part of the defect: every caller guessed a different size (64 in phase2's `arp_reply`, 128 in the unit tests, 2048 in the fuzzer), each adequate only by coincidence for the frames it happens to pass.** Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — Net Stack".
- **Phase 3 Final Report:** `phase3/docs/PHASE_3_FINAL_REPORT.md` — beta summary (soak/scaling/TQ-RQ deferred; v0.2.1-beta tagged 06de75c)
- **Decision records (ADRs):** `docs/decisions/` — dynamic-scaling removal (2026-04-17), 30-day soak deferral + TurboQuant/RotorQuant deferral (2026-06-15), GPU-inference deferral (2026-06-16), x86 verification stance (2026-06-16), **enable SMP / Branch A (2026-06-17)**, **close Phase 6 + defer the supervised 6-7 run as owner-scheduled (2026-09-05)**
- **Phase 4 Goal #1 (Inference Perf) Plan:** `phase4/docs/PHASE_4_GOAL1_INFERENCE_PERF.md` — AVX2/XSAVE + SMP findings (VERIFIED) + M0–M4 plan (all DONE — goal #1 CPU COMPLETE 2026-06-18)
- **Phase 4 Goal #1 Benchmark (M4):** `phase4/docs/PHASE_4_GOAL1_BENCHMARK.md` — recorded seL4-build tok/s (Gemma 4 E2B **5.46 @ NN=6**, 3.57× 1T; progression + F1/F2 sweep tables + methodology + honest target-vs-actual)
- **Phase 4 Final Report:** `phase4/docs/PHASE_4_FINAL_REPORT.md` — v1.0 final report (what shipped per goal, metrics scorecard, honest success-criteria table — #3 keyboard CUT / #5 soak NOT run, #7 release in progress — limitations, Phase 5 handoff, fresh codebase stats); mirrors `phase3/docs/PHASE_3_FINAL_REPORT.md`

- **Phase 5 Plan (Memory):** `phase5/docs/PHASE_5_PLAN.md` — keystone-first plan (mission + 5 canon done-when; "it-remembers" MVP arc #1/#2/#3/#6 then #4/#5/#7; locked decisions; VERIFIED on-box NVMe free-space map + raw-LBA storage decision in the ~1.66 TiB gap; risks; Phase 5/6/7 boundary). Mirrors `phase4/docs/ROADMAP.md`.
- **Phase 7 Plan (Autonomy):** `phase7/docs/PHASE_7_PLAN.md` — ACTIVE 2026-09-05; §0 is the LIVE STATUS BOARD (one row per goal-arc-milestone; a cell reads DONE only with the commit hash and date, written in the same commit that lands the work); canon quoted verbatim; the first arc is the operator's choice; the wearable is goal 8, CANON since 2026-09-05 (operator decision; scope per the idea doc's §8 — owner-only enrollment, the wife guessed, transcribe-everything with raw audio deleted and a manual purge) with stage rows owner-voice / pipeline / memory research / store + the guess / profile view / digest / V0 / V1 / V2 and V3 BLOCKED-decided-last; the carried hardening backlog sits under the board.
- **Phase 7 Memory Research (the substrate goals 7.1, 7.5 and 7.8 share):** `phase7/docs/PHASE_7_MEMORY_RESEARCH.md` — the state-of-the-art survey, 2026-09-06, strategist-authored from a five-angle search: 24 sources, 120 falsifiable claims with quotes, 25 adversarially voted by three verifiers each (9 V / 12 R / 4 X — **in 24 of 25 the numbers were confirmed against the primary source; every R refutes FRAMING, never a figure**). Nine design implications, re-read after the votes in §8a: three layers over one provenance spine (an event layer that is an INDEX over the preserved transcript, never the truth; typed bitemporal profile facts with non-destructive supersession; a people layer whose relationships carry confidence and evidence turns); the LLM off the write path wherever a rule exists, every loss an audit row (Mem0's shipped v3 dropped LLM UPDATE/DELETE for ADD-only, Graphiti ships deletes its paper's model omits — two vendors' scars); inference that never feeds itself; a SEPARATE preference and style model (the preference gap is real as a number and unproven as a mechanism); our OWN pre-registered household benchmark (the field's numbers are real, its comparisons are not); 2070-feasible by construction (the deployed Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B, a measured small extractor, BM25 as the baseline lane, a single-process store chosen by measurement); forgetting = demotion, never erasure; the box receives a projection, not the store. Honest ceiling: every measured system is wrong a large fraction of the time at conflict resolution, relationship inference and preference transfer; the state of the art is mechanisms that make wrongness visible, reversible and measurable. The design followed on 2026-09-06: `phase7/docs/PHASE_7_MEMORY_DESIGN.md` (the next row).
- **Phase 7 Memory Design (the household memory store; approved by the operator 2026-09-06):** `phase7/docs/PHASE_7_MEMORY_DESIGN.md` — strategist-authored, plan-first, the design goals 7.1, 7.5 and 7.8 share. Approach A of three: ONE SQLite file as the provenance spine (spans = the verbatim ground truth; events an INDEX over spans; typed bitemporal profile facts; people + relationship edges carrying confidence and evidence spans; a SEPARATE preference-and-style model; an audit row for every loss); the extractor runs LOCAL ONLY on the RTX 2070 (household transcripts never leave the Main PC; a cloud model may generate SYNTHETIC benchmark transcripts only) and SELECTS predicates from a static registry (the K-b select-never-synthesise instinct applied to memory); seven write rules — append-only; source rank owner-stated > other-stated > inferred; newest valid-from wins by comparison, never by a model's judgement; multi-valued predicates coexist; confidence recomputed from spans every time (1 − exp(−(D_s − D_c)/3 days), surfaced at ≥ 0.80), so a wrong belief cannot feed itself; decay only demotes; the owner's purge is the only delete; three retrieval lanes (FTS5/BM25, the deployed Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B, a two-hop graph walk over people edges) with the verbatim span always attached; the guess = a cluster earns personhood after 3 distinct days and the owner→cluster edge's confidence accrues over days, surfaced with its evidence and never confirmed; a pre-registered household benchmark with bands fixed before code (five sets — updates, coexisting facts, preference transfer as retrieval recall, who-is-what-to-whom, evidence-preserving growth; MS0 oracle candidates ≥ 95 % update/coexist, MS2 extracted ≥ 85 %, the spouse edge surfaced in ≥ 8 of 10 synthetic households with 0 wrong relations); the box receives a PROJECTION into its existing JSEM store (4096 × 512 B @ LBA 21,110,000, a new `SEM_FACT_PROFILE` type) by OFFLINE provisioning under the JCON discipline — a live push is a later slice with its own ADR. Milestones, each a board row: MS0 the store + rules + harness → MS1 the extractor bake-off (Gemma 4 E2B, Llama 3.1 8B 4-bit, an encoder extractor; JSON validity ≥ 99 %, F1 floor 0.60) → MS2 real transcripts + the people layer + the guess → MS3 the projection tool. Honest ceiling: every measured system is wrong often at exactly these tasks; the promise is that wrongness is visible, reversible and measured — never "knows" or "understands".
- **Memory Store (Phase 7 goal 8, MS0, Main PC):** `phase7/memory/` (`jarvis_memory`: registry / candidate / confidence / freshness / rules / retrieve / people / schema / store / paths / `__main__`; `bench/` corpus + harness; `bench_ms0.py`; data under `%USERPROFILE%\.jarvis\memory\`, `*.sqlite*` ignored) — the pure core and the SQLite store of the design's §3–§6, the full-text lane only (the embedding lane is MS1); stdlib-only, no model, no GPU, nothing about the owner. `test_memory_logic.py` **114 checks** → CI "Phase 7: memory store logic (Python, stdlib-only)"; the MS0 benchmark on the seeded template corpus (10 households, ORACLE candidates) → CI "Phase 7: memory store benchmark, MS0 bands (Python, stdlib-only)" (3 households in CI; **`--assert-bands` deliberately NOT passed while one band stands as a published miss — the reason is written above the step in `ci.yml`**): **update 96.25 %, coexisting 100 %, audit 100 % (0 violations), p99 0.354 ms at 100k facts (p50 0.0985) — four bands PASS; growth drop 46.25 points against a ≤ 5 band MISSED**, and the miss is the milestone's finding, measured not argued: 30× filler puts `lives in` and `works as` into half the documents each, IDF collapses to ≈ 0.02, and BM25 length-normalisation then returns the shorter `lives_in` fact for every *works_as* question — a diagnostic with the filler moved to a predicate the update set never asks about gives a 0.0 drop, so the cause is predicate-term dilution in the full-text lane, not index size, the ranker, or a collision with the gold facts. Nothing was retuned (corpus, rendering, ranker weights, `TAU_DAYS`, the 0.80 threshold are all the design's). transfer recall@5 **0 %** and relation precision **1.0** REPORTED, never banded (the full-text lane cannot find a scenario that shares no word with the preference — the MS1 band; and precision 1.0 is by construction, the oracle plants only true relations). **The guess behaved exactly as pre-registered: the inferred owner→partner `spouse` edge crossed 0.80 on day 8 in 10/10 households (fifth distinct supporting day, 0.8111), dipped to 0.7364 after the day-11 contradiction, finished 0.8647.** Log: `phase7/docs/PHASE_7_MEMORY_LOG.md`; results `phase7/memory/bench/results/ms0_run.json` (synthetic, 5,828 B).

Second carry-forward — **COMMENT CORRECTED IN PLACE 2026-07-26 (comment-only, zero behaviour change; `main.c.obj` `.text`/`.rodata`/`.data` + `nm` byte-identical, DWARF-only whole-file delta).** Canon (`ROADMAP.md:90`): "conversation is the default interface … gated on the full security checklist: auth + HMAC, real SHIELD (close SEC-039), rate-limiting, a hardened/fuzzed inbound parser, and ideally a less-privileged input process (SEC-014)." Honest reading locked: a control-IN message is a QUERY (joins the SAME cache/inference path, returns TEXT; K-b holds — inbound text can never mint an action) — NOT a shell, NOT command execution, NOT the "operates a workspace" arc (Beyond-Phase-7). The 6-item HARD checklist: (1) hardened/fuzzed inbound parser (fragment-reject) · (2) auth + HMAC-SHA256 (net-new bare-metal, constant-time verify) + nonce/sequence replay (floor PERSISTS across reboot) · (3) rate-limit backed by SCHEDULING (input process pinned off the PA core) · (4) real QUERY SHIELD closing SEC-039 for queries (a query threat model — injection/jailbreak — NOT the action keyword blocklist, which false-positives + has zero injection coverage) · (5) a less-privileged SEC-014 input process (a SUBTRACTION from the PB-spawn cap flow — RX buffer pages + a doorbell + one shmem ring, **NOT BAR0**) · (6) the I211 **RX** bring-up (VIRGIN surface — RX code exists but is gated-NULL `main_x86.c:3339`, never run; the guarded init also OMITS `RXDCTL.ENABLE`, the TX-B2 analog). inbound Q&A is never WRITTEN to episodic (→ the done-when needs the control-IN write, not a synthetic fact); "not yet" is the correct default until "provably safe." **FLIP EVIDENCE (M4e, 2026-07-21, boot_id=30, supervised bare metal):** deployed image md5 `ba93fe4ecf8b098e1ccbc513bdbb6d76` verified byte-identical to a rebuild from committed source **[CORRECTED 2026-08-10: that claim was FALSE FOR PROCESS B and is left in place rather than deleted, because it is what the record said at the time. PB was built from `src/ipc/shmem_ipc.c`, a file the script copied nowhere and the repo therefore did not control — see the Shared Memory IPC row. A rebuild from committed source would NOT have reproduced this image's PB. Note the SEPARATE and still-TRUE claims of the form "byte-equal to the box-built binary": those compare the ESP against what the box built, not against source, and are unaffected. Fixed `148551a`; from that commit the phrase is true for both processes. DEPLOYED 2026-08-10: boot_id=52 runs image `244ce42ebb74b61436d01e1529b3c464`, built from `148551a`-inclusive source — from that boot the claim is TRUE again for the DEPLOYED image too.]**; Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — Phase 6 Goal 6-5 (Control-IN / Natural-Language Primary) — ✅ COMPLETE 2026-07-21 (`a9c1d9a`): `JARVIS_CONTROL_IN` FLIPPED DEFAULT-ON, all 6 checklist items closed, the deployed image is TWO-WAY.".

- **Embed Vector-Parity Harness (Phase C / C/M1a Stage 2, HOST-ONLY, model-gated-local):** `phase3/src/ai/embed_vector_probe.c` — loads a Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B GGUF through the DEPLOYED engine (`qmodel_load` → `gguf_vocab`/`tokenizer` → `qmodel_embed_last`), encodes the 15 golden probes, appends EOS 151643, and writes 15×1024 raw LE float32 vectors for the comparator. MEASURE-FIRST: prints the loaded arch/`rope_neox`/`embed_scale`/`use_gelu`/`rope_theta` and **hard-FAILs if a qwen arch loaded with `rope_neox=0`** (the silent-parity breaker). Self-guards `#ifndef JARVIS_EMBED → 0` (inert stub in a default build); takes the model path as `argv[1]`, so ONE binary serves both the Q8_0 and F32 runs. Uses 64-bit file offsets (`_fseeki64` on Windows — `long` is 32-bit there, and an F32 GGUF of this model is 2.38 GB, so plain `ftell` overflows and the read fails before any inference; verified behavior-neutral — Q8_0 output md5-identical before/after). **NEVER in the deployed build or CI.** Companion `phase3/src/ai/embed_host_fmemopen.h` — a `_WIN32`-only `fmemopen` PROTOTYPE force-included (`-include`) into every host TU so `gguf_parser.c` compiles under the MSVC CRT (which lacks POSIX `fmemopen`); the DEFINITION lives in the harness (DELETE_ON_CLOSE temp-file backed, self-cleaning), keeping `<windows.h>` and its `min`/`max`/`ERROR` macros out of the engine sources. Host-harness support ONLY — never compiled into the seL4 build or CI.
- **Embed Vector-Parity GATE (Phase C / C/M1a Stage 2):** `phase3/scripts/embed/cm1a_vector_parity.py` — the committed comparator. Takes the C Q8_0 vectors plus `--c-f32` (the matched-precision run) and prints ALL 15 per-probe cosines/gaps/max-abs BEFORE any verdict: **(a) GATE 2′** C-F32 vs the ST F32 golden = THE port-correctness gate, on **pre-registered bands** (≤1e-5 noise / ≤1e-4 arithmetic-order / >1e-4 = real discrepancy — fixed in advance so a verdict cannot be re-based after seeing numbers); **(b)** C-F32 vs C-Q8_0 = our OWN weight-quant delta, sanity-checked against llama.cpp's measured 8.45e-4; **(c)** C-Q8_0 vs the llama.cpp Q8_0 golden = the cross-engine **DIAGNOSTIC** (the original GATE 2 — reported with its "would have said FAIL" verdict stated honestly, never silently passed); plus **GATE 3** at the measured floor. Exits 0 only if GATE 2′ AND GATE 3 pass; exits 2 (INCOMPLETE) if `--c-f32` is omitted, so the port gate can never be skipped by accident. Model-gated-local (no CI step).
- **Embed Region staleness predicate (Phase C / C/M1b-3, host):** `phase3/src/ipc/embed_region.c/h` — the PA↔PB embedding transport's control header + `embed_result_usable()`. The PA to PB embedding transport, wired 2026-07-30 behind JARVIS_EMBED - MSG_EMBED 0x12 and MSG_EMBED_RESULT 0x14 over a dedicated 2-page region, shared frames 3 to 5 via one named SHMEM_FRAME_COUNT, KM2B_LANE_EMBED = 7 attribution only; test_embed_region.c 15 PASS into CI Phase C: C/M1b-3 embed region staleness (C). **A 1024-float vector does NOT travel through the response ring** (4096 B = 18 chunks into a 15-slot, 240 B-payload ring — impossible regardless of how well the chunk loop handles a full ring), so it travels through a DEDICATED 2-page region and the ring carries only a small typed request/completion. That leaves exactly ONE place a subtle bug hides — deciding whether the bytes in the page answer the request you just made — which is pure logic, so it is extracted and host-tested per the unbroken precedent (`km2b_miss`/`km2b_trigger`/`wake`/`control_floor`/`control_console`; `main_x86.c`/`inference_server.c` are seL4-only and NEVER host-compiled). **`seq` is compared by EQUALITY, never ordering** — at the uint32 wrap the newest seq is numerically the SMALLEST, so any `>=`/`>` inverts exactly once every 2³² requests. **`_Static_assert(EMBED_VEC_BYTES == 4096)`** pins that 1024 floats × 4 B is exactly one page with zero spare — the dimension is NOT permanent (the Phase C plan contemplates Matryoshka truncation to 128), so a future change must BREAK THE BUILD rather than silently corrupt a page boundary. **PB echoes the REQUEST's seq, never a counter of its own** — that is what makes a LATE result detectable, and it forced the request counter to be **uint16**, because `shmem_ipc_send`'s seq parameter is uint16 and 16 bits is all PB can be told. **`KM2B_LANE_EMBED = 7` is ATTRIBUTION ONLY — an embed timeout deliberately does NOT advance the miss counter and never touches `q_errors`:** PB serves one message at a time, so a request issued mid-generation legitimately waits up to ~46 s, and feeding those to the shared counter would let THREE of them restart a healthy PB. **Recall itself was never the problem:** recall@3 = **1.000** in all four configs; **Tag-4 (`EPI_ACT_CONTROL_IN_LOCAL`) exclusion is MORE load-bearing under cosine than under exact-key** — a stale *"up 476 seconds"* can semantically match an unrelated uptime question — and thought-marked records are excluded at SELECT time so they cannot occupy a slot and crowd out a good record. Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — Embed Region staleness predicate (Phase C / C/M1b-3, host)".
- **Embedding Vector Store (Phase C / C/M2a, host):** `phase3/src/ai/embed_store.c/h` — the durable per-record vector store, so semantic recall does not re-embed the corpus at boot. Durable per-record vector store, magic JVEC over 21,150,000 .. 21,158,192 (8193 sectors, 4.00 MiB) with two-sector records carrying the owner seq and query_key so desync is detectable per record; test_embed_store.c 13 tests / 115 asserts into CI Phase C: C/M2a Embedding vector store (C); off-box reader since f2ee438. **SECTOR ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING — vector at +0, identity at +1**, so one `write(lba,2,…)` publishes the payload before the thing that claims it (the `embed_region.c` release-store discipline). **APPEND-OR-NO-OP, NOT UPSERT** (`embed_store_put` → 0 inserted / 1 already present): a vector is a deterministic function of its query under a frozen model and `mu`, so it never legitimately changes; Carries the C/M2 caveat verbatim: **"0 false out of 36" is NO FAILURES OBSERVED, not a zero rate** (95% upper bound ≈ 8%) — never write that false recall is zero, prevented or eliminated. **Deliberately does NOT `nvme_flush`** (the primitive exists, wired for the control-IN replay floor at 6-5/M4c-fix): the floor flushes because losing it reopens a SECURITY window, whereas losing a vector costs a RECALL and falls back to exact-key — which is exactly what the whole `JARVIS_EMBED=0` path does today, i.e. Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — Embedding Vector Store (Phase C / C/M2a, host)".
- **Embedding Vector Store reader (Phase C, host):** `phase3/scripts/parse_embed_store.py` + `phase3/scripts/test_parse_embed_store.py` (+ a `--dump <path>` mode on `phase3/src/ai/test_embed_store.c`) — the JVEC store was the LAST raw-LBA store with no off-box reader, so a vector store that semantic recall depends on could only be inspected by the box itself. Decodes the header (magic/version/cursor/total/boot_id/dim/checksum) and every 1024 B record (`vec[128]` @+0, `owner_key` @+512, `boot_id` @+520, `seq` @+524, `owner_seq` @+528, `dim` @+532, `status` @+536, `checksum` @+540), reporting per-record `checksum_ok` and `l2_norm` — the norm matters because cosine == dot ONLY for unit vectors, so a non-unit record is a silent magnitude-ranking bug and the reader surfaces it. C→Python round-trip against a deterministic fixture from the REAL `embed_store.c` → CI "Phase C: Embedding-store parser round-trip (C -> Python)".

- **The frozen mean-projection direction `mu` (Phase C / C/M2b prerequisite):** `phase3/src/ai/embed_mu.h` (GENERATED — do not hand-edit) + its generator `phase3/scripts/embed/gen_embed_mu.py` + the anchor test `phase3/scripts/embed/test_embed_mu.py` + the C round-trip test `phase3/src/ai/test_embed_mu.c`. Frozen GENERATED constants, 1024 dims, in C99 hex float, pinned to EMBED_DIM by a _Static_assert and anchored bit-exactly to golden_vectors.npz; storage only, applied by embed_project.c; Python 23 checks / 7 groups plus C 22 asserts / 5 groups into CI Phase C: mu bit-exactness vs golden_vectors.npz (Python) and Phase C: mu compiler round-trip (C). Mechanism follows the `gen_control_vectors.py` → `sha256_vectors.h` precedent: 4 KB of frozen constants belongs in version control, NOT in a raw-LBA sector needing provisioning, a read path and a failure mode for a value that never changes. **LITERAL FORM: C99 hex float (`%a`), never decimal**, emitted as `static const float` so the box takes `const float *` with zero runtime unpack. **A CORRECTION worth keeping, because the usual justification for that choice is wrong: decimal at 9 significant digits is NOT unsafe** — `FLT_DECIMAL_DIG` is 9 precisely because 9 digits round-trip binary32, and measured over this exact `mu`, `%.9g` gave **0/1024** round-trip failures. **⚠️ THE FINDING THAT MATTERS MOST — the prompt's premise was falsified: the floor was NOT measured on the npz bits.** Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — The frozen mean-projection direction `mu` (Phase C / C/M2b prerequisite)".
- **C/M2b — SEMANTIC RECALL WIRED, GATED INERT (`JARVIS_EMBED` still 0):** PA gains a vector store + in-RAM index at init (**`embed_mu_verify()` gates FIRST — a wrong `mu` is a wrong FLOOR, so it fails closed**), an **exact-key-first** recall lane that falls through to `g3_select_semantic` on **`cplen == 0`** (a MISS *or* a fragment-suppressed hit — measured as the common case: 8 of 14 recorded hits, and only 22/62 stored records recallable), **embed-on-write AFTER `ctrl_send_reply`** (gated on `ctrl_epi_write`'s own `g3_candidate_usable` result, not a restatement), and a **bounded backfill** of `EMBED_BACKFILL_PER_BOOT` 8, ONE per `[STATS]` window. NEVER "semantic recall works".** **LEG A — the box REPRODUCES the host measurement**, and it closes a real hole: every prior parity check stopped at the RAW 1024 vector, so projection/truncation/renormalisation/cosine on the box had never been compared to the run that produced 0.55. **[MEASURED AND CLOSED 2026-08-09 — `phase3/scripts/embed/punct_remeasure.py` + `punct_remeasure_results.json`. Done with DIRECTED CASES, not more iterations: the three random modes in each fuzzer are byte-for-byte the same 100,000 apiece, and the directed mode runs LAST and consumes no RNG so those modes draw the identical stream they drew before it existed; `fuzz_query_shield` 300,000 -> 300,010 and `fuzz_route` 300,000 -> 300,012. Directed cases assert the HARNESS CONTRACT ONLY (crash-freedom + the result-struct invariant) and deliberately pin NO expected verdict/class — a semantic expectation belongs to the unit suite, and asserting one in a fuzzer would make it fail on a legitimate reviewed retune of the pattern or keyword tables. **THE FINDING THAT MATTERED IS ABOUT THE INSTRUMENT, NOT THE FUZZERS: at -O0 gcov attributes each clause of a MULTI-LINE `||` chain to the line BEFORE it**, so two of the eight judgements named the WRONG CLAUSE. Measured, not inferred: four `been running` inputs produced a true-count of exactly 4 on the line holding `been up`, and `route.c:269` showed outcomes totalling exactly the 5 inputs that fell through to the next clause — so the ":269 rt_seq2(been,running)" gap was really `been on` (line 270) and the ":296 (... day is ...)" gap was really `date is` (line 297). Aiming at the quoted clause covered a different line and left the reported gap open, which reads exactly like a directed case not working; `have you been on` and `what date is it` closed both immediately (outcome counts 0 -> 1 in each). **The LINE NUMBER is reliable; the SOURCE TEXT printed beside it may belong to the next clause down** — recorded as `_instrument_line_attribution_warning` in `coverage_judgements.json`, with both affected entries corrected in place rather than deleted. The reachability judgements were RIGHT; only the attribution was wrong. Repo worth-fuzzing **161 → 150** — **CORRECTED 2026-08-12 from the "162 → 150" first recorded here, and the correction is worth more than the number.** 162 was the baseline BEFORE the ICMP commit (`3bf126c`), which itself moved it to 161 (−1 as the directed oversized-ICMP case reached the `NET_MAX_FRAME` clamp, +1 as its own underflow-guard ternary entered as unreachable-by-construction). So the aim commit's true predecessor is 161, and only that reconciles with its own component deltas: −10 gguf and −1 net_stack = −11, and 161 − 11 = 150 exactly, whereas 162 − 11 = 151. **THE LESSON, attached to the number because that is where it will be read: this passed review because the AFTER figure was verified against a fresh `coverage_report.json` and the BEFORE figure was taken on trust from the preceding record — a delta is two measurements, and checking one of them is checking neither.** Verified from the committed artifacts themselves: `a66eebc` 162, `3bf126c` 161, `cc70e3d` 150. `cc70e3d`'s commit body carries the stale 162 and CANNOT be amended — a record is a snapshot. **HONEST SCOPE: this buys COVERAGE, not correctness — "fuzzed" here means "reached", nothing more.** And pointing ASan at a deployed parser body for the first time surfaced **NO `gguf_parser.c` finding**: 0 ASan/UBSan diagnostics under `halt_on_error=1`.]** **THE HEADLINE FINDING IS ABOUT THE FUZZ CORPUS, NOT THE MODULES: across ~99,560 fuzz opens NOT ONE gguf buffer has ever parsed past the header** — `fuzz_harness.c` sub-test 4 sets `buf[4]=3` but leaves version bytes 5–7 RANDOM, so the u32 version check rejects ~everything (probability ~2/2²⁴ of passing), and sub-test 2 zeroes them but leaves n_tensors/n_kv random for SEC-008 to reject — so the entire parse pipeline behind the version gate (read_kv_pair, skip_gguf_value, read_gguf_string's over-long handler, read_tensor_info incl. the never-fired `n_dims > GGUF_MAX_DIMS` bounds guard, both type switches) is unit-fixture-covered ONLY. net_stack's mirror: the ARP sub-test builds frames of `14 + rand_range(28)` = 14–41 bytes, ONE byte short of the 42 a full ARP frame needs, so no random ARP frame has ever parsed either. The 162 worth-fuzzing entries ARE the recorded follow-ups (concrete input shape named per entry in `coverage_judgements.json`); fixing the two harness aim defects is a separate change — no code was touched in the judgement commit. **[THE AIM FIX LANDED AT `cc70e3d`, AND IT FALSIFIED THE STATED REASON OF 32 ENTRIES THAT THE INSTRUMENT WENT ON RE-EMITTING VERBATIM — re-judged 2026-08-12 against the FIXED corpus, rewritten or reclassified with an argument, never blanked. Worse than a stale comment, because the judgements file is what a future session consults INSTEAD of re-deriving. THE REACHABILITY WAS MEASURED, NOT REASONED: a scratch probe replicating the fixed sub-test 4's exact input space over its 10,000 buffers found **109 parse successfully and ALL 109 are the `n_tensors==0 && n_kv==0` draws — ZERO with any KV, ZERO with any tensor**. So the fix opened exactly two things: the success return (`gguf_open_memory:423/427`) and the error-propagation branches that now fire INSIDE `read_kv_pair`/`read_tensor_info` instead of at the version gate (`:230`, `:359`). It did NOT make the type switches reachable — a random body's u64 string length is astronomically likely to be huge, so `read_gguf_string` takes its over-long path and fails at the FIRST key or tensor name, and `read_kv_pair`'s scalar cases and both `ggml_type_size`/`ggml_type_block_size` switches still need a DIRECTED buffer carrying a short length prefix. That distinction is what the rewritten reasons now say.]** Also flagged for disposition: `gguf_get_kv_i32`/`gguf_get_kv_u64` have ZERO callers repo-wide (re-grepped with positive controls — the `shmem_ipc_reset` class), and `gguf_read_tensor_data`'s `(long)abs_offset` cast is a low-severity observation (u64 wrap + LLP64 truncation; host-tools-only exposure, fails safe on LP64), reported not fixed. **⚠️ **[FIXED 2026-08-11 — see the `net_stack` row for the ASan-red-then-green proof and the harness defect that hid it; the report-and-stop record below is preserved as the finding that led there.]** THE JUDGEMENT PASS ALSO SURFACED A REAL DEFECT IN `net_stack.c`, REPORTED-AND-STOPPED PER THE NO-FIX RULE — and it is recorded here rather than fixed because the fix is a separate reviewed change on an untrusted-input parser. `handle_icmp`'s reply builder CLAMPS the copy but NOT the checksum:** `total_len` is clamped to `min(ETH_HLEN+ip_total, len, NET_MAX_FRAME)` so the `memcpy(reply, frame, total_len)` is bounded — but three lines later `icmp_len = ip_total - ip_hdr_len` uses the **UNCLAMPED** `ip_total`, so `net_checksum(reply + icmp_off, icmp_len)` reads up to `ETH_HLEN + ip_total` bytes from `reply`. VERIFIED BY READING THE SOURCE, not taken from the agent: the `:230` guard bounds `ip_total <= len - ETH_HLEN`, so the read is bounded by the RECEIVED length and the overrun needs `len > NET_MAX_FRAME` (1536) against a 1536-byte reply buffer — e.g. a 1600-byte frame with `total_len=1586` reads 64 bytes past a `uint8_t[NET_MAX_FRAME]` reply. **`net_process_frame` has NO upper length guard (only `len >= ETH_HLEN`), and every other precondition is sender-controllable** (valid IP checksum, our IP, unfragmented ICMP echo request). **EXPOSURE TODAY IS NIL, and that is why it is recorded rather than hot-fixed — measured, not assumed: `phase3/src/drivers/net_stack.c` is NOT copied to the box tree and appears in NO vendored CMakeLists, so it is not in the deployed x86 image at all** (its only phase3 callers are the fuzzer and the unit suite); **the only production caller anywhere is Phase 2's SEPARATE copy** — which carries the identical line (`phase2/.../net_stack.c:271`) — **driven from `main_arm64.c` with self-built FIXED 42-byte and 74-byte frames, so the `len > 1536` precondition cannot occur there.** It goes live the moment `net_process_frame` is wired to a real RX path with a `NET_MAX_FRAME` reply buffer. **A SECOND, LOWER-SEVERITY FINDING in the same function: the x86 `#else` arm of the ICMP rate limit has no timer, so `ICMP_MAX_REPLIES_PER_SEC` is a LIFETIME cap — `net_icmp_rate_reset()` has callers only in `test_net_stack.c` (5 of them; `net_init`'s 4 files as the positive control), so a real x86 deployment would stop answering pings permanently after 10 replies.** Neither is fixed; both are the strategist's call.** **THE SINGLED-OUT QUESTION, ANSWERED: the `shmem_ipc` ring guards are NOT reached.** Both `ring->header.size == 0 || > SHMEM_RING_SLOTS` guards (send `:69`, recv `:109`) and the `return -1` at `:70` are uncovered, and the mechanism is measured rather than guessed: `fuzz_shmem_ipc()` is described as "send/recv with corrupted ring data" and it DOES randomise the indices (`fuzz_harness.c:185-186`) but NEVER touches `header.size`, while every sub-test calls `shmem_ipc_init()` first, which sets size = 15. So the guard's true outcome cannot occur for any input the harness can produce — judged `reachable-worth-fuzzing` with the concrete fix (one sub-test setting `header.size` to 0 and to > 15 before send/recv), and it matters because this is the module whose two implementations diverged for 4.5 months and this guard is what stands between a bad size and slot addressing past the 4096 B page. **[WRITTEN + COVERED 2026-08-11, the closures sweep: sub-test 7's directed corrupted sizes {0, 16, 255} against both send and recv + a restored-size round-trip positive control; all 3 gaps measured covered on the instrument re-run, and the judgement entries are retained inert with a precision correction — recv's `size>15` outcome had been reached by sub-test 6's full-random fill all along; its real gap was `size==0` alone.]** **A SECOND FINDING, flagged as a disposition question rather than a coverage chore: `shmem_ipc_reset()` is DEAD CODE** — zero callers anywhere in `phase3` outside its own definition and declaration (measured), so all 5 of its lines are uncovered and nothing in the deployed image calls it. Filed `unreachable-by-construction` knowingly, because the three-value taxonomy still has no "dead code, delete-or-justify" value — the same limitation `_taxonomy_note` records for `route_decline_text`. **[RESOLVED 2026-08-11 (closures sweep): DELETED — zero callers re-verified, definition + declaration removed, its 6 judgement entries REMOVED rather than retained (deleted source can never re-bind under the stale-keying rule, unlike covered code, which can regress); joins the pending-deploy delta since both PA and PB compile this module.]** NO new CI step: the `coverage` job already runs `coverage_report.sh` and picks the three targets up automatically, and no new test file was added.]**** **THE HONEST READ — scoped to the ORIGINAL THREE A8 modules (query_shield/route/control_verify; the 2026-08-11 judgement pass found the fuzz-harness trio's corpora need exactly the aiming these did not): those corpora are in good shape and mostly do NOT need aiming** — of 263 branch outcomes only 20 are never-taken and half of those are structurally impossible. It is genuinely uncovered and IS live (`main_x86.c:3713`/`:3728`; its string is load-bearing in `g3_retrieval.h`'s recall reasoning), so the honest disposition is a **UNIT-TEST gap in `test_route.c` — not a fuzz gap, and not dead code** *(closed 2026-08-11: T6 added — the closures sweep)*. DEPLOYED 2026-08-09 (image 5b16dda6, boot_id=51) and NOT exercised - a healthy boot never restarts, so the only hardware evidence is inertness (restart_count=0, 0 [RESTART] and 0 [FATAL] across 1606 durable lines); the behaviour stays proven by 41 checks into CI Phase 6: A9/2 PB crash-loop window (C). **This is the logic that decides whether the box SELF-HEALS or PERMANENTLY GIVES UP**, and it was chosen for extraction because its defect class is PROVEN rather than hypothetical: at K/M2b-2 STEP-2 `KM2B_HEALTHY_RESET` was defined but never incremented, silently converting a windowed bound into a LIFETIME cap that shipped and had to be found on hardware. **A PLAN CLAIM CORRECTED BY READING THE SOURCE: the `window > 0` guard is NOT "behaviourally equivalent".** It sits in a short-circuit `&&` chain, so `++healthy` never executes with no window open; **The bound evaluation moved to the executed path, proven neutral rather than assumed:** the deployed check was UNCONDITIONAL and also ran on the SHIELD-REFUSED branch where the window was never bumped — dead code for any non-zero bound, since `dead` is set only at `>= bound` and the funnel early-returns on `dead`, so on entry `window < bound` always (the `bound==0` exception is documented in the header). **KVM CANNOT EXERCISE EITHER BRANCH THIS MODULE EXISTS FOR** — every restart-driving probe zeroes the window between cycles, so the maximum reachable window is **1** against a bound of **5**, and a healthy run never restarts at all; **DEPLOYED 2026-08-09 (image `5b16dda6…`, `boot_id=51`) — and NOT EXERCISED, which was expected and is stated rather than glossed:** a healthy boot never restarts, so both branches this module owns stayed untaken. the behaviour remains proven by the 41 host checks and the induced-restart KVM A/B, never by hardware. - **Control-IN Security Core (Phase 6 6-5/M1, host-pure — NOW LIVE IN THE DEPLOYED IMAGE since the 6-5 flip 2026-07-21; the "DEPLOY-INERT" status below is M1-era history):** `phase3/src/crypto/` (`sha256.c/h` = copied public-domain Brad-Conte SHA-256, 3 NIST KATs; `hmac_sha256.c/h` = RFC 2104 + a **constant-time** `hmac_sha256_verify` — `volatile` OR-accumulator over all 32 tag bytes, NO early-out, deliberately NOT the `shmem_ipc.c` `!=` compare; `{sha256,hmac}_vectors.h` = committed differential-fuzz vectors) + `phase3/src/net/` (`control_msg.h` = the authoritative 36-B-header/≤240-B wire format, big-endian, **canonical HMAC span = payload[0..len−32)**; `control_parser.c/h` = hardened copy-free Eth/IPv4/UDP+JCTL parse — **fragment-reject (MF|offset) before any UDP/HMAC work**, exact length math, `IP_MIN_HDR`=20 guard; `control_replay.c/h` = PA-side seq-floor + epoch + 128-nonce ring, ordered epoch→seq→nonce truth table, persistence contract; `control_ratelimit.c/h` = uint32-wrap-safe milli-token bucket (millisecond-driven by the CALLER — feeding it a per-frame counter starves the channel at exactly CAP; see the M4e finding); `control_verify.c/h` = the orchestrator, order parse→ratelimit→HMAC→replay so **`control_replay_check` MUTATES state ONLY after a valid HMAC** — an unauth frame can never walk the floor / fill the ring; `fuzz_control_in.c` = 300K-iter ASan/UBSan) + `phase3/scripts/gen_control_vectors.py` — **which gained a `--check` DRIFT GATE at A6b (2026-08-03)** → CI "Phase 6: control-vector generator drift gate (Python)". LIVE in the deployed image since the 6-5 flip 2026-07-21 - every inbound frame is parsed, rate-limited, HMAC-verified and replay-checked on real hardware; CI counts control_parser 43, control_replay 23, control_ratelimit 13, control_verify 22 and fuzz_control_in 300,020 iters ASan/UBSan, plus the A6b drift gate CI Phase 6: control-vector generator drift gate (Python). Generation is deterministic (fixed `SEED = 0x6C6F6E67`, no clock, no `urandom`), so a mismatch is real drift and never flake; Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — Control-IN Security Core (Phase 6 6-5/M1, host-pure — NOW LIVE IN THE DEPLOYED IMAGE since the 6-5 flip 2026-07-21; the 'DEPLOY-INERT' status below is M1-era history)".

- **Control-IN Query SHIELD (Phase 6 6-5/M3-1, host-pure — NOW LIVE on the deployed box since the 6-5 flip 2026-07-21, scoring every inbound query before PA routes it; "HOST + CI ONLY / DEPLOY-INERT" below is M3-1-era history):** the query-threat gate that closes **SEC-039-for-queries at the LOGIC level** — a control-IN frame that survived M1/M2b auth+replay yields a ≤172 B validated QUERY, and `query_shield.c/h` scores it QS_ALLOW vs QS_REFUSE for four DEFINED abuse classes (KEY_EXTRACTION / EXFIL / JAILBREAK / CONFIG_DISCLOSE) BEFORE PA would route it (the box route + induced-BLOCK proof + response are M3-2). LIVE on the deployed box since the 6-5 flip 2026-07-21, scoring every validated inbound query before PA routes it and auditing a refusal by reason-class LABEL only; 87 asserts with measured FP = 0/100, plus fuzz_query_shield 300,010 iters ASan/UBSan (300,000 random + 10 directed, 2026-08-11). injection is contained STRUCTURALLY (K-b: inbound text can never mint an action; tagged-untrusted → no cache-growth/retrieval store contamination; answer-to-console-address-only → no exfil). Engine: `qs_normalize` (length-carried, lowercase alnum words, every non-alnum byte → a single-space separator; NEVER strlen'd) → a static human-reviewed table of EMIT-ANCHORED ORDERED phrase patterns — an emission verb (a 23-verb set) + the possessive `your`/a sensitive target within a TIGHT per-pattern gap (up to 4 slots; the canned jailbreak requires adjacency gap-1; first-match-wins, severity-ordered so a combined attack resolves to the most severe class). Audit discipline (`query_shield_reason_label`): a REFUSE records the reason-class LABEL ONLY, never the raw query — so a hostile can't smuggle a blocklist keyword (e.g. "format") into the M3-2 JACT audit; labels are keyword-clean by construction, teeth-proven BOTH ways (label → `shield_assess` EXECUTE + a bounded substring proving the raw query is never inside the label). Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — Control-IN Query SHIELD (Phase 6 6-5/M3-1, host-pure — NOW LIVE on the deployed box since the 6-5 flip 2026-07-21, scoring every inbound query before PA routes it; 'HOST + CI ONLY / DEPLOY-INERT' below is M3-1-era history)".

- **Query Router (Phase 6 6-6/B/M0, host):** `phase3/src/ai/route.c/h` — a pure, host-testable keyword intent classifier `route_classify(q,len)` → ROUTE_{SYSFACTS, INFER, DECLINE} (the `query_shield.c` precedent: host-pure, length-carried, NEVER strlen, crash/UB-free for any attacker bytes) + `sysfacts_answer` over a host-WHITELISTED `sysfacts_t` (uptime / boot_id / model / nodes / a coarse health bit ONLY — a `_Static_assert` sizeof+offsetof pins the whitelist; the load-generator counters and all internal/security state are DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED, goal doc §4) + a canned honest DECLINE for no-source status metrics (cpu / disk / wall-clock / temp / memory-used → "I don't track that", NEVER routed to inference to be fabricated). DEPLOYED - JARVIS_ROUTING default-ON since 2026-07-23, linked into Process A and called from pa_ctrl_gate on every QS_ALLOW control-IN query; keyword-blind HELDOUT 70/73 = 95.89%, DEV 64/64, 0 INFER misroutes; fuzz_route.c 300,012 ASan/UBSan into CI Phase 6: 6-6/B0 Query router (C) and its Fuzz (C, ASAN) step. **RETUNED 2026-07-23 (`5224a85`) after the supervised flip validation caught a real defect on hardware:** the DECLINE rule matched untracked-metric nouns (cpu/disk/ram/load/temperature/time…) **BARE**, so a CONCEPTUAL question merely containing one was declined — the operator's improvised "why doesn't adding more CPU cores speed up a single-threaded program?" got "I don't track that." That **violated route.h's own conservative rule** (ambiguity → INFER, never toward a wrong answer) and was a **capability REGRESSION** vs the routing-off box. **Why the suite missed it: every DECLINE case in BOTH splits was a genuine status query, so the DECLINE-vs-INFER boundary was never exercised and 95.52% passed WITH the defect present** — the concrete reason a held-out score is a POINT ESTIMATE, not production accuracy. - `phase3/src/drivers/net_udp.c/h` — `net_build_udp_unicast` (the gated unicast reply builder; fail-closed on a NULL dst MAC, NEVER falls back to broadcast). - **Episodic Store (Phase 5 G1/M0):** **RECALL PROVENANCE added 2026-08-01 — the record now says WHICH record supplied its preamble, and how close the misses came.** Boot 48 recorded `sem_recall_hits=4` and durable `recall=4` and **which four was unrecoverable**: `[CTRL-RECALL]`/`[CTRL-SEM]` are `puts_serial` and vanish at the deployed `JARVIS_DBG_BOOT_LOG=0`, and the answer text cannot settle it either (every answer named the right concept, which the model would do with or without a hit). G1 COMPLETE (M0 to M4) and deployed; the record gained recall provenance 2026-08-01 and was widened 2026-09-03 with the full selected set plus recall_emit_mask @502 (pad 10 to 9, still exactly 512 B) - that widening is NOT DEPLOYED, the box stays on boot 48's image 0b771bba; test_episodic_store.c 14 PASS, parser round-trip 239 PASS. 1+4+2 = 7 B so `pad` 24 -> **17** and **the record stays EXACTLY 512 B with the one-sector `_Static_assert` UNCHANGED** — that assert protects the on-disk layout and must not move. There is deliberately **NO version byte**: `boot_id` identifies the era, and the parser renders an all-zero block as **`unknown`**, never asserting `none`. **[WIDENED 2026-09-03 — the record now names the FULL selected set.** The `[23:00110]` reconstruction proved a shape these three fields cannot express: a TWO-fact preamble (seq 93 @ cos 0.944, then seq 108 @ 0.765) whose SECOND fact carried the answer, while `recall_src_seq` named only the first. `G3_MAX_FACTS` is 2, so the full set is at most two records and seven more of the seventeen pad bytes close it: **`recall_sel_count` @495 · `recall_src2_seq` @496 · `recall_cos2_x1000` @500**, pad 17 -> **10**, and the record is **STILL exactly 512 B** — the one-sector `_Static_assert` is untouched, and THREE more offset asserts join the existing three, because sizeof alone would also be satisfied by swapping two field widths (mutation-proven: that swap keeps sizeof at 512 and fails ONLY the offset asserts, by name). Set at the control-IN write site via PARAMETERS, never statics — the 2026-08-01 rationale verbatim. The second cosine reuses the SAME truncation and clamp as the first, so it equals what `soak_prov_driver.c` prints for rank 2. **COMPATIBILITY, and it is the same trap as before: a record written 2026-08-01..2026-09-03 carries `kind != 0` with count 0. That is UNKNOWN, never ONE** — `[23:00110]` is itself such a record and its true count was two; the parser renders `n=?` and says so once, and a NON-ZERO count is what proves a record post-widening. **TWO READING TRAPS are now documented in the header rather than left for the next reader, both found by an adversarial review of this very change: (1) the src fields are the SELECTOR'S ORDER, not proof of contribution — the builder SKIPS a fact whose cleaned length is 0, so with two selected and the first cleaning away, `recall_src_seq` names a record that supplied no bytes (the `[23:00110]` shape one level down, and reachable); (2) `recall_cos_x1000` is the best over EVERY gathered candidate — a population that skips neither the thought-marker nor the unit-vector filter the selector applies — whereas `recall_cos2_x1000` IS its own record's score, so the two are not comparable.** The mode-4 probe prints the widened line and then READS THE RECORD BACK off the store (`[PROV] readback seq=…`), which is the writer -> NVMe callbacks -> store -> reader proof a host test cannot give: KVM-measured, all six fields field-for-field identical on all four turns, and the re-ask stored `n=2 src2=<the statement turn's seq> cos2=716`.]**  **[EMIT MASK 2026-09-03 — the record now says which selected facts were actually EMITTED.** The widening above recorded the SELECTED set; its own review found the remaining hole: the builder SKIPS a selected fact whose cleaned text holds no complete sentence, so a record could still name a source that contributed NOTHING — the `[23:00110]` shape one level down, and reachable (no-complete-sentence was 8 of 14 hits when measured at C/M2b). One more byte closes it: **`recall_emit_mask` @502** (pad 10 → **9**, record STILL 512 B, a seventh offset pin beside the others). Bit i (selector order, 0-based) is set iff selected fact i put ≥ 1 byte into the preamble — **measured from the builder's own write position, NOT from the cleaned length**, because `g3_append` stops at the cap and a perfectly good fact can still emit nothing (T-EMIT case 6 pins exactly that, deriving the cap length from the builder so the case cannot rot). **The builder gained `g3_build_preamble_answer_only_ex`; the old name is a one-line WRAPPER passing NULL — ONE decision point, so the bytes and the mask cannot disagree, and the deployed WORKLOAD lane is untouched (proven, not argued: across the refactor its 17 `[RETR]` lines and 17 `[INFER]` prompts are md5-IDENTICAL on the same KVM fixture).** **UNKNOWN, NEVER ZERO:** a record written before this field carries `kind != 0` with mask 0 and renders `emit=?`; a set kind implies a non-empty preamble, which implies a set bit — so "recalled but emitted nothing" is **not reachable at the deployed cap**, and that clause is deliberate: the property is ARITHMETIC (limit 511 > an 80-byte header), not structural, so `g3_retrieval.c` now `_Static_assert`s the header-vs-limit relation AND the 8-bit mask width — the latter because the `G3_MAX_FACTS <= 2` pin lives in a host-only TU the seL4 build never compiles. Mode 4 prints `emit=` on both the stored and read-back lines; KVM-measured, all SEVEN fields field-for-field identical on all four turns, the re-ask storing `emit=3` against an independently-recorded 185-byte two-fact preamble.]** **NOT DEPLOYED — the box stays on boot 48's image `0b771bba…`.** A control-IN turn that PA answered ITSELF (a 6-6 SYSFACTS reply from live box state, or the canned DECLINE) is tagged 4 instead of 3: still written, still auditable, corpus preserved — simply **never recallable**. **ONE CORRECTION TO THE "excluded for free" CLAIM, found by reading rather than assuming: that was true of the FETCH filter only.** **M1a (2026-06-27):** episodic init decoupled from nvme_log (was nested in its success block → never ran when nvme_log_init fails, incl. the 12 G QEMU image); Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — Episodic Store (Phase 5 G1/M0)".

That is exactly what makes it discriminating where `TLM_F_CONTROL_IN` is NOT: any checksum-valid slot sets that flag and the `[CTRL-CONSOLE] addr=` line prints mac:port only, never the parsed IP, so the flag would have looked identical against the OLD slot. The sender side moved too — the receiver picks its NIC from the route to 192.168.100.143, which now falls to the default route: Ethernet 3, the only IPv4 default route on the PC.
