- **Control-IN Replay Floor (Phase 6 6-5/M3-3, host-pure):** `phase3/src/net/control_floor.c/h` (NEW) — the cross-reboot persisted replay-floor logic (host-pure, the `control_replay.c` precedent — the CALLER does the NVMe I/O). Host-pure and wired into PA: the double-buffered A/B JFLR slot pair @ LBA 21,130,001/2 holds the cross-reboot persisted replay floor, written by ctrl_floor_persist_ahead (WRITE-AHEAD, fail-closed on write failure) with a fail-safe on FLOOR_CORRUPT; closes checklist item-2. test_control_floor.c 48/48 -> CI Phase 6: 6-5/M3-3 Control floor (C). A 512 B **double-buffered A/B floor-sector pair** (`ctrl_floor_slot_t`: magic `JFLR`/`0x4A464C52`, version, `reserved_floor` u64, `write_count` u32, rotate-xor checksum; @ **LBA 21,130,001/2** — the control-IN raw-LBA sub-region right after the JKEY sector @ 21,130,000; **the FULL storage map: episodic 21,100,000 / semantic 21,110,000 / JACT 21,120,000 / control-IN sub-region 21,130,000-3 = JKEY 21,130,000 (WRITE-ONCE) + JFLR A 21,130,001 + JFLR B 21,130,002 + JCON 21,130,003. **⚠️ THE 1.66 TiB OF "FREE SPACE" ON THE BOX IS NOT FREE — NEVER PARTITION IT.** `parted /dev/nvme0n1 unit s print free` reports a gap from **21,094,400s to 3,581,364,223s** (between `JARVIS_DATA`, which ends at 21,094,399s, and Ubuntu's ext4, which starts at 3,581,364,224s). **GPT calls that unpartitioned; it is not unused** — every raw-LBA store above sits ~5,600 sectors into it, and a `mkpart` aligned to the START of free space (**the default**) would destroy the episodic store, **the JACT audit trail that goal 6-7's done-when depends on**, the control-IN HMAC key, the replay floor, the console address, and the cross-session recall corpus validated at boot 47. **Any future partition in that gap MUST start above 21,200,000s.** The SECOND gap, after the ESP (from 4,000,794,624s), holds the **durable telemetry log** (2,701 of its 2,703 sectors) — likewise not free. `parted`'s "Free Space" is a statement about the PARTITION TABLE, not about the disk being empty; nothing else in this repo said so, and it was found while looking at disk pressure rather than after an incident.**). Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — Control-IN Replay Floor (Phase 6 6-5/M3-3, host-pure)".
- **Telemetry Packet:** `phase3/src/drivers/jarvis_telemetry.c/h` — 276-byte (v14) packed versioned `telemetry_packet_t` + zlib/IEEE CRC-32 (`jarvis_tlm_crc32`/`jarvis_tlm_finalize`); v14 - 276 B, CRC@272 - DEPLOYED and FIRST-ON-WIRE VALIDATED 2026-08-02 at boot_id=49: 1315 captured packets, EVERY one decoding version=14 / 276 B / crc_ok, veto_inited=1 on all of them, q_errors=0 and restart_count=0 the ONLY values in the capture. The receiver stays VERSION-TOLERANT by LENGTH across all five tails, v14 down to v10. Host test_jarvis_telemetry.c 113/113. latched between inferences — never fabricated, never the 5.46 benchmark constant. ESP-deployed (md5 `bd610c79…`) + live I211 — 521/521 records `version=4` crc_ok, **`infer_last_tok_x100` 551–555 = 5.51–5.55 tok/s measured live** (within ~1.6% of the 5.46 benchmark it replaces), `infer_gen_tokens=50` real, the latch+idle lifecycle proven on-wire (`infer_active` 1→0 with the value held, never fabricated; cache-growth co-live, err=0 at q=40,100). **v5 (P5 #5/M2, 2026-07-04)** appends `uint16 shield_learn_keys` + `uint16 shield_learn_max_risk_x100` → **222 B, CRC@218, version 5**, +`TLM_F_SHIELD_LEARN` 0x200 — the SHIELD failure-learning MONITOR signal (keys with learned risk + max learned adjustment ×100, filled from the same `g_shield_learn` walk as the `[SHIELD-LEARN]` summary, gated `JARVIS_SHIELD_LEARN` → the flag-OFF deploy emits 0s + flag clear, honest; a learned-risk count, NEVER a block count — SEC-039 unchanged, enforcement is Phase 6). **v6 (P5 #4/M2, 2026-07-04)** appends `uint16 semantic_fact_count` → **224 B, CRC@220, version 6**, +`TLM_F_SEMANTIC` 0x400 — the distilled-fact count (observable repeated Q&A patterns compacted by the deterministic distill, never "knows preferences"; gated `JARVIS_SEMANTIC` fill → the flag-OFF deploy emits 0 + flag clear). **v8 (P6 6-1/M3, 2026-07-09)** appends `uint16 monitors_fired` + `uint8 last_monitor_event` + `uint8 mon_pad` → **236 B, CRC@232, version 8**, +`TLM_F_MONITORS` 0x1000 — the always-on-monitor NOTIFY activity (a NEUTRAL debounced event count: a MIX of degradation signals (error-rate, self-heal-rate) and benign liveness events (uptime milestones, store wraps) — NEVER "anomalies/problems detected"; sourced from `g_monitors_fired`/`g_last_monitor_event`, bumped ONLY in `mon_notify`'s EXECUTED branch; flag set on `g_mon_inited`; gated `JARVIS_MONITORS` fill → the flag-OFF deploy emits 0s + flag clear, honest — the SAME pattern as v5/v6/v7). **v9 (P6 6-2/M3, 2026-07-12)** appends `uint16 wakes_fired` + `uint8 last_wake_event` + `uint8 wake_pad` → **240 B, CRC@236, version 9**, +`TLM_F_WAKE` 0x2000 — the event-driven-wake CONSULT activity (DISPATCHED consults only — never suppressed/refused; a consult = a fixed, human-reviewed question per monitor event, cache-served or one bounded inference — NEVER "thinking"/"reasoning"; sourced from `g_wakes_fired`/`g_last_wake_event`, bumped ONLY at the dispatch site's executed branch; flag set on `g_wake_inited`; gated `JARVIS_WAKE` fill → the flag-OFF deploy emits 0s + flag clear, the SAME honest pattern). **v10 (P6 6-3/M3, 2026-07-13)** appends `uint16 behaviors_fired` + `uint16 behaviors_mask` + `uint8 last_behavior` + `uint8 beh_pad` → **246 B, CRC@242, version 10**, +`TLM_F_PROACTIVE` 0x4000 — the proactive-behavior INFORM activity (`behaviors_fired` = USER INTERRUPTS via the always-fires mark, a cap-suppressed inform never counts; `behaviors_mask` bit id−1 latches per fired behavior — the console's live per-row source; `last_behavior` = the most recent id; a B1/B2 consult bumps BOTH `wakes_fired` AND `behaviors_fired` BY DESIGN — two honest views of ONE event, documented, never summed; flag set on `g_proactive_inited`; gated `JARVIS_PROACTIVE` fill → the PROACTIVE=0 deploy emits 0s + flag clear; the UDP frame buffer grew 288→304 for headroom — the v10 frame is exactly 288). **v11 (P6 6-5/M3-4a, 2026-07-19)** appends `uint16 control_in_answered` + `uint16 control_in_blocked` + `uint32 control_in_dropped` → **254 B, CRC@250, version 11**, +`TLM_F_CONTROL_IN` 0x8000 (**the LAST u16 flag bit — flags is now exhausted; a future flag needs a flags-width bump**) — the control-IN two-way channel (answered = routed+answered queries; **`control_in_blocked` = a DEFINED-ABUSE-CLASS refuse count from the query SHIELD, NEVER "injection blocked"** — general injection is contained STRUCTURALLY, not detected; dropped = frames rejected pre-SHIELD by auth/replay/parse/ratelimit; sourced from `g_ctrl_in_answered`/`g_ctrl_in_blocked`/`g_ctrl_dropped`; the flag means the CHANNEL is UP (`g_ctrl_key_ok && g_ctrl_floor_ok && g_ctrl_rx_ready`), NOT that a query arrived; gated `JARVIS_CONTROL_IN` fill → the CONTROL_IN=0 deploy emits v11 with all three 0 + flag CLEAR, honest "channel gated off"). **HONESTY CRUX: these are ROUTING DECISIONS counted at classification time — they are NOT a breakdown of `control_in_answered` and must never be rendered as one** (an INFER decision whose dispatch later DEGRADES or TIMES OUT counts in `route_infer` but never reaches the answered exit; box-proven at B/M1 — sysfacts=2 decline=1 infer=2 = 5 decisions with answered=4; the golden fixture deliberately encodes 6 decisions vs 3 answered so the fixture itself is teeth). Different mechanism, different store, different ceiling — never merged, summed or cross-labelled (the C test populates both with DIFFERENT values so a confusion fails rather than coincides). **A miss is NOT an error — it degrades to EXACTLY today's no-preamble path.** **A LATENT DEFECT THE BUMP WOULD HAVE TRIPPED, found and fixed here:** `main_x86.c`'s emit buffer was `static uint8_t tlm_frame[304]` with a comment reading *"14+20+8+254 = 296 <= 304 (v11; headroom, never exact-fit)"* — stale since v12, because 42+262 is **EXACTLY 304**, i.e. `vetoed` = SYSFACTS captures rerouted to INFER — **one direction only, never the reverse, and neither count derivable from the other** (the golden infer frame carries the deliberately non-derivable pair `checked=5 / vetoed=2` so a consumer computing one from the other FAILS the pins). The receiver is VERSION-TOLERANT by LENGTH across **all five** tails — v14 (276 B, CRC@272) / v13 (270 B, CRC@266) / v12 (262 B, CRC@258) / v11 (254 B, CRC@250) / v10 (246 B, CRC@242) — an older packet decoding cleanly with its newer fields `None`, never a fabricated 0 (**the live deployed box emits v13** — the boot-48 C/M3b image — and will until the next deploy, so this tolerance is load-bearing, not hypothetical; `veto_inited` inherits the sharpened None-vs-0 distinction: **0** means "the box reports the veto and it is gated off" while **None** means "this box does not report the veto at all", two different true statements that must never collapse — exactly as `sem_inited` before it). M3-4b (the browser input box + the receiver-side signing helper) landed 2026-07-19 Main-PC-side only — it changed NO box code and does NOT change this wire format. Note the wire changed for EVERYONE at this deploy (the struct is v14 regardless of `JARVIS_ROUTE_VETO`; only the fill is gated), which is why the flip validated v14 FIRST and the veto behaviour SECOND — a v14 failure could then never be confused with a veto failure. Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — Telemetry Packet".
- **Telemetry Receiver / SSE bridge:** `phase3/scripts/telemetry_receiver.py` — Main-PC Python UDP receiver for the N-c-1 telemetry_packet_t (decode + zlib CRC-32 validate + seq-gap drop detection; `--once/--follow/--json`; honest fields only). The Main-PC receiver and SSE bridge: decode + CRC + seq-gap, the /events stream, --replay, and the optional --send signer, loopback-PINNED behind a TRIPLE localhost guard, plus the :51002 JRPL v2 listener that DROPS whatever is not both crc_ok AND hmac_ok. Since 2026-09-01 it appends a durable reply-path JSONL to ~/.jarvis/receiver/reply_log.jsonl (default-ON with --send). 350/350. Now: nothing logged = the datagram never arrived (wire loss, UDP has no retransmit); append-only and NEVER self-rotating (the `jarvis_menu` transcript rule); never any key material; **the :51000 telemetry stream is NEVER logged** (it runs ~2 Hz forever — this is the reply path only); FAIL-OPEN, a write failure warns ONCE and never breaks receiving. (b) a UDP **:51002** listener that decodes **and AUTHENTICATES** the box's JRPL v2 reply (**6-5/M4b**: `hmac.compare_digest` over `payload[:14+tlen]`; accepted only when BOTH crc_ok AND hmac_ok, and **DROPPED otherwise — a tampered tag/text/CRC, a wrong key, a v1 reply, or NO key at all never reaches the console**, with a bounded `[reply] DROP unverified (<reason>)` log + counter; the M3-4b box-IP filter is retained but demoted to a cheap pre-filter, since a spoofed source cannot forge a tag) and fans it out over the **SAME** `/events` SSE stream as a `control_reply` record (`kind` is the **STRING** `"control_reply"` — telemetry records carry an INTEGER `kind`; the discriminator). **#9 (2026-07-28): `VERDICT_NAMES` gains `4: 'answered (truncated)'`, and nothing here validates the verdict as a RANGE — an unrecognised value falls through to `'?%d'` and STILL DELIVERS ITS TEXT, deliberately: a box that learns a new verdict before this receiver does must not have its answers silently vanish, which is the M2 `tlen`-ceiling failure shape applied to verdicts. `test_telemetry_receiver.py` pins both (the 5-entry table, and that verdicts 4 and 9 both round-trip their text)**. **TRIPLE localhost guard:** #2 the HTTP/signing surface is loopback-PINNED at bind resolution (the `''` default is FORCED to `127.0.0.1`; an explicit non-loopback `--http-bind`/`--bind` is REFUSED with exit 2, never silently downgraded — only the telemetry UDP socket stays on the LAN), #1 every request's `client_address` is re-checked BEFORE any parse/sign/send (a non-loopback POST never consumes a sequence number), and #3 **the request's PROVENANCE** is checked — because #1/#2 only prove the TCP connection is local, **not that the operator asked for it**. **FIXED before commit:** `/send` now requires `Content-Type: application/json` (NOT safelisted ⇒ a cross-origin POST must preflight, and there is no `do_OPTIONS`, so it never lands), refuses a foreign `Origin` or a `Sec-Fetch-Site` that is not same-origin/none (both browser-set, unforgeable by page JS), and requires a loopback `Host` (the DNS-rebinding bypass); Errors are honest JSON with an `error` key and never a key or a traceback: 400 bad JSON/empty/non-printable/**>172 BYTES** (utf-8, never `len()`), 403 non-loopback, 500 no/invalid key, 503 `--send` absent or scapy/Npcap/route unavailable. `TelemetryHub.publish_event` fans out **only** and deliberately never touches `self.latest` (a one-shot reply must not be replayed to every later page-load as stale telemetry state). **WITHOUT `--send` the receiver is byte-for-byte the display-only bridge it was** (no scapy import — it is a lazy import inside `send()`, never a hard dependency). Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — Telemetry Receiver / SSE bridge".
- **Control-IN Key Generator (Phase C, Main-PC):** `phasec/scripts/gen_control_key.py` — promotes the throwaway `~/scratch/make_ctrl_key_slot.py` into a committed, tested tool that emits the two provisioning artifacts: the **512-byte JKEY slot image** (the operator `dd`s it) and the **raw 32-byte key file** for the Main PC (`%USERPROFILE%\.jarvis\control_key.bin`, the launcher default). A committed, tested provisioning tool: it emits the 512-byte JKEY slot image and the raw 32-byte key file from secrets.token_bytes, the key is NEVER printed, it is FAIL-CLOSED without --force, it NEVER writes to the box, and the layout is parsed from control_key.h. CI Phase C: Control-IN key generator (Python) -> test_gen_control_key.py 25 PASS. the key **never touches the wire** (only HMAC tags do), so rotation closes no realistic leak path — every path that leaks today's key leaks tomorrow's; Properties: 32 bytes from `secrets.token_bytes` (**never `random`**); **the key is NEVER printed** (only paths, sizes, and the `dd` command); and it **NEVER writes to the box**, preserving the property that **the box has NO code path that writes the key sector** — it only READS it once at boot. Box-side validation is **inline in `main_x86.c:5048-5054`** (magic == `CTRL_KEY_MAGIC` **and** a non-zero 32-byte key; note it does NOT check `version`) — a generated slot satisfies both by construction. **IGNORE RULE (matters more than the tool):** `.gitignore:112-113`+ covers `control_key.bin` / `jkey_slot.bin` / `*.jkey` / `.jarvis/` / `ctrl_key*.bin` / `*_key_slot.bin` — **proven** by generating real artifacts with the default names in the repo root and confirming `git status` never saw them; Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — Control-IN Key Generator (Phase C, Main-PC)".
- **Control-IN Launcher (Phase C, Main-PC, Windows) — TWO files, and the distinction matters:** **`phasec/scripts/start_receiver.bat` is the ENTRY POINT** (what the operator double-clicks); In use on the Main PC: the .bat forwards to the .ps1 with -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass and gives a pause on any failure exit, UAC self-elevates for the SEND path only (-DisplayOnly never elevates), the preflight is FAIL-CLOSED, and the console URL opens via explorer.exe so the browser never inherits the Administrator token. Deliberately NO ci.yml step - -Check IS the CI substitute. **No double-wrap:** the `.ps1`'s own UAC re-launch (`start_receiver.ps1:198`) builds its elevated relaunch with its OWN `-NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass` and re-invokes `$PSCommandPath` — the `.ps1`, never the `.bat` — so the elevated second launch has no policy gap and no nested wrapper. UAC self-elevates for the SEND path only (raw L2 via scapy/Npcap needs it; `-DisplayOnly` = `--sse` telemetry-only, never elevates); params `-KeyFile` (default `%USERPROFILE%\.jarvis\control_key.bin` — the operator provisions the key out-of-band; the script NEVER embeds key material and weakens NO receiver guard) / `-BoxMac` / `-BoxIp` (omitted = the receiver's provisioned defaults, never duplicated here) / `-Port` (`--http-port`, default 8800) / `-Check` (validate + print the exact command + exit — the fail-loud self-test; a Windows .ps1 cannot run in the Linux CI, so `-Check` IS the CI substitute and there is deliberately NO ci.yml step). On success it runs `py -3 phase3/scripts/telemetry_receiver.py --sse --http-port <Port> [--send --key-file <KeyFile>]` in-console (Ctrl+C stops; the banner warns about the QuickEdit freeze gotcha) and a detached helper polls the port then opens the console URL **via `explorer.exe` so the browser never inherits the Administrator token** (a plain `Start-Process <url>` from the elevated context starts an ELEVATED browser when none is already running — an adversarial-review MED finding, fixed pre-commit). Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — Control-IN Launcher (Phase C, Main-PC, Windows) — TWO files, and the distinction matters".
- **Operator Menu (Phase C, Main-PC, Windows) — `phasec/scripts/jarvis_menu.bat` is the ENTRY POINT / `phasec/scripts/jarvis_menu.ps1` is the implementation** (the `start_receiver` precedent — a `.ps1` cannot be double-clicked). v1 shipped and box-verified 2026-07-25: the 7 READ-ONLY options - receiver send / display-only / -Check, which all three CALL start_receiver.ps1, plus box state and four NAMED-store reads - with constants DERIVED from the headers at run time, ssh-dependent options shown DISABLED with the reason, and an append-only transcript. CI: N/A BY DESIGN - -Check IS the substitute (measured PASS/exit 0). **v1 = the 7 READ-ONLY options** of `phasec/docs/OPERATOR_MENU_DESIGN.md` §6 (design `f14e527`, built + box-verified 2026-07-25, §10 RESULT): receiver send / display-only / `-Check` — all three **CALL `start_receiver.ps1`**, never reimplement its UAC-for-send-only, its `explorer.exe` non-elevated browser hand-off or its fail-closed preflight (R1) — plus box state and four NAMED-store reads (telemetry log / episodic / control-IN / JACT). **Constants are DERIVED from the headers at run time** (`nvme_log.h` / `episodic_store.h` / `action_audit.h` — the `clear_nvme_log.sh:34-41` precedent), so a menu constant cannot drift from the firmware and **the operator never types an LBA or a count**. **The `dd | python3` pipe runs ON THE BOX:** raw sector data never crosses a PowerShell pipeline (pipelines carry TEXT and would corrupt it — the same class as the `os.O_BINARY` key corruption), so only text crosses ssh, and it means **no intermediate file is written**, which is what keeps the invariant true; **State-aware (R3):** `ssh -o BatchMode=yes` → an already-running receiver's `/events` **requiring a FRESH `recv_ts`** → dumpcap → **UNKNOWN, never "off"** (under JARVIS the box answers no ICMP and no ARP, so unreachable is ambiguous by construction). The freshness gate is load-bearing: the receiver **replays its cached record to every NEW subscriber** (`telemetry_receiver.py:1039-1043`), so "a record arrived" proves nothing and a receiver left running since the box was last up would report a dead box as live. ssh-dependent options are shown **DISABLED with the reason, never hidden**; **Transcript** `%USERPROFILE%\.jarvis\menu\transcript.log` — **append-only and NEVER self-rotating** (a tool that trims its own audit log is one whose audit log cannot be trusted), recording `timestamp | option-key | RESOLVED COMMAND | exit code` — the resolved command, never the menu label — and never output, never key material. and **`-Lines` silently never working** — PowerShell variable names are **CASE-INSENSITIVE**, so a local `$lines = @($out)` overwrote the `$Lines` parameter and `$lines.Count -le $Lines` compared the array against itself, always true, so the full dump always printed. **`-Check` could never have caught that one — it never reads a store**; Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — Operator Menu (Phase C, Main-PC, Windows) — `phasec/scripts/jarvis_menu.bat` is the ENTRY POINT / `phasec/scripts/jarvis_menu.ps1` is the implementation".
- **Operator Admin (Phase C, Main-PC, Windows) — `phasec/scripts/jarvis_admin.bat` is the ENTRY POINT / `phasec/scripts/jarvis_admin.ps1` is the implementation.** The DESTRUCTIVE half, in use: three modes with NO DEFAULT (-Check, -Rekey behind a typed REKEY-WRITE-NOW, -Rollback), TEN GATES, ALL HARD ABORTS, key material never printed (FINGERPRINT only), and a bare invocation now opens a STATE-AWARE CHOOSER. Used for a real re-key 2026-07-26 (a38577f1 -> 37b420f0, box boot_id=45) which also proved REVOCATION. CI: N/A by design. **`-Check` is the ANTI-ROT mechanism, not merely the CI substitute** (a Windows `.ps1` cannot run in this repo's Linux CI — the `jarvis_menu.ps1` precedent, so there is deliberately NO ci.yml step): re-keying is **INCIDENT RESPONSE, not rotation**, so this script sits unused for long stretches and must be verifiable on demand — and it verifies by **really reading the box** (it runs P1–P5 and the neighbour reads for real), not by printing, which is exactly how the menu's `-Lines` bug shipped (`-Check` never read a store). **TEN GATES, ALL HARD ABORTS**, each earned by the manual run — the ones the RUNBOOK does not have: **P4** the two halves must ALREADY MATCH before starting (if they differ the channel is already broken and re-keying is the WRONG action — it would overwrite the only copy of the box's current key) · **B1/B2** backups verified by **FINGERPRINT, not size** (a 512-byte file of zeros passes a size check; fingerprinting is what makes the write safe to take) · **G4** the key INSIDE the slot must equal the key file (without it a mismatched pair provisions "successfully" and the channel is dead with **no error anywhere** until a query gets no reply) · **T2** whole-slot md5 equal on both sides, not "512 bytes arrived" (this project has silently corrupted binary through a text channel three times) · **W1** read back from `/dev/nvme0n1`, NOT from the file just written · **W3** neighbours must survive (`LBA+1/+2` JFLR, `LBA+3` JCON) — one wrong `seek=` digit lands on the replay floor, the console address, the JACT store (21120000) or the control-IN store (21140000), damage that would **not show up in the target sector at all**. **EVERY LBA/magic/version is PARSED from the firmware headers** (`control_key.h` + `control_floor.h` + `control_console.h`), never hardcoded — **including the neighbour OFFSETS, which are expressions** (`CTRL_KEY_BASE_LBA + 1ULL`) and are parsed out of the expression rather than assumed. **KEY MATERIAL IS NEVER PRINTED, LOGGED OR ECHOED** — every identity claim is a FINGERPRINT = `sha256(key)[:8]`, 32 bits of a hash, so the transcript is safe to paste into a bug report; the transcript itself is `%USERPROFILE%\.jarvis\admin\transcript.log`, **outside the repo**, append-only, never self-rotating. **`--force` is never passed:** generation goes to a fresh scratch dir, which keeps `gen_control_key.py`'s own fail-closed clobber guard armed and means nothing live is touched until the new pair is fully verified. **CLEANUP IS A GATE, NOT A COURTESY, and runs ONLY after the final gate passes** so an aborted run never destroys its own rollback path: the manual run left six stray artifacts and the one that mattered was **neither old key** — it was the `scp`'d scratch slot in the **BOX's** home directory, a plaintext copy of the **LIVE** key on a dual-booting LAN machine (the key is supposed to exist in exactly two places: the JKEY sector and the Main PC file). **RUNBOOK-FULL-EXERCISE §5.1 CORRECTED:** it says "confirm the backup is exactly 32 bytes" and applies that to the **slot** — the key file is 32 B, the **slot is 512 B**, so as written it would green-light a truncated (unrestorable) backup, which is the one thing a backup check exists to prevent. **Two defects found by RUNNING it, not by review** (the §10 lesson repeating): (a) **embedded double quotes do not survive Windows PowerShell 5.1's native-argument escaping** — `tr -d " \n"` and `cut -d" " -f1` reached the box with their quotes eaten and **silently became no-ops** (the first `-Check` printed a magic of `59 45 4b 4a` vs an expected `59454b4a`); **USED FOR A REAL RE-KEY 2026-07-26 (`a38577f1` → `37b420f0`, box `boot_id=45`) — the write path that had never been exercised end-to-end IS NOW EXERCISED, and the negative test proved REVOCATION: a CORRECTLY-SIGNED frame carrying the SUPERSEDED key was rejected while frames either side of it were answered (`answered=2 dropped=1`). That is a strictly stronger property than the historical `DROP_AUTH` tests, which only ever showed that a TAMPERED frame is rejected.** The header reports **halves MATCH / halves DIFFER / box UNREACHABLE** (the `jarvis_menu.ps1` R3 pattern), a DIFFER points at Rollback rather than offering a re-key, and the three ssh-dependent options are shown **DISABLED WITH THE REASON, never hidden** — a hidden option reads as a missing feature, and the usual reason is that the box is booted into JARVIS, which is exactly the state in which a re-key must not proceed. Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — Operator Admin (Phase C, Main-PC, Windows) — `phasec/scripts/jarvis_admin.bat` is the ENTRY POINT / `phasec/scripts/jarvis_admin.ps1` is the implementation.".
- **Telemetry Console:** `phase4/console/` — honest browser console for the Remote Telemetry Console (8 screens: CommandCenter / Routing / LastResponse / Models / SHIELD / **Capabilities** / **System** / **Control-IN** — the TELEMETRY stream is read-only (nothing on those 7 screens can change the box); **Control-IN (6-5/M3-4b) is the ONE non-read-only surface** and is a separate, gated send path, so the chrome says "telemetry is read-only", never "the console is read-only" — Capabilities auto-populated from the telemetry `flags_list` per the UI–feature-parity rule: one row per reported flag, unknown flags surfaced as "new capability" never dropped, plus derived-from-real-field rows; **System** renders ONLY the real System telemetry: RAM available to JARVIS (`total_ram_mb`), model resident + fixed floor, inference ACTIVE/IDLE + workload duty cycle, NVMe size + telemetry-log fullness, and (2026-07-09) box uptime — the real `uptime_ms` v1 wire field rendered as a CommandCenter header `up ≈… · boot N` line + a System "Uptime" stat, ≈-marked + "TSC-derived (approximate)"-caveated (no RTC, never wall-clock) — no CPU% gauge, no disk-activity graph, no fabricated used/free/IOPS/SMART). The live 8-screen honest console; Control-IN is the ONE non-read-only surface and its composer is ENABLED against the live box since the 6-5 flip 2026-07-21, while the Routing.jsx veto rows RENDERED LIVE FROM THE REAL FEED at the C/M4 FLIP 2026-08-02, boot_id=49. CURRENT counts: honesty 254/254, logic 27/27, e2e 51/51, receiver 350/350. **hit/latency only, never a "memory helped" claim** (e2e value-pins the rendered retrieval_hits == the live field). **#5/M2 (2026-07-04):** the SHIELD screen gains a flag-gated "Failure-learning" section (`shield_learn_keys` "Actions with learned risk" + `shield_learn_max_risk_x100`/100 "Max learned risk", `—` until `TLM_F_SHIELD_LEARN` live) worded **"learns which actions fail and raises their risk — monitor-only, not a live blocker; enforcement is Phase 6"** + a Capabilities auto-row "SHIELD failure-learning (monitor-only)" — NEVER a blocked-count; **live tok/s is REAL since v4** — the CommandCenter Throughput tile renders `infer_last_tok_x100`/100 captioned "live · last inference" (dimmed + "idle (serving from cache)" when not generating; "no inference yet this boot" before the first), with the 5.46 deployed benchmark kept as a separately-labeled reference — never conflated, never a fabricated `tok_s` alias (receiver key-ban stays); the card NEVER sums wakes+behaviors (the deliberate double-count = two honest views of one event); **6-5/M3-4b (2026-07-19) — the two-way SEND UI:** a NEW **`ConsoleControl.jsx`** "Control-IN" screen (rail item `message-circle`) = a composer (`<input type="text">`, never a `textarea`, never "Ask JARVIS") + a seq-correlated reply transcript + an honesty-ceiling card. It POSTs `{"query":…}` to the receiver's loopback `/send` (the browser holds NO key and never touches the wire) and renders the `control_reply` SSE record by verdict (0 answered / 1 refused / 2 degraded / 3 failed / **4 answered-but-truncated**); an unverified reply is never presented as a trusted answer (**since M4b the receiver DROPS it upstream, so it never reaches the console at all** — the panel's `UNVERIFIED` badge is defence-in-depth). a non-200 renders the server's `error` verbatim (503 adds "run the receiver with `--send`"), never a traceback. `telemetry.js` routes `kind === 'control_reply'` to a SEPARATE bounded ring (`state.replies`, cap 30) **before** `ingest()` — a reply never becomes `state.latest` and never touches connState/lastArrival/seq-gap accounting. **One deliberate deviation:** correlation masks BOTH sides to 16 bits, because the JRPL reply echoes `seq` as u16 LE while the request seq is u64 (a raw `===` would never match past 65535) — commented in-file. every reply row carries the trust marker (**M4b**: "signed by the box (HMAC-SHA256), verified here · signed, not encrypted — the text was plaintext on the wire"; the M3-4b "CRC-checked, not authenticated" wording is RETIRED as now-false, and the badge is `UNVERIFIED` gated on crc_ok && hmac_ok); and the delivery claim was corrected from "the answer comes back to this console only" to "**addressed** only to this console (unicast to the provisioned address, never broadcast; that is addressing, not a proof no other host saw it)" — the project's own record marks third-host exclusivity NOT PROVEN, so the gate now BANS the exclusivity phrasings and REQUIRES the addressing framing. **the honesty gate REQUIRES the "routing decision" framing + the explicit "not a breakdown of queries answered" line and BANS any answered-breakdown phrasing or a total derived against `control_in_answered`** (the counts genuinely do not sum to answered — a routed INFER can still degrade or time out). The pre-existing "Workload split" aggregate (with its "aggregate counters — not per-query routing" caveat) and the static specialist roadmap diagram are BOTH KEPT and gate-pinned — two different query populations must never be rendered as one number. **THREE distinct states, never collapsed:** `sem_inited===1` live · `===0` renders "semantic recall gated off (not yet live)" (never a live-looking 0) · `null` (a v12 frame from the deployed box) renders "this box does not report semantic recall". and adds a **SCOPED** ban on the bare "understands" inside the card only — global would break `ConsoleSystem.jsx`'s existing honest NEGATION for cache growth (*it never "understands" them*), exactly as "encrypted" is scoped so "signed, not encrypted" stays sayable. **C/M4 (2026-08-01): the Routing.jsx "Control-IN routing" card gains the ROUTING-VETO rows** — field-derived from the v14 `route_veto_checked`/`route_vetoed` fields, gated on `veto_inited` (no Capabilities auto-row: the flags word is exhausted, the v12/v13 precedent), **THREE states never collapsed**: `veto_inited===1` renders "Vetoes checked" + "captures rerouted to the model" · `===0` renders "veto gated off (not yet live)" (never a live-looking 0) · `null` (a v13/v12 frame — what the deployed box emits today) renders "this box does not report the veto". The surface carries the direction + semantics in words: *"the embedder may only reroute a status capture to the model, never the reverse; **identifier-shaped derivation teeth** (`route_veto_checked -` / `route_vetoed +` — expressions, not prose, the recorded self-matching-grep trap) **mutation-proven with the unmutated control run first**: dropping the direction line → 1 FAIL, deriving rerouted from checked → 2 FAIL, claiming "routing is fixed" → 1 FAIL, control 0 FAIL both sides. The transcript is now a chat — your query as a right-hand bubble, the box's HMAC-verified reply as a left-hand bubble under the DS "J" mark, newest at the bottom, stick-to-bottom auto-scroll (never yanks the view during scrollback; sending re-sticks), the composer pinned at the bottom of the screen, and the "What this channel is" honesty card as the channel intro at the top of the scroll; receiver-side errors / awaiting / the 15-s quiet notice / an UNVERIFIED record render as avatar-less SYSTEM STRIPS — only a reply that passed HMAC verification is ever presented as the box speaking. Every pinned honesty string/behavior preserved (verdict rendering 0–3, seq correlation masked to 16 bits, the per-reply trust marker, the 172-byte counter, "the browser never holds the key"). **Console truth-audit 2026-07-09 (5-agent review + live-feed validation, every rendered value re-verified against `packet_to_record`):** `last_text` is the response **HEAD** (first ≤47 chars — `main_x86.c` copies `full_response[0..47]`), so the old "truncated tail" framing/ellipsis was WRONG → all 3 render sites now say "response head" with a trailing ellipsis ("truncated tail" is gate-BANNED); The three states stay distinct and must not be collapsed: `veto_inited===1` live · `===0` "gated off" · `null` "this box does not report the veto" (a v13/v12 frame). Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — Telemetry Console".
- **Console vendored libs:** `phase4/console/vendor/` — pinned offline runtime libs (React 18.3.1 + ReactDOM dev builds, `@babel/standalone` 7.29.0, **Lucide 1.21.0**) so CI/serve is hermetic — no live CDN (replaced the `lucide@latest` supply-chain risk). `index.html` loads `./vendor/*.js` (no SRI needed for local files). The honesty gate's non-recursive `os.listdir` scan does not reach this subdir.
- **Console golden fixture:** `phase4/console/fixtures/golden_telemetry.json` — the single source for the `/events` key contract (`meta.keys`/`fmt`/`size` + a deliberate frame sequence: loading×2, STATS, INFER, STATE, HAS_ERROR, seq-gap, corrupt) — plus `golden.pcap` (generated, committed; CI-gated for drift).
- **Telemetry fixture (shared packer):** `phase3/scripts/telemetry_fixture.py` — `build_packet`/`build_pcap_many`/`frame_to_packet` + `REQUIRED_RECORD_KEYS` (derived from `packet_to_record`, never hardcoded). Used by `test_telemetry_receiver.py` (key-contract Layer A → 112/112) and `gen_golden_pcap.py`.
- **Golden pcap generator:** `phase3/scripts/gen_golden_pcap.py` — regenerates `golden.pcap` from the JSON (guards that the packed size still matches `meta.fmt`/`meta.size` — three asserts at `:38-40` — plus the canonical 0xCBF43926 CRC vector; **the size is deliberately NOT restated here — the wire version lives in exactly ONE place, the receiver's `FMT_V*` ladder, and every other copy is a future stale fact**); CI step "Phase 4: Golden telemetry fixture up-to-date" fails on drift.
- **Console logic test (Layer B):** `phase4/console/test_console_logic.py` — Playwright-Python (headless Chromium) drives the real `telemetry.js` with a stubbed `EventSource` + `page.clock` (no real SSE); deterministically asserts connState live/stale (the 2800 ms watchdog)/crcfail, seq-gap `droppedPackets`, new-boot reset, and cold-start `queriesPerSec()` via `getState()`; zero console errors. 14/14. CI: "Phase 4: Console logic unit test".
- **Console E2E test (Layer C):** `phase4/console/test_console_e2e.py` — Playwright-Python boots the real `telemetry_receiver.py --replay golden.pcap`, asserts the page mounts (Babel+React, no errors), connState `live`, model/`num_nodes`/INFER-`last_text` render, `simulated==false` (false-pass guard — no fallback) and **every live `flags_list` flag renders a Capabilities row** (feature-not-shown = hard fail); Layer C: boots the real telemetry_receiver.py --replay golden.pcap and asserts the page mounts, connState live, simulated==false, that every live flags_list flag renders a Capabilities row, the System screen fields, and each value-pin from episodic records through retrieval and cache growth to uptime. 33/33 -> CI Phase 4: Console E2E; it needs playwright==1.60.0. **Now also PINS the episodic console rendering (Phase 5 G1/G2): waits for a MEMORY-bearing frame, then requires the "Episodic memory store" Capabilities row + the System "Episodic records" value == the live episodic_count (was opportunistic flag-only — closes the host-CI hole where episodic rendering was proven only on the box); **G3/M4b** adds a "Retrieval before inference" Capabilities-row label + a System "Preambles packed" value-pin (rendered == live `retrieval_hits`; the golden infer frame carries 3); **#6/M2** adds the "Cache growth — learns frequent queries" flag-label + a System "Patterns promoted" value-pin (rendered == live `cache_growth_count`; the golden infer frame carries 12); **v4** adds an early-watcher Throughput value-pin (rendered live tok/s == `infer_last_tok_x100`/100; the golden infer frame carries 152 → "1.52"); **#5/M2** adds the "SHIELD failure-learning (monitor-only)" flag-label + a SHIELD-screen "Actions with learned risk" value-pin (rendered == live `shield_learn_keys`; the golden infer frame carries 1) that also requires the "not a live blocker" wording on-screen; **#4/M2** adds the "Semantic memory (distilled facts)" flag-label + a System "Distilled facts" value-pin (rendered == live `semantic_fact_count`; the golden infer frame carries 1 — the honest box yield); **uptime (2026-07-09)** adds a System "Uptime" value-pin (rendered == the live `uptime_ms` formatted + ≈-marked; the expected string is computed by an INDEPENDENT Python-side formatter so a broken JS `fmtUptime` can't self-consistently pass); **truth-audit (2026-07-09)** adds Routing/Models/LastResponse render coverage (previously ZERO — three screens were never mounted), the llama.cpp-provenance + "not the deployed build" + "response head" on-screen assertions, and STRENGTHENS flag-parity to require the maximal 12-flag frame (it could previously pass opportunistically on a 3–6-flag frame, leaving most feature rows unexercised); replay-rate 0.1→0.3 s/frame for render-commit headroom.** Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — Console E2E test (Layer C)".
- **Llama Weight Loading:** `phase3/src/ai/llama_load.c` — **KV cache sized by `n_unique` (`n_layers − shared_kv_layers`) for shared-KV Gemma 4** (`llama_alloc_state`): own-KV layers are the dense first `n_unique` and every `kv_share_map[i]` resolves `< n_unique` (see `llama_quant.c:826-828`), so slots `[n_unique, n_layers)` were `calloc`'d-but-dead — **~40 MiB reclaimed for Gemma 4 E2B (57% of the old KV alloc)**. Allocation-only: the read/write path is unchanged, so generation is **byte-identical by construction (the reads never touched the freed slots)** — BOX-CONFIRMED 2026-07-01: the G3 A/B OFF run generated 22 coherent Gemma responses on the shared-KV build (the previously-owed Gemma-coherent smoke; no longer outstanding). `state->kv_n_layers` exposes the allocated slot count; the per-generation KV memsets (deployed PB `inference_server.c` + bench/test `reset_state`s) were swept `n_layers` → `state->kv_n_layers` to match the smaller buffer. Non-shared models + the F32 `llama_forward.c` path get `kv_n_layers == n_layers` (unaffected). Host-tested `test_llama_load.c` (Test 8: 35/20→15-slot alloc + last-slot writable; Test 9: non-shared == n_layers).
- **Load-time quant-type gate (H2):** `qmodel_load` now HARD-REJECTS unsupported quant tensor types at load (whitelist F32/F16/BF16/Q4_0/Q8_0/Q4_K/Q5_K/Q6_K via `dequant_type_supported` = `dequant_type_block_size != 0`); a post-resolve sweep over every non-NULL `qtensor_t` (the 6 globals + all 25 per-layer fields, incl. inline-assigned w_gate/wq) `goto fail`s on the first unsupported type — no more silent all-zero activations → coherent-looking garbage. Hot path untouched. Regression in `test_llama_quant.c` (predicate truth table + F32 positive control + Q2_K token_embd/ffn_gate negatives).
- **GGUF Vocab Extraction:** `phase3/src/ai/gguf_vocab.c/h` — **SEC-hardened (2026-06-30):** the raw-binary string readers (`buf_read_gguf_string_fixed` + `buf_skip`) now use **overflow-safe subtraction** (`r->pos > r->len || len > r->len - r->pos`) instead of `r->pos + len > r->len`, which **wrapped** for an attacker-controlled u64 `len` → spurious pass → up to ~511 B **OOB `memcpy` read** from a crafted GGUF (the sole `_fixed` caller reads a key into `char[512]`); `_fixed` also gained a `len > out_size` size cap (mirroring `_alloc`'s existing 1 MB cap). GGUF is owner-provisioned (semi-trusted) → **defense-in-depth**. ASan regression `test_gguf_vocab_overflow.c` (9/9, **teeth-proven** — the pre-fix code trips ASan `heap-buffer-overflow`) → CI step "Phase 3: GGUF Vocab overflow guard (C, ASAN)". Found in the briefings strncpy/OOB audit.

PA creates the N−1 workers in PB's VSpace/CSpace (PB has no allocator) and resolves `jarvis_sel4_worker_entry` from PB's ET_EXEC `.symtab` (PA never links it); PB-only `JARVIS_SEL4_SMP`. Production `NUM_NODES=6` → Gemma 4 E2B 5.46 tok/s (3.57×). Worker MUST `seL4_SetIPCBuffer` (started outside sel4runtime). **Host-CI-covered since H1** (was bare-metal-verified only / zero CI) — see next.

#### Compile-time flags (`phase3/src/sel4/jarvis_debug.h`)
Defaults are read from the header, never from here — count probes with `grep -cE '#define JARVIS_[A-Z0-9_]*PROBE[[:space:]]+[0-9]' phase3/src/sel4/jarvis_debug.h` (16, all 0).

  - `JARVIS_DBG_IPC` — Per-query IPC tracing in Process A (`[DBG] q=N slot=N`)
  - `JARVIS_DBG_PB` — Process B inference tracing (`[PB] handle_query, generating, decoded`)
  - `JARVIS_DBG_RING` — Ring health checks before send (`[PB] ring @... magic=... w= r=`)
  - `JARVIS_DBG_STATS` — Periodic stats every 100 queries (default: ON)
  - `JARVIS_DBG_INFER_SUMMARY` — Per-inference summary line (default: ON)
  - `JARVIS_DBG_BOOT_LOG` — NVMe log at every boot stage + auto-capture serial output (default: OFF)
  - `JARVIS_G3_PROBE` — **P5 G3/M4c synthetic-fact probe (default: OFF, box-only):** with `JARVIS_G3_RETRIEVAL=1`, PA seeds ONE **arbitrary, UNKNOWABLE** fact (marker `SYNTHPROBEZX9Q7`; query = the "JARVIS reactor authorization phrase" — unanswerable from the model's own knowledge) and **forces it as the first inference query** (deterministic, one-shot), then self-checks (case-insensitive contains) whether the model echoes the marker after retrieval injects it → `[PROBE] marker=… hit=<0|1>`. Because the question is unanswerable without context, **hit=1 proves the model USED injected memory (present-AND-used), hit=0 proves it IGNORED usable context** — both honest, neither a "memory helped" claim (offline A/B). (M4c-fix: the original page-fault query was answerable from the model's own knowledge, so hit=0 was uninformative.) Gated → deploy byte-identical. (The `[RETR]` line also now carries `lat_us=` — the M4a-measured in-RAM retrieval latency in µs; the smoke confirms < 50 ms.)
  - `JARVIS_G3_AB` — **P5 G3/M6 OFF-vs-ON A/B harness (default OFF, box-only):** one `[AB] q= query= resp=` full-transcript serial line per inference for offline OFF-vs-ON judging over the deterministic (`rng_state=42`) query set — the M6 re-flip gate ("does retrieval HELP", not just "work"). Independent of `JARVIS_G3_PROBE` (leave PROBE=0 for the A/B). Gated → deploy byte-identical.
  - `JARVIS_ACTIONS` — **Phase 6 K: the it-acts self-heal spine (default: ON since K/M4, 2026-07-08).** In the deploy default (1), Process A inits the JACT action-audit store (@ LBA 21,120,000), links the SHIELD action gate (`shield_action.c`), and runs the fault-EP receiver + miss-counter self-heal (PB crash/wedge → `shield_assess` SHIELD-scored → reuse-in-place respawn → JACT audit). Closes SEC-039 FOR THE ACTION PATH; the QUERY path stays passive (Process B returns ALLOW) BY DESIGN — this does NOT block queries. When 0 the includes + store + probe compile out (action-inert, byte-identical to pre-K).
  - `JARVIS_ACTION_PROBE` — **Phase 6 K/M1 induced-BLOCK probe (default: OFF, box-only; needs `JARVIS_ACTIONS=1`):** one-shot at boot, three cases through the REAL linked gate, NOTHING executes — (A) benign allowlisted → `[ACTION-PROBE] ... EXECUTE risk=10 (not executed)`, (B) the blocklisted poison id → `BLOCKED risk=100 (audited)` (the SEC-039 closure teeth), (C, needs `JARVIS_SHIELD_LEARN=1`) PROBE_HIGH `risk=75 EXECUTE → (failure) risk=85 BLOCKED on 2nd attempt (K-e)` (Phase-5 criterion-2's live half). **Box-verified 2026-07-04** (OFF-vs-ON KVM: 3 probe lines exact, 2 BLOCKED JACT audit records, `[INFER]` byte-identical OFF↔ON, err=0). Gated → deploy byte-identical.
  - `JARVIS_KM2A_SPIKE` — **Phase 6 K/M2a-2 reuse-in-place respawn spike (default: OFF, box-only KVM measurement).** When 1, Process B gains a muslc-init-safe `pb_restart_entry` (re-enters PAST musl's one-time init on a dedicated ABI-aligned restart stack, REUSING the warm model — no re-alloc) and Process A runs an N=16 spike after the ready handshake: suspend PB-main → `WriteRegisters(rip=pb_restart_entry, fresh SP, fs_base preserved)` → Resume → drain-then-poll ACK → one inference → measure the 3 zero-RESOURCE axes. **Box-measured 2026-07-05 — Strategy A LOCKED** (16 respawns all flat: PB-heap pointers byte-identical, cslot-delta=0, coherent gen, `fs_base` kept, no musl assert; OFF byte-identical). Independent of `JARVIS_ACTIONS` (stays 0 — deploy action-inert). `pb_serve_loop` is extracted unconditionally (behavior-neutral); everything else compiles out when 0. Committed as the K/M2b foundation (SYSTEM_DESIGN §4.2).
  - `JARVIS_MONITORS` — **Phase 6 goal 6-1: always-on monitors (default: ON since the flip 2026-07-09).** Default-ON since the flip 2026-07-09: the honest watcher set - q_errors-delta, self-heal-rate, store-wrap, uptime-milestone - fires ONCE per crossing through mon_notify into the K spine, giving one [ANOMALY] line, a JACT record and actions_fired++. Supervised bare-metal boot_id=16 gave the first on-wire v8; boot_id=17 the first NONZERO v8 (monitors_fired=2, last_monitor_event=5). **First on-wire NONZERO v8 validated 2026-07-11 (boot_id=17 — closes the flip's one UNVERIFIED item):** a 28.7 h unattended bare-metal run (q=28.0M, err=0, restart_count=0, 5.47 tok/s live, crc_ok) crossed the 1 h + 24 h uptime marks → `monitors_fired=2`/`last_monitor_event=5` live on the wire + the two JACT `action=2` uptime records read back off-box — exactly the predicted honest shape, zero false positives. Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Flags — JARVIS_MONITORS".
  - `JARVIS_MONITOR_PROBE` — **Phase 6 6-1/M1+M2 monitor probe (default: OFF, box-only; needs `JARVIS_MONITORS=1`):** induces every watcher deterministically — a synthetic over-threshold delta into the q_errors watcher's WINDOW DELTA (never the real counter — honest) for the first 3 `[STATS]` windows, SHORT uptime marks (5/15/30 s instead of 1h/24h/7d), wrap thresholds = current `total_entries`+1 (the next store write trips), and 2 REAL respawns in one window for heal-rate (real `km2b_do_respawn` cycles + real JACT records — wire counters stay truthful); `[MON-PROBE]` proof lines. Its OWN flag (the G3_PROBE/SHIELD_PROBE one-flag-per-probe precedent) because `JARVIS_ACTION_PROBE` runs end at the committed HARDLOOP experiment's Outcome-B starve and never reach the workload.   - `JARVIS_CONTROL_IN` — **Phase 6 goal 6-5 control-IN — the two-way conversation channel (DEFAULT-ON since the 6-5 FLIP, M4e 2026-07-21; the box's FIRST STANDING untrusted network inbound).** DEFAULT-ON since the 6-5 FLIP, M4e 2026-07-21 - the deployed image is two-way whenever JARVIS runs, behind three INDEPENDENT fail-closed gates (key + floor + console address). The re-flip at boot_id=30 passed every leg: acc=55 drop=16 in one boot, err=0 at q=175,600, JACT 49 EXECUTED + 21 BLOCKED with all 25 raw-query probes ZERO. Third-host non-observation MEASURED FOR THIS LAN at boot 52. The deployed image is two-way whenever JARVIS runs; **NEVER committed-flipped in M2a** — the box test flips it TRANSIENTLY; `pa_ctrl_gate` (`main_x86.c`) runs `query_shield_assess` on each validated (post-auth/replay) query: **QS_ALLOW → routes ONE inference** (the 6-2 wake-lane discipline: fold-duty / F9 drain / **PREAMBLE-CLEAR** so a stale workload preamble can't contaminate the user query / **strict `pk_seq==cseq` first-chunk correlation** but a permissive multi-chunk drain since PB renumbers chunks / the 3 wake deviations — a fault funnels the self-heal + breaks (never `goto`), a timeout NEVER bumps `q_errors`, feeds the PB miss under `KM2B_LANE_CTRL`) → `[CTRL-IN-RESP]` + episodic write (`EPI_ACT_CONTROL_IN=3`, **excluded from cache-growth/retrieval/distill by the tag value alone** — all three filter `==EPI_ACT_INFER`) + ONE JACT `action=5` EXECUTED; `pa_ctrl_gate` now sends the answer BACK on EVERY exit path via `ctrl_send_reply` (a `JRPL`-magic + version + echoed request-`seq` + verdict + bounded printable-sanitized text + trailing zlib CRC-32, offsetof-style so a later field auto-extends the CRC region), built with `net_build_udp_unicast(…, CONTROL_CONSOLE_MAC/IP, src=51000, dst=CONTROL_REPLY_PORT=51002)` — **NEVER broadcast, NEVER `cres`-derived addressing**. Four verdicts, exactly ONE reply per exit path (an auditable invariant): 0=answered (the sanitized answer), 1=refused (**the reason-class LABEL ONLY** — the raw query never leaves the box), 2=degraded, 3=failed. **HONEST CLAIM — PROVEN:** the reply is unicast-ADDRESSED to the provisioned console only (box-side `dst_ok` + on-wire dst MAC/IP), never broadcast, and the raw query never leaves the box (the refuse reply is len=21 = the LABEL only). **NOT PROVEN:** that no OTHER LAN host received it — no third-host negative capture (a switch property, not our code; deferred to M4). **JACT** newest two = `action=5 EXECUTED "control-in answered"` + `action=5 BLOCKED "refuse key-extraction"` (teeth: label only, never the query — from this run alone since only `pa_ctrl_gate` writes action=5). **HONEST CLAIM — PROVEN:** the reply is unicast-ADDRESSED to the provisioned console only (box-side `dst_ok` + the Main-PC L2/L3 capture, never broadcast) and the raw query never leaves the box (refuse = label only). **NOT PROVEN:** that no other LAN host received it — no third-host negative capture (a switch property; deferred to M4). Security impact NIL (no attacker path to a cold power cut — no physical access, no Ubuntu path, no network power control; and a replay only RE-ANSWERS an already-answered query, unicast to the CONSOLE, never to the attacker) ⇒ not flip-blocking — but the record must not rest on an overstated absolute. Every leg exists and is proven separately (the browser→receiver leg by the Playwright e2e against a stubbed `/send`; the frame-format leg by the differential test; the box→console leg by the M3-2b bare-metal wire proof at boot_id=25 using a standalone scapy signer) — but they have never been run END TO END, because the deployed box has control-IN gated off. **Box gate PASSED 2026-07-20 (KVM only; the deployed 6-3 image, md5 `379f6bdb…`, was never touched):** Phase A **OFF object-identity** — `main.c.obj` `.text` 45699 / `.rodata` 1128 / `.data` 96 + `.text` BYTES + `nm` md5 `e4553a35…` all byte-identical to the `c43ffb8` baseline, `control_console.c` neither compiled nor in the CMake list (0 `src/net/*.c` entries, 0 `JARVIS_CONTROL_IN` defs after teardown). **NOT PROVEN — the on-wire HMAC'd round-trip** (KVM has no I211, so the reply never leaves): that is the M4e/flip validation. CONTROL_IN=1 was built TRANSIENTLY and booted via ONE-SHOT `--bootnext 0000` (BootOrder never changed). **V2 third-host confidentiality PARTIAL** — the Pi ran on WiFi (no spare cable) and saw **1358 `:51000` broadcast frames from the box MAC (positive control, pre-flight-proven) and ZERO `:51002` replies**: this PROVES the reply is **not broadcast**, but NOT non-observation by another host (a wired-to-wired unicast never enters the wireless medium), so **carry-forward row 4 stays NOT PROVEN** pending a WIRED capture point. **AUDIT HYGIENE teeth-verified:** the JACT `action=5` trail reads `EXECUTED "control-in answered"` x4 + `BLOCKED "refuse key-extraction"`, and a grep for EVERY query string sent returns **0 occurrences** — a refusal records the reason LABEL only, never the raw query. **The re-flip (boot_id=30, the FIXED image) PASSED every leg:** **V-LOAD** = **15/15** benign queries at human pace all answered, `control_in_answered` past the old cap, `dropped=0` (the discriminating proof the bucket REFILLS — M4d's 3-4 queries/boot were BELOW the cap and could never have seen it); durable read-back **`[CTRL-IN-STATS] acc=55 drop=16 (parse=0 rl=16 auth=0 replay=0) bp=0 down=0`** (55 accepted in ONE boot vs the old ceiling of 8; every drop attributable to the deliberate flood; no backpressure loss; input process never down), **err=0** at q=175,600, **0 FATAL/RESTART/ANOMALY**, JACT `action=5` = 49 EXECUTED + 21 BLOCKED with ONLY the two fixed literals as triggers and **all 25 raw-query substring probes returning ZERO**. that is safe because the HMAC key lives in PA and never enters Process B's context, so the model cannot leak what it does not have (structural containment, K-b). **STILL NOT PROVEN — the WIRED third-host capture** (carry-forward row 4): the reply is unicast-ADDRESSED and provably not broadcast, but non-observation by another wired host has never been captured (M4d V2 ran the Pi on WiFi, which cannot see a wired-to-wired unicast). Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Flags — JARVIS_CONTROL_IN".
  - `JARVIS_CONTROL_IN_PROBE` — **Phase 6 6-5 synthetic-frame self-test (default: OFF, box/KVM-only; needs `JARVIS_CONTROL_IN=1`). The flag VALUE is a MODE (the WAKE_PROBE precedent):** **mode 1** (M2b-1) = the SPLIT-pipeline accept/tamper probe (PA stages a signed JCTL frame through the mailbox → `jarvis-input` parses → PA HMACs the candidate → `[CTRL-IN-PROBE] accept q=status cand_seq=N` + a tampered tag → `tamper=DROP_AUTH`); Default: OFF, box/KVM-only (needs JARVIS_CONTROL_IN=1); the flag VALUE is a MODE - 1 the split-pipeline accept/tamper probe, 2 the induced-death probe, 3 the query-SHIELD gate + ROUTE over all four verdicts, the CAPPROBE verdict-4 leg and, since 2026-08-11, the EMPTYPROBE leg (seq 16 / nonce 0xF0) that must report verdict 3, 4 the cross-reboot floor 2-boot gate. Stays default-0 after the 6-5 flip. Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Flags — JARVIS_CONTROL_IN_PROBE".
  - `JARVIS_CONTROL_IN_RECALL` — **Phase 6 6-5/M5-recall: cross-session control-IN recall (DEFAULT-ON since the M5-recall FLIP 2026-07-22 — the deployed box now recalls a repeated control-IN query's OWN PRIOR ANSWER across sessions, three-gate hardware-proven; the honest scope is EXACT-REPEAT recall, NOT semantic association — see the RE-RUN evidence below).** DEFAULT-ON since the M5-recall FLIP 2026-07-22, three-gate hardware-proven, over a DEDICATED control-IN store @ LBA 21,140,000 (4096 slots) whose index HOLDS RECALLABLE TURNS ONLY; the window is G3_R_MAX_CONTROL_IN 256 with the workload UNCHANGED. The [CTRL-IN-STATS] line gained recall= and, since 2026-08-09, idx=, both durable - deployed 2026-08-10 at boot_id=52 where the first read idx=88. When 1, `pa_ctrl_gate`'s §7.6 preamble-CLEAR becomes a control-IN-ONLY RETRIEVAL: a DEDICATED control-IN index (`g_ctrl_epi_index`) over control-IN records, filtered `g3_candidate_usable(..., EPI_ACT_CONTROL_IN, EPI_OUT_OK)`, selected **EXACT-KEY-ONLY** (`g3_select_exact_only` — the G3/M6 P6 lesson; no recency fallback) and rendered by `g3_build_preamble_answer_only`, so the ONLY thing injectable is **this box's own prior ANSWER to THIS EXACT question** (the prior QUESTION text is never embedded). **The P6 hygiene the clear enforced is preserved BY CONSTRUCTION** — every candidate is tag-3, so a workload preamble can still never reach a control-IN inference; **KEY CURRENCY (subtle, load-bearing):** the key is `cache_hash(cache_normalize_query(qs))` over the SANITIZED 120 B-bounded `qs` that `epi_batch_add` stores under — hashing the raw `cres->query` frame bytes would mint a key that never matches the persisted one whenever sanitisation or the bound bites, and recall would silently never fire. NEVER claim "JARVIS remembers your conversation" or any conversational-reasoning capability. **That guard is LOAD-BEARING — NOT vestigial, and must NOT be deleted** (its original rationale WAS stale and was corrected 2026-07-21: it claimed RECALL rides the shared boot recall-scan's `g_epi_recall_rec` fetch buffer, which stopped being true once the lane took its own store/buffer/scan). `[CTRL-RECALL]` is a plain `puts_serial` line, which the BOOT_LOG=0 deploy config never captures to the durable NVMe log; indexing one but not the other is useless.** WHY (a real defect, fixed): `epi_index_lookup` is NEWEST-WINS, returns a SINGLE index, and has NO fallback to the next-newest entry for the same key, while `pa_ctrl_gate` applies `g3_candidate_usable` AFTER the fetch — so a FAILED turn (degraded/timeout/fault, `resp_len` 0) indexed under the same key becomes the newest, the lookup returns it, the filter rejects it, and recall silently returns `hit=0`, **PERMANENTLY shadowing the good prior answer for that key**. Failed turns are STILL WRITTEN (forensics), just never indexed. Pinned by `test_episodic_store` T10 (9 -> **10 PASS**). **KNOWN BOUNDED LIMITS — recorded, NOT handled (neither is fixed):** (a) **post-wrap `logical_index` drift** — the index stores logical indices captured before the dedicated region rolls, so after a wrap a stale entry can point at a re-used slot; the `read-key == qkey` re-check catches it and the turn degrades to a MISS; (b) **index saturation** — `g_ctrl_epi_index` is capped at `CTRL_EPI_MAX_ENTRIES` (4096) and simply stops appending when full, so later turns are never indexed and also degrade to a MISS. **Both degrade to a silent MISS, never a WRONG recall**, and both are ~4096 human-paced control-IN turns away — but neither is repaired and neither is exercised by any gate. **[BOTH LIMITS NOW EXERCISED 2026-08-05 — A9/1 (`cc0dfb9`); "not exercised by any gate" is CLOSED, "not repaired" still STANDS (the extraction was behaviour-neutral by construction).** (a) post-wrap drift is `test_ctrl_epi_index.c` T5, (b) saturation is T3. **The safety claim is CONFIRMED and SHARPENED rather than merely re-asserted, and the sharpening is the finding:** the index promises nothing about staleness — after a roll a stale entry does resolve to a re-used slot — so the tests pin the safety at the CALLER's `query_key == ckey` re-check, which is where it actually lives, instead of crediting the index for it. **RESIDUAL, measured not assumed (T5m/T5n): drift CAN land on a MATCHING key.** That is not a counter-example, because a matching `query_key` IS the same question by construction, so the record fetched is a different answer to the RIGHT question — an identity/freshness imprecision, never a wrong-question recall. So "never a WRONG recall" stands as written; what it does NOT promise, and never did, is "always the NEWEST answer".]** **DEBT:** `ctrl_epi_write`'s index logic has NO direct host coverage because it lives in `main_x86.c` (seL4-only, never host-compiled); T10 pins the INVARIANT (a failed turn must not shadow a good one under newest-wins lookup), not the function. Cross-session recall would therefore return `hit=0` for every realistic case on the deployed box, so flipping it ON would ship a feature that provably never fires. Two fixes, both pure/host-tested: (a) a record whose text contains `<|channel>` / `<channel|>` / `<|think|>` is **NOT RECALLABLE** (bounded scan, length-carried — the text is never NUL-terminated, so never `strstr`), checked BEFORE truncation so a marker anywhere disqualifies the record; GATE 3 (the load-bearing proof, boot_id=34, a FRESH session, off the durable NVMe log) `[CTRL-IN-STATS] acc=5 drop=0 (parse=0 rl=0 auth=0 replay=0) bp=0 down=0 recall=1` — **recall=1** = the persisted marker recalled from the dedicated store on a fresh boot = cross-session recall, err=0, 0 FATAL/FAULT (the session-34 answer opened "Based on the notes provided…" referencing the injected answer-only preamble — corroboration; recall=1 is the proof). **HONEST SCOPE (unchanged): EXACT-REPEAT recall of a repeated query's own prior answer, un-attributable to model knowledge via the unknowable marker; SEMANTIC association (state a fact, ask a DIFFERENT question) stays OUT OF SCOPE, unimplemented (needs embeddings). NEVER "remembers your conversation"/"conversational memory".** Proven on the REAL engine with the question held fixed and only the preamble varied (native harness on the box, Gemma 4 E2B, the exact PB prompt shape `<bos><|turn>user\n[PRE][Q]<turn|>\n<|turn>model\n<|think|>` + ≤50 greedy tokens): **no preamble → clean prose** (byte-identical to the box's `[8:00042]`), **the real mid-sentence preamble → `<|channel>thought`** (byte-identical to the box's `[9:00045]`), **the same fragment with the markdown `**` removed → still garbage** (kills the unclosed-markup theory), **the same text completed to a sentence → clean prose, with and without markdown**. **TWO CORRECTIONS TO THE ORIGINAL DIAGNOSIS, both falsified by measurement:** (a) the cut was NOT the `EPI_RESP_MAX`=256 store cap — the record measured `resp_len=246` (the others 204/199), so a `resp_len >= EPI_RESP_MAX` filter would have matched NOTHING and shipped as a silent no-op; the real cutter is the RETRIEVAL-side `G3_R_MAX`=120, which fires on EVERY answer over 120 B; (b) PB additionally caps generation at **50 tokens** (`inference_server.c:500`), so an answer can arrive in the store already cut mid-sentence well under any store cap — which is why the fix is applied unconditionally, not only when retrieval itself truncated. **FIX:** `g3_clean_answer_len` now cuts back to the last COMPLETE SENTENCE (terminator `.?!` plus any closing `"'`)]*` `, and only when followed by whitespace or end-of-window so `3.5` cannot fake a sentence end); if the window holds NO completed sentence it returns 0 and `g3_build_preamble_answer_only` emits **NOTHING** — not even its header, since a "Notes from a previous answer:" label with no notes is itself a malformed prompt. A miss degrades to exactly the clean no-preamble path. Verified end-to-end on the engine after the fix: the failing record now yields a **0-byte** preamble → the clean answer returns; the marker record still recalls (its completed sentence is kept, the dangling "Please" dropped). **BLAST RADIUS, stated:** `g3_clean_answer_len` is shared, so this also changes the **default-ON workload retrieval lane** (`main_x86.c:6025`), which had the identical latent defect — the deployed workload's generated text will differ wherever its preamble previously ended mid-clause. **WHY THE FLIP GATE MISSED IT:** the M5-recall gate used "what is the JARVIS-MINIFLIP-7X reference value", whose answer COMPLETES inside the 120-byte window — so the truncation path was never exercised. It proved recall *fired*, never that the answer was *good*; the second such miss in a week (cf. the 6-6 held-out suite scoring 95.52% with a real DECLINE false-positive in an untested boundary). **HONEST CEILING, NARROWED: recall now grounds SHORT answers — a fact that fits a sentence inside 120 B. The residual exposure is bounded and worth naming precisely: routing short-circuits **before** the recall block (`served_locally` at `main_x86.c:3062/3070` vs the recall at `:3167`), so a query that still routes SYSFACTS/DECLINE never reaches recall at all and its stale tag-3 record is unreachable. **SCOPE, stated so it is never restated as something stronger: 22/62 counts RECORDS, not QUERIES** — `epi_index_lookup` is newest-wins and consults exactly ONE record, so it is an upper bound on the corpus, never a predicted per-query hit rate. Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Flags — JARVIS_CONTROL_IN_RECALL".
  - `JARVIS_CONTROL_IN_RECALL_PROBE` — **Phase 6 6-5/M5-recall 2-boot demonstration probe (default: OFF, KVM-only; needs `JARVIS_CONTROL_IN_RECALL=1`). ⚠️ ITS NEGATIVE CONTROL HAS EXPIRED ON THE SHARED KVM FIXTURE (measured 2026-08-05, A9/1 G2) — the leg is INCONCLUSIVE there, not failing, and a session that reads it as a failure will chase a defect that does not exist.** Gated default: OFF, KVM-only (needs JARVIS_CONTROL_IN_RECALL=1): it stages a synthetic control-IN query with the FIXED, UNKNOWABLE marker ZQ7X4M, calls epi_commit() so durability is FORCED not assumed, and derives every seq fresh as seq_floor + 1. The MARKER leg is the load-bearing one and still reads hit=1 recall=1 src=exact in both boots. Gated - deploy byte-identical. **The MARKER leg is unaffected and still the load-bearing one** (`ZQ7X4M` recalled `hit=1 recall=1 src=exact` in both boots, across `boot 11 count=17 → boot 12 count=19`). **A second consequence of the same contamination: the probe never takes its boot-1 branch on a used fixture** — the persisted replay floor is already > 0, so both boots log `boot2 (resumed floor)`. **Durability is FORCED, not assumed:** the probe calls `epi_commit()` after the routed query and prints the pending count + a "safe to reboot" line, so "record never written" can never be confused with "record not recalled" (episodic normally commits only at the `[STATS]` cadence, so a reboot before q=100 would silently find nothing). Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Flags — JARVIS_CONTROL_IN_RECALL_PROBE".

The shipped-config silence is proven by the truth-table's deploy-defaults leg plus the box build after this landed.]** **LIVE FALSE POSITIVE FOUND 2026-07-27 (#6 step-2 pre-flight; MEASURED, NOT FIXED — needs its own slot): the SYSFACTS rules match BARE WORDS with no status anchor, so a CONCEPTUAL question is claimed as box state.** `route.c:260` is `if (rt_any(&W, RT_HEALTH_WORDS))` with `RT_HEALTH_WORDS` = {healthy, health, ok, okay, **errors, error, faults**, fine, alright, problems, smoothly, wrong} — so **any query containing the plural `"faults"` (or `"errors"`/`"error"`/`"wrong"`/`"problems"`) is answered from the `err==0` health bit instead of reaching the model**; `RT_UPTIME_WORDS` = {uptime, duration, **hours**} does the same for `SF_UPTIME`. Measured with the SHIPPED `route.c` on the host: `"what is a page fault?"` (SINGULAR) → `ROUTE_INFER`, but *"A page fault costs 10ms. My workload has 40000 faults over 2 hours. What percentage of wall-clock time is lost to faults?"* → **`ROUTE_SYSFACTS` / `SF_HEALTH`**. **This is the SAME CLASS as the bare-noun DECLINE false positive that ABORTED the first 6-6 flip attempt** — `5224a85` added a status anchor to the DECLINE rule and **left the SYSFACTS rules bare**. On an OS project whose subject matter IS faults, errors and memory, the practical effect is that conceptual questions about the system's core topics get a status answer. **Why the held-out suite still reads 95.89%: every SYSFACTS case in both splits is a genuine status query, so the SYSFACTS-vs-INFER boundary is no better exercised than the DECLINE-vs-INFER one was** — the second time this blind spot has produced a green suite over a live defect, and the concrete argument for adding conceptual-question negatives to BOTH sides rather than only the side that last broke. Cost to the #6 run: the pre-registered Q1 had to be reworded, or it would have measured box-state rendering and called it model reasoning. **SCOPE CORRECTED + FIX ATTEMPTED AND REVERTED 2026-07-27 (full evidence: `phase6/docs/PHASE_6_GOAL_6-6_ROUTING.md` §9).** The defect is **FIVE reachable paths, not two families**: HEALTH, the UPTIME bare-noun list, the UPTIME `rt_seq2` PHRASE clauses (`how long does a context switch take?` → `up 76 seconds`), BOOT_ID, MODEL (`how does a transformer model work?` → `Gemma 4 E2B`) and NODES (`how many nodes are in a red-black tree?` → `6 workers`). **MODEL and NODES were both expected to be already-guarded; only NODES is, and only against count-context, not against concept questions.** A keyword ANCHOR mirroring `5224a85` was built and **measured NET WORSE THAN THE DEFECT, so it was REVERTED**: against a two-sided corpus (37 genuine status questions incl. 24 adversarially-reproduced regressions / 32 conceptual) the baseline scores **0 false-negatives + 32 false-positives = 32 wrong**, the anchor **23 + 22 = 45 wrong**, and the best of **30 keyword configurations 12 + 15 = 27** — i.e. every configuration still loses a third of genuine status questions (`do we have errors?`, `give me a health check`, `can i get the uptime?` handed to a model that cannot know; the classifier turned on whether the operator said "you" or "we"). **THE FINDING: no keyword fix exists.** Shrinking the vocabularies instead drives false positives to 0 but costs 22 false negatives, and **nine family words appear on BOTH sides and are unfixable by vocabulary at all — `boot`/`cycles`/`errors`/`faults`/`gguf`/`model`/`ok`/`problems`/`wrong`** (*"do we have errors?"* vs *"how are errors handled in rust?"* — same word, opposite intent). Separating them needs an unbounded technical-noun vocabulary, which is precisely `route.h`'s stated non-goal and **the measured miss that gates the Phase C routing lane** (`PHASE_6_GOAL_6-6_ROUTING.md` §8 / C/M4). **A THIRD structural lesson, and the sharpest one: the attempt scored DEV 76/76, HELDOUT 96.47%, 0 INFER misroutes, 127 asserts and a clean 300K fuzz while being net-negative** — because of 66 SYSFACTS positives, **ZERO occupy the (no-self-reference AND concept-word) quadrant any anchor actually decides**, and all 7 auxiliary-bearing positives carry `you`, so the rule under test is never exercised on a positive. **A green suite can be structurally incapable of seeing the regression it was extended to catch; The defect remains LIVE on the deployed image until the veto FLIP, which is a separate operator-supervised session.** **C/M4 FLIP UPDATE (2026-08-02, boot_id=49): THE VETO IS NOW LIVE ON THE DEPLOYED IMAGE and this defect is CUT, not fixed — 32 -> 6 FPs at the cost of 1 FN.** Proven on hardware: the two recorded live-defect examples both die (`what causes page faults?` and `how does a transformer model work?` now reach the model instead of returning `err=0` health / `"Gemma 4 E2B"`), and the never-measured 40000-faults arithmetic question — the one whose SYSFACTS capture forced the #6 pre-registered Q1 to be reworded — is also rerouted. **The 6 surviving quantity-question FPs remain LIVE and one is deliberately demonstrated each run** (`how many cpus does a raspberry pi have` still answers `"6 workers"`). `route.c` and `routing_suite.h` are STILL byte-identical — the veto never edited the classifier — and with VETO=1 the `route_*` counters record the POST-veto FINAL decision with no counter site moved (boot 49: `route_infer 5` = 4 rerouted + 1 natively-INFER). **The honest ceiling is unchanged: a measured cut in ONE defect class, never "routing is fixed"; Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Flags — JARVIS_ROUTING".
