Re-verified under traffic shaping 2026-08-09. **FLIP EVIDENCE (supervised bare metal, boot_id=48, image `0b771bba…`):** two-stage validation — STAGE 1 proved v13/270 B/crc_ok on real hardware with `sem_inited=1`, `err=0`, NN=6, 0 restarts BEFORE any control-IN query, so a wire-format failure could never be mistaken for a recall failure; the port is PROVEN to float32 machine epsilon.** Stage 2 adds (all `#if JARVIS_EMBED`): the qwen **NEOX-RoPE override** in `llama_load.c` (the deployed line is `rope_neox = is_gemma`, so qwen3 would otherwise get INTERLEAVED RoPE — the #1 silent-parity breaker: wrong vectors, no crash) + `qmodel_embed_last` in `llama_quant.c` (last-token pool at the `:1801` tap, post-`output_norm`/pre-LM-head, → L2) + the `embed_vector_probe.c` harness + the `cm1a_vector_parity.py` comparator. **Measured (15/15 probes): GATE 2′ (port correctness, same precision) worst `1−cos = 0.00e+00`** (maxabs 2.6e-7…5.1e-7 = fp32 epsilon) — decisive; **GATE 3** (F32 sanity) worst **3.69e-4** vs the **1.268e-3** MEASURED floor — PASS; our own weight-quant delta (C-F32 vs C-Q8_0) 3.69e-4 = **0.44×** llama.cpp's own 8.45e-4 (same order, and we lose LESS — what keeping F32 activations predicts). **The ORIGINAL GATE 2 (`≤1e-4` cross-engine vs the llama.cpp Q8_0 golden) is HONESTLY RESTATED as a DIAGNOSTIC, not deleted: it is MIS-SPECIFIED BY CONSTRUCTION** — llama.cpp quantizes its ACTIVATIONS (integer dot) while JARVIS's `qdot_row` dequantizes the WEIGHT and dots against **F32 activations**, so the engines differ at identical weight quant and no port fix can reach 1e-4 (measured 1.82e-4…3.69e-4; the legacy threshold would say FAIL). It was **REPAIRED into GATE 2′, not dropped** — GATE 3's 1.268e-3 tolerance is ~3.4× the observed gap, leaving a band where a SUBTLE port bug (wrong `rope_theta`, eps mismatch, misapplied q_norm/k_norm) would pass unnoticed. GATE 2′ = run OUR engine on an **F32 GGUF converted from the same HF checkpoint** vs the ST F32 golden: same activation precision on both sides, and because the checkpoint is **BF16 on disk** and BF16→F32 is a **LOSSLESS widening**, the converted weights are **bit-identical in value** to what PyTorch used ⇒ ZERO weight delta, so any residual is pure arithmetic order. Bands were fixed BEFORE measuring (≤1e-5 noise / ≤1e-4 arithmetic-order / >1e-4 = real discrepancy). **EMBED=0 identity PROVEN on two axes** (first Phase-C change to touch deployed-path files): `llama_load.o` (.text `0x2175`, md5 `dc09abdb…`) + `llama_quant.o` (.text `0x7aed`, md5 `3bb15c01…`) — `.text`/`.rdata`/`.data` contents + `nm` (113/152 syms) all identical vs `da8d5f8`, AND the **preprocessed sources are byte-identical** (compiler-independent). NOTE: **whole-`.o` md5 is an INVALID test here** — the control (same source compiled twice) differs, because clang/COFF embeds a build TimeDateStamp; compare sections+symbols, never the whole artifact (the object-level analogue of the 6-1 "never md5 a packed image" lesson). **C/M1b CARRY-FORWARD (box latency, NOT parity):** `qmodel_embed_last` invokes no LM head itself, but `qmodel_forward` runs the output projection **UNCONDITIONALLY** (`llama_quant.c:~1815`, right after the tap), so every embed token pays a discarded **151669×1024 ≈ 155M-MAC** matmul — embed mode must skip it on the box. **MODEL-GATED-LOCAL, no CI step** (needs the 609 MB Q8_0 GGUF + a 2.38 GB converted F32 GGUF — the model-gated-SKIP precedent). Reproduce: `python llama.cpp/convert_hf_to_gguf.py <hf-snapshot> --outfile <f32.gguf> --outtype f32` (needs `pip install sentencepiece` — NOT because qwen uses SentencePiece, but because `Qwen2Model.set_vocab` catches only `FileNotFoundError` to reach the correct `_set_vocab_gpt2()` fallback, and a missing module raises `ModuleNotFoundError`), then build the harness with `-DJARVIS_EMBED=1 -mavx2 -mfma` (link list incl. `qdot.c`; `-include embed_host_fmemopen.h` on Windows) and run `cm1a_vector_parity.py <c_q8.bin> --c-f32 <c_f32.bin>`. **C/M1b-1 DONE 2026-07-25 (box half, still gated default-0): PA PROVISIONS, LOADS and MAPS the second model; PB does NOTHING with it.** Scope is exactly the design doc §6 — no `MSG_EMBED`, no embed forward, no argv change (PB's log still reports only the Gemma base `1536M` = `0x60000000`). **SEPARATE FAILURE STATE, never `g_model_bad`:** `g_embed_ready` / `g_embed_bad` / `g_embed_absent` disable ONLY the embed capability — `g_model_bad` means "GEMMA is unusable, refuse ALL inference", so letting a missing embedder set it would stop the box answering anything because a not-yet-wired feature failed to load (design OQ3: "fatal to the embed capability, never to boot"). Same fail-closed PATTERN as `103dea6`, different flag: frame-alloc exhaustion skips the read (no overflow), a partial map disables embed only, and Gemma keeps serving in every case. **ABSENT IS THE NORMAL CASE:** with no `QWENEMB.GUF` the box logs `[EMBED] no QWENEMB GUF on JARVIS_DATA - embed capability OFF (normal; Gemma unaffected)` **exactly once** — never per window, never degrading Gemma. **Provisioning:** `QWENEMB.GUF` on `JARVIS_DATA` (the same partition/LBA as Gemma); `setup_nvme_partition.sh` gained an OPTIONAL `EMBED_MODEL_PATH` and hard-asserts the 8.3 short name exactly as it already does for Gemma. **8.3 GOTCHA (cost a failed gate):** `fat32.c`'s `name_match` compares EXACTLY 11 bytes = an 8-char name field SPACE-PADDED + the 3-char ext with NO separator. `"GEMMA2B"` is 7 chars so it takes one pad space; an 8-char stem takes NONE. A first draft used `QWEN3EMB.GUF` → the literal `"QWEN3EMB GUF"` is 12 chars and could never match, so the file was silently "not found". Name is now `QWENEMB.GUF` → `"QWENEMB GUF"` (11), and the provisioner's `fat_8dot3` helper — which used a single literal space and was therefore only correct for stems ≤7 chars — now pads explicitly (`%-8s%-3s`), leaving Gemma's assert unchanged. **T3 — OVERLAP IMPOSSIBLE BY CONSTRUCTION:** the embed base is DERIVED AT RUNTIME from the MEASURED first-model span (`embed_base_for` = Gemma end + a 1 GiB guard, rounded up to 1 GiB), never fixed — a fixed base silently encodes a max-Gemma assumption — and a checked assertion refuses to map if it ever computed low. The `[VMAP]` boot dump prints both regions with computed ends so the non-overlap is provable from the log: `gemma base=0x60000000 pages=758480 end=0x1192d0000` / `embed base=0x180000000 pages=156043 end=0x1a618b000` / `guard=0x66d30000 overlap=NO`. **MEASURED (KVM `-smp 6`, real 639,150,592 B Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B Q8_0):** `156043/156043` pages mapped with **merr == 0**; embed cslot cost **156,940 slots for 156,043 pages ≈ 1.006 slots/page** (`[VMAP] cslot … delta_since_prev=156940` of a 4,194,304-slot CNode). **That measurement corrected the design doc**, which estimated 2 slots/page (~1.83M for both models) — the real per-page cost is about half that. **Absolute CNode headroom is NOT measured by this probe and is not claimed:** a first draft derived `used = (1<<22) − probe` from the K/M2a-2 "descending cslots" note, and the box falsified it (the probe ROSE across the embed map while the derived "used" FELL, and the same call site gave different absolutes in two runs), so only the single-run DELTA is trustworthy. **BOOT-TIME DELTA: UNMEASURED** — it requires bare metal; KVM's emulated NVMe runs at ~130 MB/min and a number from it would be meaningless. The honest fact available is the added I/O volume: **+609 MB on top of Gemma's 2962 MB = +20.6%**. **EMBED=0 identity (§7), with the control the method demands:** the same-source-twice control was run FIRST on the box's own gcc/ELF and md5-of-`.o` **is** deterministic there (all five objects stable across two builds of identical source) — unlike clang/COFF at C/M1a, and now proven rather than assumed. Against baseline `fb1404a`, all five objects (`main.c.obj`, `inference_server.c.obj`, `llama_quant.c.obj`, `llama_load.c.obj`, `tokenizer.c.obj`) are IDENTICAL on `.text`/`.rodata`/`.data`/`nm`; four are identical whole-`.o`; `main.c.obj` differs whole-`.o` in **DWARF ONLY** — verified section-by-section as `.debug_info`/`.debug_abbrev`/`.debug_loclists`/`.debug_line`, with `.text` byte-identical at `0xe8a8` (the documented DWARF avalanche from the added lines). `llama_quant.c`/`llama_load.c` stay identical as C/M1a requires. **C/M1b-2 DONE 2026-07-25 (PB embed state + forward, PROBE-driven, still gated default-0; NO IPC — that is C/M1b-3):** PB now loads the co-resident model (`g_pbe_qm/vocab/tok/state`, all file-scope), caps the context, allocates state, and computes embeddings via `qmodel_embed_last`. PA hands the base/size in argv **appended after the per-worker wake block** (PB recomputes the index from `argv[5]`, so the variable-length wake block does not move; PB's wake loop is gated to stop at the block end instead of walking to `argc`). `llama_quant.c` deliberately does not include `jarvis_debug.h`, so the build script now propagates `JARVIS_EMBED=1` as a `-D` on the PB target with a symmetric teardown — without it `qmodel_embed_last` is silently not compiled and PB fails to LINK. **CONTEXT CAP = 64 tokens** (OQ1): C/M0.5 measured symmetric query-to-query beats asymmetric, so C/M2 embeds the stored QUERY and a control-IN query is hard-capped at 172 B ≈ 40–55 tokens. **COUPLING:** if C/M2 ever embeds ANSWERS (≤512 B ≈ 130 tokens) that is a HEAP RE-BUDGET, not a free switch — 128 tokens is 28 MiB of KV, the 512 default is 112 MiB and does not fit beside Gemma at all. Applied to `config->max_seq_len` after load (grep-proven safe: `LLAMA_MAX_SEQ_LEN` is only a default/clamp at `llama_load.c:146-148`; every allocation sizes off `config->max_seq_len`). **MEASURED (KVM `-smp 6`):** embed ready at 28 layers / dim 1024 / kv_layers 28, ctx `512 -> 64`; **box-vs-HOST vector parity worst `1-cos = 8.3e-13`, max element diff ~1.3e-7, all 15 probes unit-norm** — compared against the C/M1a HOST harness's own output, NOT the loose 1.27e-3 golden floor, which would have hidden a box-specific difference; **per-embed latency ~190 M cycles/token** measured AS-IS (5 tok ≈ 0.98 Gcyc, 15 tok ≈ 2.86 Gcyc) with the LM head still running. **THREADING — the expected ~1e-6 divergence did NOT appear, and the reason matters:** PB's forward IS threaded (`threads=6`, `JARVIS_SEL4_SMP` ⇒ `JARVIS_PARALLEL`), but `jarvis_parallel_for(0, M, qmatmul_qdot_row, …)` partitions by **output ROW** — each row's dot is computed entirely by one worker in serial order — so threading introduces **no reduction reordering at all**; the residual ~1e-7 is clang-host vs gcc-box codegen, not parallelism. **RESPAWN BASELINE EXTENDED (the reason this milestone stands alone):** `pb_restart_entry` asserted four pointers that are ALL GEMMA'S, which would stay identical even if the embed state leaked entirely; it now also prints the embed state's `key_cache`/`value_cache`/`logits`/`layers` plus the heap break. **Proven across 10 induced respawns (≥8 required): all four embed pointers identical ×10, Gemma's flat, heap break FLAT at 29,400 KB (no leak), 0 FATAL, and the post-respawn embed vector BIT-IDENTICAL to the boot-time probe** (`e_v0/v1/v2` = `3d20c4e2/bd75f97c/bc214e34`, matching `[EV] 0` exactly) — so the reused state is not merely un-reallocated but still *usable*. **HEAP (T1, measured BEFORE allocating):** break after Gemma's state **26,644 KB**, after the embed state **29,392 KB**. **HONEST LIMITS ON THAT NUMBER, both box-falsified rather than assumed:** the first version also printed a total/free derived from `morecore_top` and the box showed that total CHANGING mid-boot (80,668 → 56,276 KB), so `morecore_top` is mutated at runtime and any free/percent from it is fiction — removed; and the break is a **lower bound** on bytes allocated, because malloc satisfies requests from already-broken free space without moving it. **T3 RESOLVED BY CONSTRUCTION:** the second `gguf_ctx` is file-scope (`g_pbe_ctx`), so the embed path does not inherit the first model's `main()`-local-surviving-by-frozen-frame accident; the Gemma ctx is deliberately left alone (an ungated deployed-path edit for no functional gain) and stays a carry-forward. **T4:** a failed embed alloc cannot permanently shrink the heap — `llama_alloc_state` frees its own partials via `llama_free_state` before returning −1 (`llama_load.c:818-827`), and this is the **musl** heap where `free` works normally (the never-frees allocator is the seL4 one); Gemma is untouched either way. **EMBED=0 identity re-proven** vs `aeeb8d1` with the control first — all five objects identical on `.text`/`.rodata`/`.data`/`nm`. **STILL UNMEASURED (bare-metal debts, not closed by KVM):** the C/M1b-1 boot-time delta and the HUD FB-panel correction. Box wiring beyond this — `MSG_EMBED` IPC — is C/M1b-3. **THE EMBEDDER IS A NAMED, SIZED, HASHED ARTIFACT — record it, because it went missing once and cost a task (T2a, 2026-07-29).** C/M1b-3 was blocked on discovering `JARVIS_DATA` held only `GEMMA2B.GUF`; the embed GGUF had been cleaned off the box, and nothing recorded WHICH file it was, so re-obtaining it was an investigation rather than a lookup. It is: repo **`Qwen/Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B-GGUF`**, file **`Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B-Q8_0.gguf`**, **639,150,592 B**, **sha256 `06507c7b42688469c4e7298b0a1e16deff06caf291cf0a5b278c308249c3e439`** (identity from `phase3/scripts/embed/golden_meta.json`, never from memory: Q8_0 · dim 1024 · kind qwen3). Provisioned to `JARVIS_DATA` as **`QWENEMB.GUF`** — the name is load-bearing, `"QWENEMB GUF"` is exactly 11 chars and `fat32.c`'s `name_match` compares 11 bytes with NO separator, so an 8-char stem takes no pad space and can never match. **ARTIFACT IDENTITY PROVEN BY HASH, NOT INFERRED:** the fresh HuggingFace download, the copy inside the KVM fixture that C/M1b-1/-2 measured against, and the newly-provisioned `JARVIS_DATA` copy are **all three sha256-identical**, so the recorded `1-cos = 8.3e-13` box-vs-host parity and the C/M1a gates still describe what is on the box. **Re-verified by running the harness, not by trusting the size:** `embed_vector_probe` + `cm1a_vector_parity.py` → **GATE 3 PASS, worst `3.69e-04`** against the measured floor `1.268e-3` — the SAME worst-case figure C/M1a Stage 2 recorded. (GATE 2′ NOT RUN: it needs the separate ~2.38 GB F32 GGUF, and it gates the PORT, which is unchanged.) **The KVM fixture `~/nvme_test.img` already carried `QWENEMB.GUF`** — only the real `JARVIS_DATA` partition was missing it, so KVM-gated embed work was never actually blocked. **`.gitignore:34` `*.gguf` — a 609 MB model must never be committed.** Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Flags — JARVIS_EMBED".
  - **W2 store-wrap now covers the control-IN store (2026-07-25, C/M1b pre-fix 2, LIVE — MONITORS is default-ON):** the 6-1/M2 W2 watcher armed episodic(1)/JACT(2)/semantic(3) but **never `g_ctrl_episodic`(4)** — it predates the control-IN store (added at 6-5/M5-recall) by ~2 weeks — so the ONE store holding real human conversation, and the future training corpus, rolled **SILENTLY** at `CTRL_EPI_MAX_ENTRIES` 4096. LIVE (MONITORS is default-ON): the control-IN store is armed as store id 4 in the W2 store-wrap watcher, gated #if JARVIS_CONTROL_IN_RECALL and reusing MON_EV_STORE_WRAP with no new event type. It is a BENIGN LIVENESS event, once per boot per store, only after CTRL_EPI_MAX_ENTRIES 4096. Extends the EXISTING CI step Phase 6: 6-1 Monitor framework (C) - test_monitors.c 49 PASS. (The `[CTRL-EPI] index FULL` warning is **NOT** equivalent cover: it counts only RECALLABLE turns, so the store can wrap long before the index fills.) Now armed as **store id 4** following the existing pattern exactly — absolute GE latch on the monotonic `hdr.total_entries` at capacity, armed at init ONLY if not already wrapped, an already-rolling store logging one `[MONITOR] control-in already rolling total=` info line so a historical wrap never re-notifies per boot — reusing `MON_EV_STORE_WRAP` (**no new event type**), gated `#if JARVIS_CONTROL_IN_RECALL` (the store's own gate; default-ON). **A rolling store is a BENIGN LIVENESS event, not a problem** — the 6-1 framing ("a MIX of degradation signals and benign liveness events — NEVER 'anomalies detected'") is preserved verbatim. Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — W2 store-wrap now covers the control-IN store (2026-07-25, C/M1b pre-fix 2, LIVE — MONITORS is default-ON)".
  - `JARVIS_MODEL_FAIL_PROBE` — **Phase C / C/M1b pre-fix induction probe (default: 0, box/KVM-only; the VALUE is a MODE).** Gated default: 0, box/KVM-only; the VALUE is a MODE - 1 = force alloc_fail (abort the frame-allocation loop at p=1000), 2 = force one merr (skip one page map at p=500), mutually exclusive BY CONSTRUCTION. KVM-proven 2026-07-25 with infer=0 and the box still up serving cache; FIRST BARE-METAL OUTING 2026-08-04 at boot_id=50. Mode 1 is also a KVM speedup. The modes are **mutually exclusive BY CONSTRUCTION** — mode 1 leaves `nvme_model_loaded == 0`, so PB is spawned model-less and the mapping loop mode 2 targets never runs. never deploy != 0. **REUSABLE TECHNIQUE — mode 1 is a ~20× KVM speedup for ANY model-independent gate, not just a failure probe:** aborting the frame-allocation loop early means the ~2.9 GB model read NEVER HAPPENS, turning a **~40-minute** emulated-NVMe KVM boot into **roughly 2 minutes** (QEMU's emulated NVMe reads at ~130 MB/min, so the model load — not the workload — is the entire cost of a box gate). **HONEST LIMIT:** it does NOT apply to any gate that needs real inference — `[INFER]` byte-identity, generation coherence, tok/s, retrieval/preamble behaviour, or anything reading model weights. **KVM-proven 2026-07-25:** mode 1 → `[MODEL-BAD] frame alloc exhausted at 1000 pages - model NOT read (fail closed)` + `inference REFUSED (frame-alloc)` + the durable `[MODEL-BAD] inference disabled why=frame-alloc detail=1000` per `[STATS]` window, with **`infer=0`** (the lane is never entered — an earlier draft that guarded AFTER `q_infer++` reported a phantom `infer=10`, caught by this very probe) and the box **still up** serving cache (hits 71→220 over q=100→300). **one-shot boot-time entries do not — the `MODEL-BAD partial model map` latch record written at spawn had already aged out** (`grep -c "MODEL-BAD partial"` = 0). Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Flags — JARVIS_MODEL_FAIL_PROBE".
  - `JARVIS_THINKING` — **Gemma 4 thinking mode (default: 0 = OFF).** Default 0 and CLOSED on VALUE, not merely cost: the #6 STEP 2 pre-registered A/B (2026-07-27) found the thought DUPLICATES the answer's reasoning, so both ON legs hit stop=CAP at 900 with the answer truncated while both OFF legs ended NATURALLY at 496/866 and got BOTH verdicts right. Think length cannot be sized - 320, 651, 828. Reopens on a larger model, a GPU, or a workload shift. in the slot where the ANSWER begins, a position the template never emits. **THE COST OF A THOUGHT IS NOW MEASURED (#6, KVM A/B, 2026-07-27) — and it is worse than "250 is tight": the model NEVER CLOSES THE CHANNEL, so the answer gets ZERO tokens.** **a thought had never been seen to end.** no closer with THINKING=0 → all answer (the first draft reported `think=n_gen` whenever the closer was absent, which made an ordinary THINKING=0 run read as 100% thought). ON = 900 tok **`stop=CAP`**, **think=651 answer=249** — the thought reached 5.56%, then the ANSWER **restarted the entire calculation from scratch** and was cut mid-sentence at *"Total run time (in seconds) = 2 hours × 3"*, so **the user never receives a result**. **Q2 bandwidth trap** (a memory-bandwidth-bound workload does NOT inherit a 50× matmul speedup): OFF = 866 tok `MODEL-ENDED-TURN`, 3558 B, **trap AVOIDED** — states *"less than 50×"* and names the reason (*"adding a faster compute unit won't help if the data pipeline can't keep up"*); ON = 900 tok **`stop=CAP`**, **think=828 answer=72** — cut at *"Your current system is explicitly **memory-"*, so **the pre-registered verdict cannot even be APPLIED to what was delivered**. **THE MECHANISM IS THE FINDING: the thought does not REPLACE the answer's reasoning, it DUPLICATES it.** The model works the problem in full inside the channel and then works it again in the answer, so the mode costs ~2× for the SAME content and at any finite budget the ANSWER is what gets truncated — both ON legs hit `stop=CAP` at 900 while both OFF legs ended NATURALLY at 496/866. **STEP 1's ~320-TOKEN SIZING DOES NOT GENERALISE, AND IS CORRECTED HERE:** think length scales with question difficulty — **320** (retrieval) → **651** (arithmetic) → **828** (multi-factor reasoning) — so a fixed think budget cannot be sized, and the harder the question (exactly where thinking would be worth having) the more it starves the answer. **D2 force-close is not a rescue**: closing at ~150 would truncate a thought needing 651–828 mid-reasoning, and `f1fc84b` already measured that a mid-clause prompt tips this model into garbage. **THE REASON IS THE BASELINE, not a defect in the mode — `THINKING=0` ALREADY EMITS VISIBLE CHAIN-OF-THOUGHT IN THE ANSWER BODY** (Q1 walked all five arithmetic steps; Q2 split into scenarios and explicitly asked *"Why not 50×?"*) and got **BOTH** pre-registered verdicts right in fewer total tokens than the ON thought ALONE consumed. **A FOURTH BLOCKER, measured incidentally: the default-ON WORKLOAD lane yields `answer=0` under THINKING=1, 3/3** (`[PB] gen n=50 cap=50 think=50 answer=0 closer=none`) — its 50-token budget is entirely eaten by a thought that never closes, so a THINKING flip is **not a control-IN-only decision**; it would silently empty the workload lane and every `[INFER]` with it. **RECOMMENDATION: the arc (separate budgets + D4 stripping + liveness tick + multi-frame, 3–4 days) is NOT worth building.** It buys elaboration, not correctness, on the two questions designed to reward reasoning; it costs ~2×; and it cannot be sized. **`JARVIS_THINKING` STAYS 0 — now on VALUE, not merely on cost.** Reopen ONLY if a question class is found where OFF is measurably WRONG and ON is right; neither pre-registered probe produced one. **Two side-findings from the same run: (a)** Q2's OFF answer is **3558 B = 2.5× the 1426 B transport ceiling**, so multi-frame replies are ALREADY binding on ordinary explanatory answers and are **not** a thinking-mode prerequisite; **(b)** the pre-registered Q1 had to be REWORDED because `route.c` captured it as SYSFACTS — see the `JARVIS_ROUTING` row's bare-`"faults"` finding. Arithmetic was preserved exactly (*"A page fault costs 10ms."* byte-identical; 40000 × 10 ms over a 2 hour run), so the verdict is unchanged; the deviation is recorded rather than glossed. **→ FULL RESEARCH + VERDICT: `phase6/docs/THINKING_MODE_RESEARCH.md` (2026-07-27). One line: CLOSED — `JARVIS_THINKING` stays 0 for THIS model on THIS workload, on convergent evidence from our own pre-registered A/B, the workload composition, and published evaluation of the family at this size (GSM8K 70%→55% WITH thinking at 2B; E2B's own AIME 2026 is 37.5%, not the 89.2% headline, which belongs to family members we do not run). Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Flags — JARVIS_THINKING".

SEC-039 closed for the action path) + **MONITORS** (default-ON since 6-1 flip 2026-07-09 — the 4 always-on watchers, live over v8 telemetry) + **WAKE** (default-ON since 6-2 flip 2026-07-12 — the event-driven wake lane, live over v9 telemetry; honest-0 on a healthy box) + **PROACTIVE** (default-ON since 6-3 flip 2026-07-13 — the ≥5 registry INFORM behaviors + the calibrated 6/hr global cap, live over v10 telemetry; a healthy box fires ~only the uptime digests) + **CONTROL_IN** (default-ON since the 6-5 FLIP 2026-07-21 — the two-way conversation channel is LIVE whenever JARVIS runs; v11 telemetry, three fail-closed gates, Ubuntu keeps `BootOrder[0]` so exposure is bounded to deliberate JARVIS boots) + **CONTROL_IN_RECALL** (default-ON since the M5-recall FLIP 2026-07-22 — the box recalls a repeated control-IN query's own prior answer across sessions via the dedicated control-IN store @21,140,000, three-gate hardware-proven; EXACT-REPEAT scope, semantic association out of scope) + **ROUTING** (default-ON since the 6-6 B FLIP 2026-07-23 — the control-IN query router; SYSFACTS from PA state / DECLINE canned / INFER to the model, HELDOUT 95.89% keyword-blind) + **EMBED** (default-ON since C/M3b 2026-08-01 — semantic recall; about half of paraphrases recall, a miss degrades to today's no-preamble path) + **ROUTE_VETO** (default-ON since the C/M4 FLIP 2026-08-02, boot_id=49 — the embedder vetoes a SYSFACTS capture that sits closer to the INFER centroid; the bare-word FP class cut 32 -> 6 at the cost of 1 FN, SYSFACTS captures ONLY, ~300–800 ms added to a genuine status question, workload lane untouched) on, IPC/PB/RING/BOOT_LOG/G3_PROBE/G3_AB/SHIELD_LEARN/SHIELD_PROBE/**PB_TICK**/**ACTION_PROBE/KM2A_SPIKE/MONITOR_PROBE/WAKE_PROBE/PROACTIVE_PROBE/CONTROL_IN_PROBE/CONTROL_IN_RECALL_PROBE/ROUTING_PROBE/MODEL_FAIL_PROBE/ROUTE_VETO_PROBE/PB_TICK_PROBE** off (every *_PROBE stays off — the deploy never induces synthetic events) (BOOT_LOG off avoids NVMe write wear over 30 days; SHIELD_LEARN is #5's monitor-only risk map — flip is an M4 call; **ROUTE_VETO FLIPPED default-ON at C/M4 2026-08-02 (boot_id=49) and has moved to the ON list above — its PROBE stays 0; PB_TICK is BUILT gated-off — the N2 liveness tick, TICK=0 byte-identical, its flip a separate decision; ACTION_PROBE stays off — the deploy self-heals real faults but induces no synthetic ones; the QUERY path stays passive/ALLOW by design**) — **flag count as of 2026-08-02, counted mechanically not quoted: 16 `*_PROBE` flags exist, all 0** (the older "14" and "17" figures elsewhere in this file are stale)
- **NVMe Driver:** `phase3/src/drivers/nvme.c/h` — **+ `nvme_flush` (6-5/M4c-fix, GATED `#if JARVIS_CONTROL_IN`, control-IN ONLY):** NVM FLUSH (opcode 0x00) commits the drive's volatile write cache to NAND, making the control-IN replay-floor write power-safe. FLUSH deliberately, NOT FUA — FLUSH is MANDATORY in the NVMe spec (always honored) while the FUA write bit is OPTIONAL (a drive may treat such a write as normal). A SEPARATE command by design: `nvme_write_sectors` is textually unchanged, so the deployed write path (episodic / JACT / semantic) is byte-unchanged and OFF-identity holds trivially (the gate rides the build script's `-DJARVIS_CONTROL_IN=1` — this driver does not include `jarvis_debug.h`, the `nic_i211.c` precedent). **That gate is ON in the deployed image since the 6-5 flip 2026-07-21, so `nvme_flush` is LIVE on the replay-floor write.** Scoped to the floor only; the logs stay best-effort unflushed.
- **NVMe Log:** `phase3/src/drivers/nvme_log.c/h` — raw sector logging for bare-metal telemetry. Its host test `phase3/src/drivers/test_nvme_log.c` (5 PASS) gained a deterministic **`--dump <case> <path>`** fixture writer at A6a (2026-08-03) — cases `plain` / `wrapped` / `migrated`, driving the REAL `nvme_log.c` through its mock disk, zeroed first so an invalid header forces `boot_id 1`, fixed payloads, no clock or rand.
- **NVMe Log Parser:** `phase3/scripts/parse_nvme_log.py` — extract + format log from raw device. **A6a (2026-08-03) gave it the C→Python round-trip it was the only store parser lacking** (`parse_episodic` and `parse_action_audit` both had one) — and this parser is the ONLY way the box's durable log is read off-hardware, so a decode bug stays invisible until someone is mid-diagnosis. The decode is now exposed as `parse_header()` / `record_order()` / `iter_records()` (it was print-only `main()`, so a round-trip could only have scraped stdout and would have been asserting on the print format); `main()` is presentation only, and the refactor is **PROVEN output-neutral** — old vs new produce byte-identical output on all three fixtures, including the 2709-line wrapped case. New `phase3/scripts/test_parse_nvme_log.py` = **32 assertions, ALL PASS** → CI "Phase 3: NVMe-log parser round-trip (C -> Python)". **THE SUBTLETY WORTH KNOWING BEFORE CAPTURING A PARTIAL LOG:** this parser derives its wrap modulus from the buffer it is handed (`slots = len(data)//512 − 1`), **NOT** from `NVME_LOG_MAX_ENTRIES` — so a TRUNCATED wrapped capture rotates by a different modulus than `nvme_log.c` used when writing, leaving both sides self-consistent and both wrong. The `wrapped` and `migrated` fixtures are therefore dumped FULL SIZE (2701 sectors) so the two moduli agree; the same applies to a real `dd` recovery, which is why the documented recipe uses `count=2701`.
- **NVMe Log Reset:** `phase3/scripts/clear_nvme_log.sh` — zero the telemetry-log region (derives LBA/count from `nvme_log.h`; archive first, `--yes` to write)
- **FAT32 Parser:** `phase3/src/drivers/fat32.c/h` — read-only FAT32; **FAT-sector cache** (`fat32_fs_t.fat_cache` — `fat32_next_cluster` reads each FAT sector once vs up to ~128× per chain → ~160 MB less NVMe I/O on the Gemma load) + a `fat32_read_file` **progress hook** (`fat32_fs_t.progress` → exact data-only load %). `fat32_init` already rejects sizes outside {512,1024,2048,4096} (SEC-028); 1024/2048/4096 are accepted but unsupported by the 512-byte `fat_cache` and fail safely (SEC-029 returns -1 at the first FAT read). Optional tightening: reject `!=512` outright. Never fires on the box's 512B NVMe. **fat32_read_file final partial-sector overflow fixed (H3)** — the trailing partial sector is read into a stack bounce buffer and only the leftover `tail` bytes copied, so it never writes up to bytes_per_sector-1 bytes past `buf+file_size` (delivers exactly file_size; deployed Gemma load byte-identical, only the unwritten junk-tail changes); regression in `test_fat32.c` (0xAA canary, single- + multi-cluster sub-sector files; fail pre-fix).
- **Model Bench-Off:** `phase3/docs/MODEL_BENCH_OFF_2026-04-07.md` — tiers, compatibility, deferred contenders (Gemma 4 §7, Qwen3.5 §7b, Qwen3 §9)
- **Bench Scripts:**
  - `phase3/scripts/bench_models.sh` — unified bench (speed/perplexity/quality) for JARVIS PC. **A PREFIXED quality run now REQUIRES `QUALITY_PREFIX_TAG` and FAILS CLOSED (exit 2, writes nothing) without it** — a prefix changes the system being measured, so it must not share an output filename. The old path used `${PREFIX:+.frontload}`, which collapsed every non-empty prefix to ONE name; the 2026-07-28 front-load-only run overwrote the two-clause run in place and those per-model artifacts were unrecoverable. The output name now derives from the tag. An unprefixed run is unaffected.
  - `phase3/scripts/bench_engine_models.sh` — JARVIS engine bench (all 11 models, quantized path)
  - `phase3/scripts/bench_speed_windows.ps1` — Windows speed bench (CPU + GPU modes)
  - `phase3/scripts/bench_perplexity_windows.ps1` — Windows perplexity bench (GPU, full WikiText-2)
- **Bench Results:** `models/bench_results/bench_results.txt` (JARVIS PC speed), `models/bench_results/bench_results_mainpc.txt` (5600 CPU), `models/bench_results/bench_results_mainpc_gpu.txt` (5600 + RTX 2070)
- **Engine Bench Results:** `models/bench_results/jarvis_engine_bench.txt` (JARVIS engine, 11/11 models, quantized path, 6 model families)

- **seL4 build tree, in version control (A10, 2026-08-09):** `phase3/sel4-tree/` + `phase3/scripts/reconstruct_sel4_tree.sh`. Committed at A10 2026-08-09: the pinned repo manifest, the 4 vendored files, the 72-path deletion list, the recorded configure flags and a 26-file sha256 baseline, with reconstruct_sel4_tree.sh PROVEN (run 31313653563, 72/72 deleted, 26/26 byte-identical to the box, and a NEGATIVE CONTROL that exits 1). Its header said MODIFIES 3 of those 27 and now says four (fixed 2026-09-04). `build_jarvis_x86.sh` copies sources into an out-of-tree checkout at `$HOME/sel4-x86` and runs ninja there, hard-failing if it is missing (`:91`, `:1032`) — it cannot bootstrap; **NOT covered:** binary byte-identity (build paths are embedded, so a rebuilt kernel differs in bytes while matching in config), and whether the 72 deletions are load-bearing — they are reproduced faithfully, not minimised. The vendored tree was never wrong; So a reconstruction could never have reproduced the stale-source bug: from a clean tree there is no `src/ipc/shmem_ipc.c` for the script to leave stale, and cmake fails loudly on the missing source instead — which is exactly what the A10 boot smoke hit. Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — seL4 build tree, in version control (A10, 2026-08-09)".
- **CI boots the shipped image (A10, 2026-08-09):** the `boot-smoke` job in `ci.yml` — manual (`workflow_dispatch`), **non-gating**, no cache (the reason is a comment in the job so it is not "optimised" back in). **TWO LEGS since 2026-09-05 (a matrix on the one job — `failprobe` = the original stub + probe boot; `generation` = the deploy config UNTOUCHED with the REAL hash-pinned Llama 3.2 1B Q4_K_M `mcopy`'d onto the FAT32 image as `GEMMA2B.GUF`, restored from the same Actions cache `model-tests` uses and re-verified by `phase3/scripts/ci_fetch_pinned_gguf.sh`).** The generation leg ASSERTS the mechanism — `[JARVIS] Reading model from NVMe` = 1, `[JARVIS] Model loaded: 770MB GGUF=` = 1, 0 `[MODEL-BAD]` / `[MODEL-FAIL-PROBE]` / `model=UNUSABLE`, `model=loaded` ≥ 1, the two once-only embed-absent notices (PA's `[EMBED] no QWENEMB GUF`, PB's `[EMBED-PB] no embed model`), the deploy-signature PREFIX `q=100 hits=71 infer=13 hb=11 shield=5` = 1 (model-independent: PRNG mix + cache), `[INFER] "` ≥ 5, 0 `[PA] TIMEOUT` / `[FATAL]` / `[FAULT]` / `respawn`, `idx=0` only — and REPORTS, never asserts, the model-dependent signals: every `err=` value (an EMPTY workload response bumps `q_errors` at the `resp_offset == 0` branch), the `[INFER]` count and first five texts, a distinct-word proxy, `untyped_count`, `M3: started`. **PLUMBING, NOT QUALITY: the model is not the deployed Gemma, and Process B's prompt template hardcodes Gemma-4 token ids (105/2364/107/106/4368 — id 98 <|think|> only under JARVIS_THINKING, default 0) that are junk for a Llama vocabulary, plus a two-clause stop (`inference_server.c:802`): the first clause, `next == 1` — Gemma's hardcoded `<eos>` — is ALSO junk for Llama (id 1 is `"` there, so on a Llama GGUF it ends generation the moment a `"` is sampled); the second clause, the GGUF-declared `eos_id` read from `tokenizer.ggml.eos_token_id` at load, is the loaded model's OWN terminator and adapts to a Llama GGUF. Only the prompt ids and the `<eos>=1` clause are junk — never the declared eos id.** Still manual + non-gating (promotion is a separate decision once a stability distribution exists). MEASURED over three dispatch runs (`33958945248` — 20/21, the one FAIL its own shared-tail assertion, corrected at B2 — then `33960039072` and `33960477131`, both every-MUST PASS): `T_MODEL_LOADED` **117 / 161 / 139 s** for the same 807,694,464 B through the emulated NVMe (**437 / 313 / 364 MB/min** — a BAND, and the 161 s run is +37.6% on the first, outside the pre-registered ±25%, reported not waived), first `[STATS]` window at 330 / 396 / 402 s, **13 of 13 dispatched inferences printed `[INFER]`** with `err=0` the only value in all three, `untyped_count` 106 and `M3: started 5 workers`; leg walls 6m53s / 7m57s / 8m19s and the failprobe leg green every time. Full trail: the 2026-09-05 bracket in the record. The boot-smoke job in ci.yml: manual (workflow_dispatch), non-gating, no cache - it reconstructs the tree, builds via build_jarvis_x86.sh and boots under KVM against a sparse 11 GiB FAT32 image carrying an 8 MiB GEMMA2B.GUF stub, built with JARVIS_MODEL_FAIL_PROBE=1, asserting by count and never by presence. Poll-until-marker passed 3/3 at 105/84/92 s. Threading is DELIBERATELY NOT ASSERTED. Asserts, by count and never by presence: `Booting all finished` = 1 · `Self-Test: 5/5 PASS` = 1 · the probe line = 1 · `[MODEL-BAD] why=frame-alloc` >= 1 · `model=UNUSABLE` >= 1 · `model=loaded` = 0 · `[FATAL]` = 0 · `err=0` the ONLY err value · and **`idx=0`** with no other `idx=` value (a fresh image has no JEPI header, so the control-IN store initialises empty — the leg `BOOT_LOG=0` makes unobservable on the box, free here because CI has full serial). **WOULD IT HAVE CAUGHT THE STALE-SOURCE BUG? Only half of it, and the distinction matters:** it would have caught the **WIRING** — a from-scratch tree has no `src/ipc/shmem_ipc.c`, so cmake fails loudly on a missing source (precisely what happened) — and it could **NEVER** have caught the **STALENESS**, because staleness only exists in a long-lived tree and CI builds a new one every run. **reconciling the box is a separate change with its own gate and deploy and must not ride a CI commit. **[DONE 2026-08-10 — deployed as its own gated change at boot_id=52; see the Shared Memory IPC row and the DEPLOYED STATE block.]**** Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — CI boots the shipped image (A10, 2026-08-09)".
- **CI model-gated suites run for REAL (2026-09-05):** the `model-tests` job in `ci.yml` — GATING (push / PR / dispatch, its own job so `test` stays fast, no `needs` either way) — fetches the EXACT file the four Llama-gated suites hardcode at `phase3/models/Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct-Q4_K_M.gguf`: the **bartowski** build, 807,694,464 B, sha256 `6f85a640a97cf2bf…`, PROVEN to be the bytes the suites' expectations were tuned against because the box's `phase3/models/` copy hashes identical. **HASH-PINNED, NEVER NAME-MATCHED:** unsloth publishes a DIFFERENT file under the identical filename (807,694,368 B, sha256 `3f5a2242…`) and the box carries BOTH (`~/Desktop/JARVIS_OS/models/` = unsloth) — a name-matched fetch would run the BOS-argmax check on bytes it was never tuned for. `actions/cache/restore` + `save`, and the sha256 AND size are re-verified on EVERY run so a corrupt cache fails closed. The fetch/verify lives in `phase3/scripts/ci_fetch_pinned_gguf.sh` (shellcheck tier 2) since commit A of 2026-09-05's image-generation slice — ONE copy of the pin, shared with the `boot-smoke` generation leg. The four steps MOVED out of `test` (one copy, no drift; step names unchanged), which is why `test` reads 135 named steps and `model-tests` 7 of 9. Three suites gate on exit code: `test_gguf_memory` 7/7, `test_gguf_vocab` 10/10, `test_generation` 6/6 (30+30+30+50+20 F32-path tokens + the BOS-argmax set). **`test_forward_compare` has NO assertions by design and exits 0 unless a load fails — its counts are REPORTED, NOT ASSERTED:** OK=14 WARN=0 DIVERGED=0 MATCH=8 MISMATCH=0 DIFFER=0, and that came back IDENTICAL locally and on the runner across both CI runs — which is what a deterministic engine over hash-pinned bytes should do, and is the argument for pinning by hash rather than by name; a threshold is a separate decision, taken with numbers in hand. An anti-vacuity guard fails any suite step that prints `SKIP` or `::warning::` with the model present — a green built on a skip is the exact failure this job exists to end. MEASURED (local WSL, gcc 13.3.0, 15 GB RAM): memory 4.3 s · vocab 3.0 s · compare 43.5 s · generation 78.3 s, every suite exit 0 with no `SKIP` and no `::warning::` in any log. CI first run (cache MISS, run 33935692805): `T_FETCH` 7 s · memory 2 s · vocab 1 s · compare 45 s · generation 87 s, job wall **2 m 47 s**. CI cache-HIT run (dispatch 33935904347): job wall **2 m 30 s**, `cache-hit=true`, no fetch, and the sha256 `: OK` line still printed — verification runs on a HIT too, which is the whole point of re-verifying every run rather than trusting the cache key. The runner is ~1.7× faster than local WSL on the two fast suites and ~10% slower on generation. **HONEST SCOPE: the HOST engine (`llama_forward.c` F32 + `llama_quant.c`) generates in CI; the SHIPPED seL4 IMAGE still never generates in CI** — `boot-smoke` boots a stub under `JARVIS_MODEL_FAIL_PROBE=1`; the image-level smoke is ROADMAP B3's remaining half. Also in this slice: `test_gemma4_forward.c`'s E2B smoke skip now prints `::warning::`, so of the six model-gated skips only `test_avx2_correctness.c:376` stays silent, by design.
- **QEMU NVMe Test (CANONICAL):** `phase3/scripts/qemu_test.sh` — `bash qemu_test.sh [--image PATH] [--log FILE] [--smp N] [--mem 8G] [--no-snapshot]`. Defaults: image `~/nvme_test.img`, `-smp` DERIVED from the built kernel's `CONFIG_MAX_NUM_NODES` (so the runner cannot drift from the thing it runs), `-snapshot` ON (a gate never mutates the shared image, and two gates can run concurrently). Use `--log` for detached/`systemd-run` runs and `--no-snapshot` only when persistence is the point (e.g. a 2-boot store-survival test). **It no longer takes a model path** — the model is loaded at runtime from FAT32 as `GEMMA2B.GUF` on the image. `qemu_test_nvme.sh` is a DEPRECATED shim that forwards here and refuses a model-path argument.
- **QEMU NVMe Disk:** `~/nvme_test.img` FAT32 with the model file named to match `JARVIS_MODEL_FILE` (currently `GEMMA2B GUF`); `-drive file=...,if=none,id=nvme0,format=raw -device nvme,serial=QEMU_NVME,drive=nvme0`
- **Inference Server (PB):** `phase3/src/sel4/inference_server.c` — **STOP-TOKEN FIX 2026-07-26 (ungated, deployed path):** the generation loop capped at 50 tokens and broke ONLY on `<eos>`=1, with a comment that deliberately rejected the model's declared terminator (*"Stop on <eos>=1, NOT eos_id=106 (that's `<turn|>` which model emits first)"*). Ungated deployed path: the loop breaks on the LOADED tok->eos_id and BEFORE storing; control-IN alone gets PB_FRONTLOAD_INSTRUCTION (EXACTLY ONE CLAUSE, measured blind E2B 9.0 vs Llama 3.1 8B 7.8) with the lane EXPLICIT. **MEASURED, and it corrected the estimate that motivated it:** a native-engine probe over 5 realistic questions with no preamble found **4 of 5 emit NO terminator at all** (the model never finishes inside 50 tokens, so there is nothing to pad) and 1 of 5 recovered 4 tokens (**8%**). **[THE DEGENERATE CASE OF THAT RISK IS NOW A MEASURED FINDING 2026-08-11 (closures sweep, A9/3 T6 reachability): a model whose FIRST sampled token is the terminator yields `n_gen == 0` (the break fires BEFORE the store), the decode yields `text_len == 0`, and the explicit empty-response branch (the `if (text_len == 0)` send — "If empty response, send empty message") emits a seq-matched ZERO-LENGTH `MSG_RESPONSE` — which on the control-IN lane is exactly the T6 told-vs-stored edge: PA replies verdict 0 "answered" with EMPTY text while storing `EPI_OUT_ERROR` and counting `answered`. A `tokenizer_decode` error (<0, coerced to 0) reaches the same branch. **[NO LONGER UNOBSERVED, AND NO LONGER A FALSE "ANSWERED" — 2026-08-11: the send is now a NAMED, EXERCISED path. `JARVIS_CONTROL_IN_PROBE=3` gained an `EMPTYPROBE` leg that clamps this query's cap to 0, so `n_gen == 0` and THIS branch fires for real; KVM-measured `[PB] gen n=0 prompt=24 instr=10 cap=0 pos=24 kv=512 stop=CAP think=0 answer=0 closer=none` → `[CTRL-IN-RESP] q="EMPTYPROBE say nothing" -> ""` → `[CTRL-IN-REPLY] verdict=3 seq=16 len=0`. The consumer changed too: `ctrl_exit_decide` now maps an empty response to verdict 3 and does not count it answered, so this branch no longer produces a false "answered". Note the leg's own evidence for the ordering rule — the frame arrives carrying `stop=CAP` (because `0 >= 0`) and still reports 3, with NO `TRUNCATED` line of its own (the run's single `TRUNCATED` belongs to the CAPPROBE leg). **BUT `stop=CAP` IS THE PROBE'S SHAPE, NOT THE DEPLOYED ONE — do not read this leg as showing what production does.** CAP requires `n_gen >= max_tokens`, which at `n_gen == 0` needs `max_tokens == 0`, and only `PB_GEN_EMPTYPROBE_TOKENS` sets that; the deployed caps are 50 and 250, so a real empty generation carries **`MODEL_ENDED`**. The probe proves the ORDERING (that emptiness outranks truncation); T6 proves the real MODEL_ENDED+empty mapping on the host. Both land on verdict 3 because the rule tests `resp_len` alone.]** Originally: not observed in any gate; report-and-stop — the fix decision was the strategist's. The serve-loop `!model_loaded` empty-send is NOT a live trigger (every load failure `goto idle`s; PB never serves model-less), and the COOP empty-send is spike-gated.]** **#18 FRONT-LOADING + #9 TRUNCATION-REPORTING 2026-07-28 (ungated, deployed path, CONTROL-IN ONLY):** `handle_query` now takes the lane **EXPLICITLY** (`is_control_in`, set from the same `MSG_QUERY_LONG` test that picks the cap) and injects `PB_FRONTLOAD_INSTRUCTION` = *"Answer the question directly in your first sentence."* (~10 tok) immediately before the question. **EXACTLY ONE CLAUSE:** the two-clause variant (*", then elaborate."*) was NET-HARMFUL in `1df800a` (control length-violations 0.0→7.0, quality 8.0→4.0) — do not re-add it. This also closes the ANSWER_QUALITY_DESIGN §12 honest-limit *"the stop reason is not observable in production"*: it is now observable ON THE WIRE, where it matters, rather than in a probe-gated log a deployed build never writes. **T1c 2026-07-29 — the BOUNDED variant was MEASURED and REJECTED; do not re-run it on a hunch.** Verdict **DO NOT SHIP**, source reverted byte-identical. Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — Inference Server (PB)".
- **NVMe Partition Setup:** `phase3/scripts/setup_nvme_partition.sh` — creates `JARVIS_DATA` at EXACTLY sector 32768 (hard-asserted via `parted unit s print`; was free-space start → silent model-not-found), default model = Gemma 4 E2B Q4_K_M, copies as `GEMMA2B.GUF` + validates the FAT 8.3 short name == `JARVIS_MODEL_FILE` (`GEMMA2B GUF`), detects+verifies an existing `JARVIS_DATA` instead of recreating (box-safe)
- **x86 Installer (one-script):** `phase3/scripts/install_jarvis_x86.sh` — orchestrates build → boot-USB → NVMe model provision → verify → BIOS/validation checklist; Phase 4 goal #4 DONE: --target usb, --target esp (reversible DUAL-BOOT on the existing internal ESP, VERIFIED on-box 2026-06-25 at boot_id=5, Ubuntu kept BootOrder[0]) and disk, which is CODE + DRY-RUN ONLY and NEVER run on the dev box. Host test 50 PASS, both shellcheck-clean (full strictness), dry-run CI-gated. `disk` = full single-OS on-SSD install that WIPES the whole disk (JARVIS only, no Ubuntu): `guard_disk_device` (whole-disk only; refuses the booted/root + mounted disk + partition paths; WIPE-AND-INSTALL-JARVIS + DESTROY-ALL-DATA triple-confirm) + `write_boot_disk` (wipefs/sgdisk zap → GPT → JARVIS_DATA @ 32768 ~10 GiB + ESP `--removable` BOOTX64.EFI; telemetry-log tail unpartitioned) — **CODE + DRY-RUN ONLY, NEVER run on the dev box** (ADR `docs/decisions/2026-06-25-target-disk-full-ssd-install.md`); Full record: docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md §"Quick Reference — x86 Installer (one-script)".
- **Check CI:** `gh run list --limit 1` then `gh run view <id> --log-failed` if failed

### Rules
- Always update CLAUDE.md after completing work: a pointer row gets its CURRENT numbers/status changed in place; the evidence narrative is APPENDED to the topic's entry in `docs/CLAUDE_RECORD.md` (verbatim, dated), never written back into the pointer. A new topic gets a CLAUDE.md row; it moves to the record only when it has grown into history. `phase3/scripts/check_claude_record.py` (CI) asserts every pointer resolves.
- **Phase 7 status board:** `phase7/docs/PHASE_7_PLAN.md` §0 is the live where-are-we. A commit that completes a milestone flips that row in the same change — the landing commit, or the flip commit immediately after it citing the landing hash — writing the hash and date into the cell; a cell never reads DONE without a hash; the strategist's prompt names the row to flip.
- COMMIT WEEKLY with week number in message
- Update `phase2/docs/PHASE_2_PROGRESS_TRACKER.md` when completing work
- Test everything: aim for 100% pass rate
- When creating new test files (test_*.c or test_*.py), ALWAYS add a corresponding step to `.github/workflows/ci.yml` with the exact compile+run command. Verify the new CI step compiles locally before committing. **Mechanically enforced for C since 2026-08-03** by the CI completeness gate (`phase3/scripts/check_ci_completeness.py`): every `test_*.c`/`fuzz_*.c` under `phase3/src/` must be referenced by a step in `ci.yml` or listed in `.github/ci_test_allowlist.txt` **with a reason** — the gate also rejects a reasonless or stale allowlist entry, and refuses to pass vacuously if it finds no tests at all. Intentional exceptions today: `test_gemma4_native.c` (needs the ~2.89 GiB GGUF) and `test_ggml_integration.c` (needs out-of-repo ggml objects). **Scope is the completeness direction ONLY** — modules with no test are deliberately NOT nagged about, because the seL4 tier (`main_x86.c`, `inference_server.c`) cannot be host-compiled and would fail such a check by construction. **The Python side is NOT gated** (the rule still applies by discipline for `test_*.py`).
- After every `git push`, check the GitHub Actions workflow status with `gh run list --limit 1` and `gh run view` to verify CI passed. If any step failed, investigate with `gh run view --log-failed` and fix before continuing.
- Phase 2 is C-only on Pi 4 (no Python runtime on seL4)
- **UI–feature parity:** when shipping a user-visible feature, update the Remote Telemetry Console (`phase4/console/`) **in the same change** — add its real live signal to the relevant screen or its auto-populated Capabilities/Features section (never hardcoded). The UI shows only real/live state: a real feature missing from the UI is a gap; UI showing anything without a live source is fiction. Enforced by `phase4/console/test_console_honesty.py`.
- **Frontend (`phase4/console/`) stays correct:** runtime libs (React/Babel/Lucide) are **vendored** under `phase4/console/vendor/` (pinned — no live CDN) so CI is hermetic. Layered tests, all Python (Playwright's **Python** binding — `pip`, no Node): the honesty grep gate + a **key-contract** test (`jarvis_telemetry.h`→`packet_to_record`→`telemetry.js` shape, against one golden fixture) + a **logic** test (`telemetry.js` connState/CRC/stale-watchdog via Playwright `page.clock`) + a real-SSE **e2e smoke** (page boots / no console errors / `simulated==false` / **every live `flags_list` flag renders a Capabilities row**). **All four layers now exist and are CI-gated** (honesty 92 + key-contract/receiver 136 + logic 14 + e2e 34; Playwright installed in CI via `--with-deps --only-shell chromium`, pinned `playwright==1.60.0`). Keep them green; one visual confirm per visual change (the e2e covers the rest).

### Codebase Metrics

**FULLY RE-COUNTED 2026-09-04** (previously 2026-07-21) (the previous figures below the Phase-2 line were badly stale — Phase 3 had drifted ~22k LOC / 87 files). **Method:** `git ls-files phase0 … phase6 | grep -E '\.(c|h|py)$'` for the file list, `wc -l` over that list for LOC, and a `test_`/`fuzz_` basename match for the test-file column (so fuzz harnesses now count as test files — the older counts matched `test_` only). Phases 5 and 6 hold docs/plans only; **all their code lives under `phase3/src`** and is counted there.

- **Phase 0:** 4,919 LOC, 11 code files (COMPLETE)

It was 7 named of 9 before, itself a 2026-08-10 correction of an earlier count that conflated steps with named steps) and they **never run on push**: it is `if: github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'` with no `needs`, and a push shows it as `skipped` (verified, not assumed). Raw `grep -c '^    - name:'` now counts named steps across all FOUR jobs, so a single total is more misleading than ever; split it. Historical: **137 named steps** (**re-counted 2026-08-07 after A8** — +8 for the parser-facing suites now running sanitised; was 126 after A9/2. **A8 ALSO ADDED A SECOND JOB**, `coverage`, which is `workflow_dispatch`-only, has no `needs`, and is NOT in the gating `test` job. **THIS BREAKS WHAT THE NUMBER MEANS, and the row must say so: `grep -c '^    - name:'` now counts named steps ACROSS BOTH JOBS, so 137 is no longer "the test job's step count".** Measured split: **`test` = 134 named of 135 total** (the difference is the unnamed `actions/checkout@v4`) + **`coverage` = 3 named of 5 total** (unnamed `checkout` and `upload-artifact`) = 137. The gating figure is **134**; quote that when the question is "how many checks guard a push". **Counting method caveat found the hard way 2026-08-07: `yaml.safe_load` CANNOT validate this file** — it silently keeps the LAST of duplicate keys, so a ci.yml with `coverage:` declared twice loaded cleanly and reported a normal job list while GitHub rejected the workflow at parse time (run finished in 0.0 s, ZERO jobs created, `gh run view --log-failed` had no log to show). Use a duplicate-key-aware loader; and note NO CI STEP CAN GUARD THIS, since the guard would live inside the file that fails to parse.** Previously **126** as re-counted 2026-08-05 after A9/2 — +1 for the crash-loop-window suite; was 125 after A9/1 and 124 after A6+A7 — the one new step is the control-IN index suite; PyYAML reports 126, the difference being the unnamed `actions/checkout@v4`, exactly as the method below describes. The prior "103 … counted 2026-07-21" was 21 behind; **read this number from the file, never from this line.** **+4 at the 2026-08-03 CI-quick-wins slice:** the A1 copy-drift invariant (12 duplicated pairs compared by CONTENT via `diff --strip-trailing-cr`, because 4 of them differ only in COMMITTED line endings; 9 genuinely-forked pairs excluded and named for backlog A7; carries a self-test that the known-forked `decision_cache.c` pair still compares UNEQUAL, so the gate cannot go vacuous), the A2 two-sided operator-tooling invariant (menu carries no `of=`/`seek=` + a positive control proving that grep is non-vacuous + admin MUST carry the write — the recipe lives in CI and never in the scripts it greps), the A5 test-completeness gate, and the A4 PowerShell parse + pinned-PSScriptAnalyzer floor. Both are CI-gated (shellcheck + dry-run). **Shellcheck coverage extended 2026-08-03 (A3) from 4 of 16 to ALL 16 `phase3/scripts/*.sh`, in two tiers:** FULL severity for the scripts that write to a device or build the shipped image (`install_jarvis_x86.sh`, `test_install_jarvis_x86.sh`, **`build_jarvis_x86.sh`**, **`create_boot_usb.sh`**) and `-S error` for the other 12. Reaching full severity took ONE code change and five targeted `# shellcheck disable=SC2012` annotations, and the whole diff across both newly-gated scripts is **one non-comment line**: `-DNUM_NODES=${NN}` → `"${NN}"`, proven argv-identical for every legitimate value by an argv probe *and* closing a real latent bug (unquoted, `JARVIS_NUM_NODES='6 -DKernelFastpath=OFF'` word-splits into a SEPARATE injected cmake flag). The SC2012 disables are deliberate: those `ls` calls render human-readable operator output (`ls -lh | awk '{print $5}'` → "1.3M"), and `find`/`stat` would change what an operator reads before trusting a freshly written USB — on a destructive script. **The step also asserts every `phase3/scripts/*.sh` appears in one of the two tiers**, so a new script cannot land silently outside the gate. **`--target esp` (reversible on-SSD dual-boot) VERIFIED on-box 2026-06-25 (boot_id=5; Ubuntu kept `BootOrder[0]`) = the deployed path; the boot USB is now recovery/re-flash; `--target usb` still supported. goal #4 DONE incl. on-SSD dual-boot; the target matrix is now COMPLETE — `--target disk` (full single-OS wipe) is implemented CODE + DRY-RUN ONLY (never run on the dev box; ADR `docs/decisions/2026-06-25-target-disk-full-ssd-install.md`).**

- **Security:** March 2026 adversarial audit — 26 findings, all resolved (phase2+phase3 C). April 2026 Phase 3c audit — 25 findings: all 5 HIGH + 5 MEDIUM fixed, 5 of 9 LOW fixed, 0 of 6 INFO fixed = **15 fixed / 10 accepted-or-deferred** (the doc's own summary block says 18/7 and is contradicted by its findings table and its three listed fix commits 8+3+4 — measured 2026-09-04, the table wins). Of the 10: SEC-039 is split (closed for the ACTION path and for control-IN queries, open by design on the workload lane), SEC-038 is superseded on the control-IN reply (JRPL v2 HMAC), SEC-050's single-core reason is stale but moot (`posix_stubs.c` ships in no build; its only caller is the host pthread backend). Phase 2's own audit: 6 C findings (4 fixed) + Snyk. SHIELD: host harness (test_shield.c exercises shield.c — keyword + model-assisted scoring); the LIVE QUERY PA↔PB path stays passive — Process B returns ALLOW, shield.c not linked on the query path, so the deployed system does NOT block queries (unchanged, by design). **Phase 6 K/M4 (2026-07-08) closes SEC-039 FOR THE ACTION PATH** — the deployed image now runs the SEPARATE `shield_action.c` ACTION gate LIVE (self-heal PB crash/wedge restarts are SHIELD-scored + JACT-audited; K/M1 proved the mechanism, K/M4 flipped `JARVIS_ACTIONS` default-ON, boot_id=15 supervised on-wire: v7 crc_ok, `TLM_F_ACTIONS` set, restart/fired/blocked honest-0, err=0, NN=6). So the deployed system now has a live, SHIELD-scored, JACT-audited ACTION gate + autonomous self-healing — NOT a query blocker for the passive PA↔PB workload path (never claim "SHIELD blocks queries" / "blocking active"). **Phase 6 6-5 (2026-07-21) additionally closes SEC-039 FOR CONTROL-IN QUERIES** — every inbound control-IN query is scored by the SEPARATE `query_shield.c` gate before PA will route it, and a refusal is JACT-audited by reason-class LABEL only. **Honest scope, non-negotiable:** that gate refuses four **DEFINED ABUSE CLASSES** (key-extraction / bulk-exfil / canned-jailbreak / config-disclose) at a **measured FP of 0/100** on realistic traffic — it is **NOT a general injection detector**; general prompt injection is contained **STRUCTURALLY** (K-b: inbound text can never mint an action; tagged-untrusted so it never contaminates the cache/retrieval stores; the answer is unicast to the provisioned console address only) — **contained, not detected**. The control-IN reply is **SIGNED (HMAC-SHA256), NOT ENCRYPTED**, and third-host non-observation of that unicast reply remains **NOT PROVEN** (it needs a wired capture point) **[MEASURED 2026-08-10, boot 52, at a wired promiscuous third host: 0 frames on :51002 against a 690-frame broadcast positive control — for THIS LAN, in its most-favourable CAM state; the signed-not-encrypted limit is unchanged. See the boot-52 DEPLOYED STATE amendment.]**.

### Hardware Setup

**Two-PC setup (April 2026):**
- **Main PC:** Ryzen 5 5600, RTX 2070, 32GB, ASRock B550M-ITX/ac — daily driver
- **JARVIS PC:** Ryzen 7 2700X, R9 280X (display only), 16GB DDR4 (measured: `total_ram_mb` 16025 at boot 55; was 32GB before — the earlier "32GB (swapped from 16GB)" wording was stale), ASUS X470-F, 2TB NVMe, Ubuntu — dedicated, wipeable for bare-metal seL4
- **Pi 4 8GB:** Phase 2 complete, ARM fallback
- CPU-only AI inference on JARVIS PC (no GPU compute). GPU deferred to Phase 4.
- X470-F Gaming NIC: Intel I211-AT — needs igb-family driver (nic_rtl8168.c targets B550M's RTL8111H)
- Phase 2 hardware pivot doc: `phase2/docs/PHASE_2_HARDWARE_PIVOT.md`
- **LAN, re-addressed 2026-09-02 (new mesh):** 192.168.68.0/22, gateway 192.168.68.1. Main PC **192.168.68.63** (adapter "Ethernet 3", MAC 9C:6B:00:AE:6A:FF) · JARVIS box Ubuntu side **192.168.68.64** · Pi **192.168.68.66** wired / **192.168.68.61** WiFi — all four Deco-reserved. `~/.ssh/config` aliases: `jarvis`, `pi`, `pi-wifi`. **The seL4 side keeps `JARVIS_BOX_IP` = 192.168.100.143 by design** (compile-time, `net_udp.h:20`): it is now off-subnet and that is MEASURED harmless in both directions — the box-side inbound path never checks an IP (`control_parser.c` matches the dst PORT only), and the console-side sender uses that address for INTERFACE SELECTION only. Only the box's provisioned REPLY address had to change, and it is a slot re-provision, not a rebuild.

### Technology Stack

- **Design tooling — Claude Design** (Anthropic Labs, claude.ai/design): a repo-aware AI design tool — it reads the codebase + design files to build a design system, then designs polished pages/prototypes using our real components/tokens, and hands off to Claude Code to land the code. The current Remote Telemetry Console (`phase4/console/`) UI was produced this way. New console screens (e.g. the System screen) are prompt-designed in Claude Design against the repo's design system, then implemented + honesty-gated by Claude Code. Claude Design designs the look; it cannot invent live data — every rendered field must have a real telemetry source.
